### 6bi. Culling / occlusion string anchors (static, 1.26.62.01; ref = (xref site, containing function start))
- `frustum_visibility` -> fn 0x10f09f0 (site 0x10f45d0). `max_shadow_frustum_radius` -> fn 0x53fcac0 (shadow cascade setup).
- `ChunkOccluder` -> fn 0x6646980 (two sites 0x6646ae5, 0x664ee5f); `SimpleOccluder` -> fn 0x66a9d50 (sites 0x66a9e07, 0x66ab692). These are the terrain-visibility occluder classes next to the terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0 (6be).
- `Occlusion_Query` / `maxOcclusionQueries` -> fn 0x660ecd0 (sites 0x660f070, 0x660faa3): hardware occlusion-query pool setup.
- `OcclusionTexture` / `OcclusionHeightOffset` -> fn 0x6745440 (weather occlusion height map; "Weather (Snow) Luminance and Occlusion Texture" pass names at fn 0x5ad3cb0).
- Block culling: `minecraft:culling.*` names are registered in fn 0x3299d20 (door/stairs/dripleaf/fence post) and fn 0x5e5c190 (bed); the culling-file parser is fn 0x6ae8ed0 ("Failed to parse block culling file"); `minecraft:culling_layer.leaves` fn 0x5ce1c30. These are per-face neighbour culling rules, not frustum culling.
- Not found by string: a chunk-level frustum test; the entity/actor frustum check is likely via `frustum_visibility` (fn 0x10f09f0, not yet decoded). Pending: disassemble 0x10f09f0, 0x6646980, 0x660ecd0.

### 6bj. Correction to 6bi: `frustum_visibility` is a lighting-config field, not a cull function
Fn 0x10f09f0 (frame 0x4e8, ~0x4600 bytes, ~311 calls, repeating call targets 0x1dc620/0x1db850/0x1d6c40 x43 each = per-field JSON read helpers) is a data-driven **light-scoring/ambient transition settings deserializer** (VV/deferred lighting). Its field names, in order: `light_update_frequency`, `max_point_lights`, `score_compute_batch_size`, `light_score_smoothing_factor`, `light_scoring_weights`, `transition_step_size`, `transitioning_ambient_scalar`, `max_pending_transition_tasks`, `max_staged_transition_results`, `max_in_flight_transition_tasks`, ... and `frustum_visibility` (site 0x10f45d0) as one scoring weight. So point lights are scored on CPU (async tasks, staged results) and only the top `max_point_lights` are submitted. This is the lighting-side limit for I1b; there is no engine frustum-cull function behind this string. H3b stays open (look at the camera/frustum math via ChunkOccluder 0x6646980 instead).

### 6bk. Correction to 6bi: fn 0x6646980 is a render-task variant dispatcher, "ChunkOccluder" is a material name
Fn 0x6646980 (frame 0x9b8, saved xmm6-13) switches on `*(u8*)(r8+8)` (jump table, 11 cases, `ja` default at 0x664ef12) with payload `*(r8)` in rdi, `this` in rcx (rbx), a context in rdx (kept at [rbp+0x8a0]). Case 0 (0x6646a10): needs `payload+0x78 -> +0x40`, flag `ctx+0x70 != 1 || payload+0x89`, then builds the material-name string "MeshFallbackPosUVNormalColor" (call 0x9d6d0, 0x1c chars). Case at 0x6646a89: builds the interned name **"ChunkOccluder"** (len 0xd) and resolves the material with 0x67d6d70(…, ctx+0x68, ctx+0x40) then checks `res+0x10 == 1` (loaded flag) with shared_ptr refcount inc/dec. Others reference "CracksBlockEntity", "Cracks", "GlowInkSignForwardPBR", "MeshFallbackForwardPBR". Case at 0x6646bc6 reads a 0x18-byte item (xmm + qword) and calls passLookup 0x5aba5c0 with `ctx[+0x10] + 0x9d0` (same lookup used in 6bg). So these are per-item "which material/pass to draw this chunk-task with" handlers (in the same family as 0x66ea3d0 and RenderMark), and ChunkOccluder/SimpleOccluder are materials used to render occluder geometry (depth-only blocks), not CPU cull classes. SimpleOccluder fn 0x66a9d50 is the matching handler. The actual frustum cull remains unfound; likely inside the terrain extractor or the chunk visibility graph (checklist H3b/H1 stays open).

### 6bl. Fn 0x660ecd0 = bgfx::Caps -> JSON dump (the "Occlusion_Query" string is a caps flag)
Not an occlusion-query pool: 0x660ecd0 (this = caps holder, rcx->rsi) serialises the bgfx capabilities. Fixed block: `homogenousDepth` (byte), `originBottomLeft` (byte), `fpEpsilon` (float), `numGPUs`; then a **supported-flags bitmask** in rbx (u64 at [rip+0xc4d0c73]) tested bit by bit, each hit emitting the flag name into an array: 0x1 Alpha_To_Coverage, 0x2 Blend_Independent, 0x4 Compute, 0x8 Conservative_Raster, 0x10 Draw_Indirect, 0x20 Fragment_Depth, 0x40 Fragment_Ordering, 0x80 Graphics_Debugger, 0x100 HiDPI, 0x400 Index32, 0x800 Instancing, 0x1000 Occlusion_Query, 0x2000 Renderer_Multithreaded; the remaining names follow in this order (bit values not read): Swap_Chain, Texture_2D_Array, Texture_3D, Texture_Blit, Texture_Compare_All, Texture_Compare_Lequal, Texture_Cube_Array, Texture_Direct_Access, Texture_Read_Back, Vertex_Attrib_Half, Vertex_Attrib_Uint10, Vertex_Id, HDR10, Structured_Buffers, Vertex_Attrib_Sint8, Vertex_Attrib_Uint16, Depth_Bias, **Ray_Tracing, Upscaling, No_Fragment_Shader, Buffer_Bind_Offset, Ranged_Resource, Dynamic_Texturehandles** (Mojang bgfx extensions: RTX/DLSS, bindless-ish handles, ranged buffer binds). The mask differs from upstream bgfx numbering (upstream HiDPI is 0x400, HDR10 0x200), so Minecraft's bgfx is a fork with its own cap bits. After the flags come the limits block, in order (names at 0xf5xxxxx): ... `maxOcclusionQueries` (last site 0x660faa3). This helps B-section: the renderer init (slot 58) fills the caps struct, and the flag word at rva-relative [0xc4d0c73+...] is the single place to read what the engine believes the GPU supports; forcing flags there is a way to steer feature use (not tested).
Consequently the occlusion-query helper H2 is NOT here; real occlusion usage lives in the draw path (bit 0x1000 consumer) and was not found.
Note on 6bl: this dump was already identified in 6j / `Caps.h` (A3 [x]; kSupported = rva 0x12adfa50, CapsFlag bit positions HiDPI=8, OcclusionQuery=12, Mojang additions at bit 32+). The bit values above agree with that header; 6bl adds nothing new except ruling out 0x660ecd0 as an occlusion-query user.

### 6bm. Terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0: callee survey (static)
Strings inside: EndPortalForwardPBR / EndPortal / **WaterPrepass** / **RenderChunk** (0x66eab34). Most-called helpers: 0x5679db0 x8 = entt-style sparse-set access (u16 entity, 16-byte packed slots `shl 4`, FNV-1 64 of the component name via the standard 0xcbf29ce484222325 / 0x100000001b3 constants; sets a "dirty" byte at slot+0xC); 0x675e440 x5 = per-item material resolver (FNV hash of the material name from the 0x10-byte name record, checks `this+0x98` loaded state and `this+0xe4 != 0x7fffffff` sentinel; reads a global flag byte via vcall +0x10 with dl=0x30); 0x67a75d0 x5 = builder that copies a 64-byte matrix (4 x movups) from `this+8` into a local and fills a pass record (calls 0xe86b370); 0x5aba5c0 x4 = passLookup (6bg). No frustum math (no mulps chains / plane tests) appears among the direct callees, so culling happens before the extractor, on the CPU chunk side (chunk visibility graph / RenderChunkCoordinator). Next static target for H3b/H1: callers of `RenderChunk` task creation and the mesher's chunk visibility set.

### 6bn. `_launchVisibilityRebuild` 0x7923d50 (task "renderchunk coordinator chunk visibility task") — sub-chunk lookup inlined
Cross-check of 6bk with the live call counts in the L-table (section above): 0x6646980 is the **item visitor** (~134 calls/frame), so it is the per-item dispatcher, as decoded in 6bk.
The launcher body contains an inlined **LevelChunk sub-chunk lookup**, useful for the mesher/visibility work: y (s16 at `pos+0x18`, block y) -> `secY = (u16)(y>>4)`; sentinel `0xF800` (== -2048 section) means "none" -> null; chunk range from `[chunk+0x58]` = height-range object with s16 min block y at `+0xC8` and max at `+0xCA` (`y & 0xFFF0` must satisfy min <= y < max); index = `secY - (min>>4)`; sub-chunk array base `[chunk+0x1C8]`, stride **0x68**; entry `+0x61` (s8) holds the section's own Y and must equal `secY` (stale-slot guard), else null. The launcher also takes three timestamps/locks via `[rdi+0x30]/[rdi+0x38]` (task-group mutexes, 0x31f83d0 = scoped timing/lock) and enqueues the visibility task with the usual lambda vtables (`lea rax,[rip+...]` at 0x792450f/0x7924522/0x7924559). The visibility computation itself (the cave-culling flood fill between sub-chunk faces) is in the task lambda, not in the launcher: to be located via the lambda vtables above (checklist H1b, H3b).

### 6bo. Visibility task lambda vtable 0xF328870 (corrects the "flood fill is in the lambda" guess in 6bn)
Lambda object vtable at rva **0xF328870** (note: rip-relative math, 0x7924560+0x7a04310): slot0 0xec2040 and slot1 0x1c9f50 are generic thunks/destructors (not real functions per vtdump), **slot 2 = 0x792f1a0 (0x14e bytes)**. This is the *completion continuation*, not the flood fill: `this+0x10` is a control block (weak_ptr count at +8, locked with a cmpxchg loop), `this+8` the VisibilityState object (`rsi`), `this+0x18` the RenderChunkCoordinator (`rdi`). Sequence: if `state[0]==1` it clears a held shared_ptr at `state+0x28/+0x30`; then a 3-state CAS on `state+0x20`: **1 -> 3** (done) , else **2 -> 0 and re-calls `_launchVisibilityRebuild(coordinator, &weak)` (0x7923d50)** = "a rebuild was requested while running, run it again"; finally walks the coordinator's listener vector `[coord+0x68 .. +0x70]` (8-byte pointers, skipping null) calling **0x537dab0(listener, state, state+0x3e)** (visibility-updated notification, dirty byte at +0x3e reset to 0 afterwards). Two data points: (1) visibility rebuild is coalesced by a 0/1/2/3 state word at `visState+0x20`; (2) the render side learns of new visibility via the listener vector at `coordinator+0x68`. The computation body (the lambda's run function passed to the task system with name string "renderchunk coordinator chunk visibility task", inside 0x7923d50 itself: the 0x76b-byte region after the 0x792450f lambda setup) still needs its callee 0x6b0c570 (called at 0x79241de before the vector walk) and 0x537dab0 decoded.

### 6bp. Visibility state = 6x6 face-connectivity matrix (fn 0x6b0c570 "compare and mark dirty")
0x6b0c570(state): XORs the six bytes `state+0x38..+0x3d` against a six-byte global (at [rip+0xbef3906..] i.e. rva ~0x12bffe0 region) and tests each XOR with masks **0x3e, 0x3d, 0x3b, 0x37, 0x2f, 0x1f** (all six bits except the row's own face, bit i cleared in row i), ORing the result into `state+0x3e` (dirty). So each visibility-state record holds a **6x6 face-to-face connectivity matrix**, one byte per entry face with 6 bits (self bit ignored): "can a view ray enter this sub-chunk through face i and leave through face j". That is the classic cave-culling graph. The coordinator's flood fill over these matrices (plus the camera frustum) decides which render chunks are visible; the dirty byte at `+0x3e` is what 6bo's continuation resets after notifying listeners (0x537dab0). Checklist H1b/H3b: layout known, the traversal that consumes it is not yet located (look for the reader of `state+0x38..0x3d` besides 0x6b0c570).

### 6bq. Visibility listener 0x537dab0 (args: this, state, flag byte)
`this` (rdi) holds a shared_ptr pair at `+0x9a0/+0x9a8` (renderer-side object `r14`), a level pointer at `+0x998` (-> `+0x678` -> `+0x480` -> `+0x978` = chunk source; vcall +0x140 = getChunk(ChunkPos) taking block coords `pos<<4`), and a "needs update" byte at `+0x9b2`. The state's sub-chunk position (3 x s32 at `state+0x14..+0x1c`) is shifted right 4 to a section position, resolved with 0x533ba90 (returns the render-chunk record for that section, null = ignore). If the flag arg is 0 it asks `r14->vcall(+0x50)(state)` whether the visibility result changed; changed or flag set -> `this+0x9b2 = 1`. It then looks the LevelChunk up through the chunk source and runs the same inlined sub-chunk lookup as 6bn (range test via `[chunk+0x58]+0xC8/+0xCA`, array `[chunk+0x1C8]` stride 0x68, entry `+0x61` Y check). So the listener's job is to map a finished visibility state back onto its sub-chunk and set the frame-rebuild flag `+0x9b2` (this is the LevelBuilder-side consumer, same class as `scheduleChunkBuild` 0x533c910). The face matrix traversal itself remains unlocated; it is run by the task body inside 0x7923d50 / its callees (0x7924xxx..0x7924b6b), not by the listener.

### 6br. Visibility COMPUTE found: 0x791f170 via 0x6b0c4c0, driven by 0x792e630 (corrects 6bp: 0x6b0c570 is the sibling compare-only variant)
- **0x6b0c4c0 (0xaf bytes) = `computeAndStoreVisibility(state, extra)`**: builds a local result `{u8 +0 =1 ... }`, calls **0x791f170(&result, state)** (the compute), then XORs `result+2..+7` (six bytes) against `state+0x38..0x3d` with the six face masks (0x3e,0x3d,0x3b,0x37,0x2f,0x1f), ORs the change flag into `state+0x3e`, then stores: `state+0x38..0x3d <- result[2..7]` (the 6x6 face matrix), `state+2 <- result[0]`, `state+1 <- result[1]`, `state+0 <- result[8]` (valid flag = 1). 0x6b0c570 (6bp) is the compare-without-compute variant. Only callers: **0x792e630 (0x949 bytes)** for 0x6b0c4c0 and the launcher 0x7923d50 for 0x6b0c570.
- **0x791f170 (0x588 bytes) = the cave visibility flood fill**: reads the render-chunk position `state+0x14` (x) and `state+0x1c` (z), adds 8 and `>>4` (chunk x/z centre), looks up the LevelChunk with the chunk source `[this+0x10]` vcall **+0x148**, then the same inlined sub-chunk lookup as 6bn (sentinel 0xF800, range `[chunk+0x58]+0xC8/+0xCA`, array `[chunk+0x1C8]` stride 0x68, Y check `+0x61`). Missing chunk/sub-chunk or Y mismatch -> result `+2 = 0xFFFFFFFF, +6 = 0xFFFF` (all faces connected = never cull). Otherwise it reads the sub-chunk's own connectivity data and runs the traversal; callees 0x7918580 (0x289), 0x32d10f0 (0x174), 0x6b1ba90 (0x422), plus 0x31f83d0 (scoped timer/lock). Body beyond 0x791f272 not read yet (rest of 0x588 bytes).
- **0x792e630 (0x949 bytes)** = the task body that iterates sections and calls 0x6b0c4c0; its other callees: 0x6b1c3e0 (0xcba, large: probably the per-face graph/frustum step), 0x6b1c220, 0x32cb4a0/0x32cb870, 0x2fc9aa0, 0x3005170, 0x6b32870, 0x7177e0.
So the cave-culling compute is now located; what is left is reading its inner loop (face-graph BFS) and the camera-side use of the 6 face bytes by the renderer (H3b).

### 6bs. 0x791f170 phase 1: opaque-voxel occupancy bitmap of one 16x16x16 section (TLS scratch)
Read 0x791f2aa..0x791f68d (the rest, 0x791f6aa onward, is the epilogue/second phase, not read).
- A **thread-local scratch** (TLS slot index at [rip+0xaf57309], pointer `S`) is initialised: `S+0x1a28 = section origin (x s32 @+0x1a28, y @+0x1a2c, z @+0x1a30)` copied from `state+0x14..0x1c`; `S+0x1a38` (**0x1000 bytes**) zeroed = the **occupancy grid** (index `((x&0xff)<<8)|(y<<4)|z`, x/y/z relative to the origin; the code writes `S+0x19f0+0x48+...`), `S+0x1a34 = 0x1000` (count of remaining non-opaque voxels, decremented per opaque voxel marked). `[rax+0x240]` of another TLS object (0x31f83d0 scoped-timer/pool result) gives `rbp` = the reference "air/empty" block pointer.
- `state[0] = 1` (valid), `state[1] = 0x7918580(&state+0x14, chunkSource)` (is-section-on-chunk-edge/has-neighbors flag).
- Triple loop x=0..15 (outer, `[rsp+0x60]`), y/z inner (`r15` 0..15, `[rsp+0x64]`, `[rsp+0x68]`), per voxel: block pointer via `blockArray = [r13+0x30]` vcall `+0x18`(index = `(x<<8)|(y<<4)|z` in r12+r15 form); `block == air(rbp)` -> skip; otherwise **`byte [block+0xA3] == 1` = "opaque / full cube (visibility-blocking)" flag** -> mark the occupancy grid and decrement the count (`dil` tracks "all remaining are opaque"). For every voxel on the section edge (x or z at 0/15 with a flag `state[1]`), the neighbour section is queried with **0x32d10f0(chunkSource, out2, pos)** whose two output bytes (`[rsp+0x5e]`, `[rsp+0x5f]` and `[rsp+0x74/0x75]`) sum `>= 3` clears `state[0]` (valid) — i.e. a section that touches an unloaded/incomplete neighbour is marked not-valid so the cull is skipped.
- Fallback for voxels in a different section (when the section's own palette pointer `[sub+8]` is null) reads a packed nibble array: `(idx>>1)` byte of `[sub+8]`, `idx&1` picks low/high nibble, masked 0xF; if the palette is null, `[sub+0x1c] != 0 -> 0xF else 0` (uniform sub-chunk). That nibble array is 4-bit-per-voxel data at sub-chunk `+8` (block light / sky light / or air mask; 0xF = full). Also `[chunk+0x1518/0x1519]` are two default bytes used when the Y index is out of the sub-chunk vector (vector size computed with the 0x4ec4ec4ec4ec4ec5 /0x68 trick).
Consequence: the flood fill is **voxel-level over opaque blocks** (full-cube flag at `Block+0xA3`), driven per 16^3 section, producing the 6 face-pair bits stored by 6br. Any hack that changes `Block+0xA3` or the occupancy grid (`S+0x1a38`) changes culling (potential "see through terrain / disable cave culling" lever) — untested.

### 6bt. Phase 2: flood fill 0x6b1ba90 over the occupancy grid (face-pair connectivity)
End of 0x791f170 (0x791f6aa): `0x6b1ba90(grid = S+0x19f0, out = rsp+0x74)`, then `result[2..5] <- out[0..3] (dword)`, `result[6..7] <- out[4..5] (word)` = the six face bytes stored by 6br, and `result[8] (state+0) <- dil` (= "all voxels opaque-or-edge" flag from the loop). Per-voxel threshold in phase 1: for neighbour-section voxels the max of the two light/occupancy nibbles `>= 3` clears the valid byte.
0x6b1ba90 itself (grid layout: remaining-count dword at `grid+0x44` (=S+0x1a34), voxel bytes at `grid+0x48` (=S+0x1a38), 0 = empty/not yet visited, non-zero = opaque or visited):
- Early out: `grid[+0x44] >= 0xF00` (i.e. <= 256 opaque voxels, nearly empty section) -> out = `0xFFFFFFFF / 0xFFFF` (everything connected). Same all-ones result the compute uses for a missing section.
- Otherwise out = 0, and it walks the **boundary voxels of the grid** (outer loop `r13 = 0..0xF0 step 0x10`, 16 bytes per step, `r15 = grid+0x57` so index `grid+0x48 + r13 + k`): for each empty boundary voxel, **0x6b1b600(grid, &tmp, &counter)** floods outwards from that voxel through empty voxels, recording which of the 6 faces the filled region touches into a bitmask (`tmp`), and **0x6b1bec0(out, &tmp)** ORs that region's face set into the result: for every face f in the set the row f gets all the other faces in the set (the matrix rows with self-bit masks 0x3e,0x3d,... from 6br). The loop aborts as soon as `grid[+0x44] <= 0xEFF` after a fill (> 256 empty voxels consumed -> remaining search stops early? the `jg 0x6b1bac7` return path), returning the partial matrix. The six calls per 16-byte step cover one face's 256 start cells; the other five faces repeat the same pattern further in the function (0x6b1bc93...).
So the classic Minecraft cave graph: connected components of empty voxels, each component contributes "entering face i, can leave through face j" for all faces it touches. Use: out bytes -> `state+0x38..0x3d`; the renderer's traversal (BFS from the camera section through faces) reads this matrix; reading side still unlocated (candidate: 0x6b1c3e0 called by the task body 0x792e630).

### 6bu. 0x6b1c3e0 is not the reader: it is a second, object-based implementation of the same visibility compute
`0x6b1c3e0(obj, out)` (0xcba bytes; callee 0x6b1d160 (0x4eb) + vector helpers 0x9bc830/0xa4c70/0x75740 "bad array new length") works on a persistent calculator object (`rbx`): chunk source `[obj+0xa020]`, section position `(x @+0xa028, y @+0xa02c, z @+0xa030)`; same inlined sub-chunk lookup as 6bn; copies `Block+0xA3` of all **4096 voxels** into `obj+0x18..+0x1018` (`byte[obj+0x18+i] = [block_i+0xA3]`), then loops `r12 = 0..5` (six faces) using six 4-byte direction/offset tables (rodata at ~0xf0xxxxx, one dword per face for each of r8/r9/r10/r11/rsi) to seed and run a queue/vector-based flood fill (vectors at `obj+0x28/+0x30/+0x38` grown with the `+0x27` aligned-alloc pattern), writing the 6 face bytes to `out` (`out+0..3` dword, `out+4` word, all-ones when the section is missing). So the engine has two implementations: the TLS-grid one (0x791f170 + 0x6b1ba90, used by the task body) and this heap-object one (0x6b1c3e0, likely used by the visibility test/debug path). Both consume the same opaque flag `Block+0xA3` and produce the same 6-byte matrix.
The **consumer** (camera BFS over `state+0x38..0x3d`) is still not found; it is not 0x6b1c3e0. Next static lead: callers of 0x6b0c570/0x6b0c4c0 siblings show only the compute side, so look for reads of `[reg+0x38..0x3d]` in functions that also read the render-chunk neighbour table (LevelRenderer-side `0x7922xxx` family, 0x79225b0 is the other caller of the launcher (0x156c bytes) = the coordinator tick).

### 6bv. Coordinator tick 0x79225b0 and its BFS work queue (0x6b0d380 / 0x6b0d730)
0x79225b0 (0x156c bytes, sole caller 0x53599d0 = the big per-frame renderer function, 0x34ae bytes) calls the visibility launcher 0x7923d50, a hash-map helper 0x7938cb0 (unordered_map growth), and two sibling helpers **0x6b0d380 and 0x6b0d730 (0x367 bytes each)** sitting right next to the 6b0c4c0/6b0c570 visibility-state code.
0x6b0d380(this, vectorOfPositions): `this+0x28/+0x30` and `this+0x40/+0x48` are two alternative (ptr, control block) pairs (shared_ptr to the current render-chunk record, one of them chosen if the first is null; refcount handled with lock inc/dec). Depth = `u8 [chunkRecord+0x25E] + 1` clamped to >= 1. For every 16-byte entry (3 x s32 section pos + one dword) of the input vector `*(rdx) .. *(rdx+8)` it appends a **24-byte (0x18) work record {16 bytes pos, u8 depth at +0x10}** to the work queue vector at `this+0x50 (begin) / +0x58 (end) / +0x60 (cap)` (growth with the 0xaaaaaaaaaaaaaaab /24 trick, aligned-alloc `+0x27` pattern). So the coordinator keeps a **BFS frontier queue of 24-byte {section pos, depth}** records, depth being the number of faces crossed from the camera section; the render-chunk record byte `+0x25E` stores the depth reached. 0x6b0d730 is the same function for the second queue/direction (identical size).
This is the traversal skeleton: pop frontier record -> read its `visState+0x38..0x3d` face bytes -> for each exit face reachable from the entry face push the neighbour with depth+1 (the loop that pops and consumes the matrix is in 0x79225b0 itself, size 0x156c, not yet read). Checklist H1b is now: compute (6br/6bs/6bt), launcher (6bn/6bo), listener (6bq), frontier queue (6bv) known; pop/consume loop in 0x79225b0 pending.

### 6bw. Render-chunk record layout seen from the coordinator tick (0x79225b0, 0x7922f29..0x79230e0)
The tick walks a hash map of dirty sections (node: key = 3 x s32 section pos at `node+0x10/+0x14/+0x18`, weak_ptr `node+0x20` (object) / `node+0x28` (control block) to the render-chunk record). For a found node it locks the weak_ptr (cmpxchg refcount loop) and reads `depth = u8 record[+0x25E]` (the BFS depth of 6bv). When a visibility result is applied (0x7922ff0..0x7923076) it writes into **both** shared render-chunk records referenced by the state (`state+0x40` and `state+0x28`, the two alternatives 0x6b0d380 chooses between):
- `record+0x254 (u32) |= result.flags` (dirty/changed bits: the compute's `+0x20` word),
- `record+0x258 (u32)` and `record+0x25C (u16)` = **the 6 face-connectivity bytes** (copied from the local result at `[rbp+0x68..0x6d]`, same 6 bytes as `state+0x38..0x3d` in 6br),
- `record+0x25E (u8)` = BFS depth.
Then the usual coalescing: if `[coord/state+0x1d] != 0 || state[0] == 1` it does `cmpxchg [state+0x20]: 1 -> 2`, and if the word is non-zero retries `3 -> 0`; when the state reaches 0 it calls `_launchVisibilityRebuild(coordinator, &state)` (0x7923d50). After that comes a chrono duration conversion (rep 0x16e3600 / 0x989680 ratios = ns/ticks) feeding the frame budget, then the frontier queue pushes (call 0x6b0d380 at 0x79231b8, 0x6b0d730 at 0x7923768) for the section just resolved (its 16-byte position at node+0x20). The record fields `+0x254 flags / +0x258 face bytes / +0x25E depth` are therefore the render-side cull data of every render chunk (what the visibility BFS result looks like to the draw path). Readers of `record+0x258` (the per-frame visible-set builder) remain to be found: grep rdi/rax-relative reads of `+0x258/+0x25c` in the 0x53599d0 per-frame function.
Check: 0x53599d0 contains no reads of `+0x254/+0x258/+0x25c/+0x25e` of a render-chunk record (the only matches are its own stack locals at rbp+0x258/0x25c), so the visible-set reader lives elsewhere (candidates: the callee tree of 0x53599d0 and the terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0 callees).

### 6bx. RenderChunk constructor 0x7926f20 (0x212 bytes; caller 0x6b0c680 `makeRenderChunk` 0x2d3 bytes, itself called from 0xa8abd0 / 0x1581700 / 0x4c8aa30) — render-chunk record layout
Args: rcx = record, rdx = section origin (3 x s32 block coords x,y,z), r8b = flag bit, r9d = dword id, stack: byte `dil` (initial depth), ptr `r14` (moved 16-byte pair), ptr `rax` (dword). Layout (offsets from the record):
- `+0x00..+0x1F` zeroed 16 bytes + header; `+0x20` byte 0; `+0x28` qword 0 / dword flags (bit 5 = `r8b`, set via `and 0xffffffdf | (bl<<5)`); `+0x30` dword = r9d.
- `+0x34/+0x38/+0x3C` s32 = section origin (block coords) (compared on re-init: unchanged origin skips the rebuild at 0x79270f1); `+0x40/+0x44/+0x48` = origin + (8,?,8) section centre (`paddd` constant + 8); `+0x44` initialised to 0x7fffffff (sentinel, overwritten).
- `+0x4C..+0x63`: chunk-space bounds of the section's neighbourhood: `(x-1)>>4, 0, (z-1)>>4` and `(x+17)>>4, 0, (z+17)>>4` (min/max chunk x,z used to find the chunks to read while meshing).
- `+0x7C` dword from arg; `+0x80..+0x257` (**0x1D8 bytes**) zeroed = embedded per-layer/mesh slot array (block-layer buffers; not yet decoded).
- `+0x254 u32` dirty flags, `+0x258 u32 + +0x25C u16` face bytes (zeroed here), **`+0x25E u8` = BFS depth (initialised from the ctor arg)**, `+0x260..+0x27F` two vector heads (zeroed), `+0x278/+0x280` = moved pair from the arg (probably a shared_ptr: mesh result / task handle); then 0x79276c0 initialises the origin-dependent members.
This ties 6bw (fields) to the object that the extractor (0x66ea3d0 callers) iterates. Checklist H1b/H3b: layout complete for header; the per-frame code that picks visible records by `+0x25E`/`+0x258` is still unfound, but its object is now identified (RenderChunk, 0x260+ bytes).

### 6by. 0x5334850 (0x5f3c bytes = 24380, sole caller 0x53599d0) = render-chunk event/result processor
Frame 0x253 locals; the event record is at `[rbp+0x238]`: `+0x08` flags (bit1 = section visible/changed, bit3 (0x8) = depth/visibility result, bit0 (0x1) = mesh result), `+0x0A` byte = expected depth, `+0x10` -> state (type dword at +4 must be 1, `+0x28` = RenderChunk record, whose `+0x28` flags bit 1 = "live"), `+0x14..+0x1c` section pos (>>4 to chunk space), `+0x20` = second RenderChunk record pointer, `+0x68` = third record. Findings: (1) accepts an event only if `record.depth (u8 +0x25E)` equals the event's expected depth byte (`cmp al,[rcx+0xa]`) or flag bit1 of the event is set, i.e. **stale visibility results (depth mismatch) are discarded**; (2) when flag 0x8 is set, calls a vcall (+0xC8 of the object at `[[this+0x128]+0x480]`) with `r8b = record+0x25E` = **reports the traversal depth to a listener** (likely the stats/debug overlay "render chunk depth"); then `0x792c3e0(event)` (finalise); (3) takes the section pos, resolves the dispatch-side chunk record with **0x533f580(this+0x250, chunkPos)** and writes `byte [found+0x63] = record+0x25E` — this is how the BFS depth reaches the per-chunk draw record (the 0x533f580 record carries the 3-D chunk table used by the draw path, +0x63 = depth); (4) flag bit0 triggers the FNV-1 hash path (offset basis 0xcbf29ce484222325, prime 0x100000001b3 on `[rbp+0x200..0x202]` = 3-byte keys: chunk-layer pass keys) to look up in a hash map at `[this+0x250]+0x520` — the same pass-name hashing as 6bg; (5) inner loops iterate `[this+0x250]+0x130/0x138` (vector of 0x10-byte entries, each pointing to a chunk source whose `+0x5e8 -> +0xec` count gates processing).
So the per-frame consumer of visibility is not a BFS reader at all here: **depth/visibility reaches draw via 0x533f580's record `+0x63`**; the actual per-draw cull test uses that record. Next static lead: callers of 0x533f580 and readers of `[chunkRecord+0x63]`.

### 6bz. 0x533f580 = chunk-record lookup by section position (only caller: 0x5334850)
`0x533f580(this, ChunkPos*)` (rcx this, rdx = 3 dwords x,y,z; y kept in ebx, x/z in [rsp+0x20/0x24]): fetches `A = [this+0x128]`, a shared_ptr + handle pair at `A+0x428` (object), `A+0x430` (control block, refcount at +0xC locked with `lock inc`), `A+0x438` (u32 handle: low 18 bits `0x3FFFF` = index, `0x3FFFF` itself = invalid sentinel, upper bits = generation) — an **entt-style generational entity handle** into a per-chunk component pool. So the "draw-side record" that receives `+0x63 = depth` is a component slot of the chunk ECS, keyed by chunk position through a hashed lookup (the rest of the function walks the pool by position hash). That explains why no flat array reader of `+0x63` exists: readers fetch the component through the pool (`0x5679db0` sparse-set access seen in 6bm, FNV-1 64 hashing of component names), mainly in the terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0 callees (0x5679db0 x8 calls). Next static step: decode one 0x5679db0 call site in 0x66ea3d0 to learn which component type id carries the depth byte, then find `+0x63` reads on that component type.

### 6ca. Correction to 6bm/6bz: 0x5679db0 is a constant-buffer slot writer with a running content hash, not an ECS sparse set
0x5679db0(this, u16* slotId, Span* data{count, ptr}) (called 8x by the terrain extractor, at e.g. 0x66eb6b5/0x66eb817/0x66eb8bf): slot record = `this+0x30 + (id<<4)`: `u16 [+8]` = byte offset into the data pool `this+0x28`, `[+0xC]` = dirty byte (set to 1); guarded by `offset < this+0x20` (pool size) and non-empty span. It copies `count<<4` bytes (vec4 array: `shl r8,4`) with 0xeec6ec0 (memcpy) to `pool+offset`, then updates a **combined content hash at `this+0x70`**: first folds the slot id (FNV-1 over its two bytes) into the hash with `h' = h ^ (x + 0x9e3779b9 + (h<<6) + (h>>2))` (boost hash_combine), then computes **XXH64 (0x19932c0) of the written bytes** (`count<<4`, seed 0), FNV-1-mixes the 8 result bytes and hash_combines again. So `this` is a ShaderBuffer/uniform staging object (shader constant block with named slots of vec4 arrays); `this+0x70` is a **change hash over everything written**, used as cache key (skip re-upload when unchanged, bind-cache key). Therefore the 8 calls inside the terrain extractor are per-draw uniform writes (fog, lighting, tint, UV animation...), identified by slot ids (u16) rather than names. Correction for 6bz: 0x533f580's handle is a separate mechanism (shared_ptr + 18-bit index/generation) and is not proven to be entt; the depth byte `+0x63` readers still unfound.
Checklist effect: this resolves part of F/G (ShaderBuffer binding): "ShaderBuffer" in the Bind vocabulary (6aq) = this staging object + hash; slot ids are the vec4-array indices of the material's uniform block. Open: map slot ids to uniform names (needs the material uniform tables `tools/lce/matparse.py` + runtime dump).

### 6cb. Terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0: the named ShaderBuffers and bindings it uses (static strings, one name hash per lazily-initialised static)
Each ShaderBuffer/texture name is hashed once with inline **FNV-1 64** (basis 0xcbf29ce484222325, prime 0x100000001b3; 4x-unrolled loop at 0x66f8c22..0x66f8c8a) into a function-local static (store sites 0x66f8c8c -> `rva 0x129f9a90`, 0x66f8d4a -> `0x129f9aa0`, 0x66f8e08 -> `0x129f9ab0`, 0x66f92a9 -> `0x129fb380`; the other statics live at `0x129f8348` (store 0x67ed7d9 in fn 0x67ed710) and `0xf99ffa0`). The slot id passed to 0x5679db0 (6ca) is found by scanning the material's binding array (16-byte entries, entry's record `+0x28` = name hash) for that hash, so it is the **index of the named ShaderBuffer in the material's declared list**.
Names present in the function (strings between 0x66f8208..0x66f8af7): constant/buffer blocks **ViewPositionAndTime, FogAndDistanceControl, MeshContext, TerrainInfo, PBRData, TextureShiftBufferData**; textures **ParallaxTexture, ColorTexture, LightMapTexture, SeasonsTexture**; material/pass names **EndPortalForwardPBR, EndPortal, RenderChunkForwardPBR, RenderChunkPrepass, RenderChunkFogAlpha, WaterPrepass, TextureShiftRenderChunk, TextureShiftRenderChunkPrepass**. This is the exact set of per-draw inputs the terrain draw writes: view position/time (animation, water waves), fog/distance control, per-mesh context (chunk origin), terrain info, PBR data, and UV texture-shift data (animated block textures like water/lava/portal). With the 14 call sites of 0x5679db0 (6ca) this is the full terrain shader interface; Mojang material uniform names match the `.material.bin` samplers/uniforms that `tools/lce/matparse.py` dumps (RenderChunk.material.bin), so slot -> name resolution can be completed by combining the two (static, no live data needed): see `tools/lce/map_material.py`.

### 6cc. RenderChunk.material.bin runtime interface (matparse.py on the installed 1.26.62.01 file) — slot-to-name table
`RenderChunk` (magic RenderDragon.CompiledMaterialDefinition v26, no parent): **3 samplers** (slot, stage): `LightMapTexture` 0, `MatTexture` 1, `SeasonsTexture` 2 (all float, 1 binding). **11 uniforms**, all type 2 (vec4), in file order (= the array index the extractor's hash scan returns, i.e. the ShaderBuffer slot id given to 0x5679db0, 6ca):
0 `DitherParams`(1 vec4), 1 `DitherParams2`(3 vec4), 2 `GlobalRoughness`, 3 `FogAndDistanceControl`, 4 `FogColor`, 5 `LightDiffuseColorAndIlluminance`, 6 `LightWorldSpaceDirection`, 7 `MeshContext`, 8 `RenderChunkFogAlpha`, 9 `SubPixelOffset`, 10 `ViewPositionAndTime`.
The extractor 0x66ea3d0 holds exactly four lazily-hashed names for the direct terrain writes: ViewPositionAndTime (slot 10), FogAndDistanceControl (3), MeshContext (7) and RenderChunkFogAlpha (8; the string appears 4x in the function, once per pass variant). The permutation axes at the end of the header (offset 0x2bd): flags `DepthOnlyOpaque` (14 slots '11111111111111') and axis `Dithering` {Off, On}. (RenderChunkForwardPBR has 15 samplers incl. SeasonsTexture 5, BrdfLUT 0, PBRData 11, plus textures with extra default-value fields: matparse.py crashes on it, the documented limitation of 6ap: sampler record has a variable-length tail for PBR-style samplers.)
So G3's "slot -> name" resolution is complete for RenderChunk; ForwardPBR needs the sampler-tail fix in matparse.py.

### 6cd. matparse.py fixed for structured-buffer samplers; RenderChunkForwardPBR interface (1.26.62.01)
Incident: LeviLauncher migrated its storage on 2026-11-12 (12:06); game versions moved from `Roaming\levilauncher.exe\versions\` to `C:\Users\Atomi\AppData\Local\LeviLauncher\Instances\<ver>\` (BS_EXE and tool defaults updated; see memory `bluey-build-notes`).
Parser fix (`tools/lce/matparse.py`): the sampler record is `name, u8 slot, u8 stage, u32 flags, string type, u32 e, u32 f, u32 g, u8 h`; when **h == 1 the record is a structured buffer and carries a trailing `string structTypeName, u32 structSize`**. This resolves the misparse noted in 6ap.
**RenderChunkForwardPBR**: 15 sampler/buffer slots: textures `SeasonsTexture`(5) `BrdfLUT`(0) `CausticsTexture`(6) `PointLightShadowTextureAtlas`(3) `PreviousFrameAverageLuminance`(4) `ShadowCascades`(8) `LightMapTexture`(1) `MatTexture`(2) `ScatteringBuffer`(7) `SpecularIBLRecords`(9); **structured buffers** (flags 0x06010001, h=1): `PBRData`(11) struct `PBRTextureData` **64 B**, `GpuEntryBuffer`(10) `GpuVolumeEntry` **16 B**, `VoxelBuffer`(12) `VoxelNode` **4 B**, `zLightLookupArray`(13) `LightData` **4 B**, `zLights`(14) `Light` **128 B** (the clustered-lighting light list: this is where VV/RTX forward lighting reads point lights; capped by `max_point_lights` in 6bj). 82 uniforms in total (vec4 type 2, mat4 type 4 with count 8 for cascade matrices), including `ClusterNearFarWidthHeight`, `ClusterDepthBounds`, `ClusterSize`, `ClusterDimensions` (cluster grid), `CascadesShadowInvProj`/`CascadesShadowProj` (8 cascades), `CascadesParameters`, `AtmosphericScattering`, `SkyZenithColor`, `MoonDir`, `CloudShadowProj`, `PlayerShadowProj`, `WaterSurfaceParameters`, `Caustics*`, `ColorGrading_OptimizeGammaCorrection`, `PreExposureEnabled`, `DitherParams(2)`, `FogAndDistanceControl`, `FogColor`, `ViewPositionAndTime`, `WorldOrigin` and combined toggle vectors.
Effect on the checklist: I1b (light volumes: clustered light list `zLights` 128 B/Light + `zLightLookupArray` + `VoxelBuffer`) and I5/I7 (shadow cascades, atmospherics) now have their exact shader inputs; G3 is complete for the two RenderChunk materials.

### 6ce. UI rendering anchors: UI pass extractor 0x674a3a0, UI frame function 0x6664a00, material draw helpers
- **0x674a3a0 (0x4c66 bytes) = UI group extractor** (the Frame renderer UI group, vtable slot 0x368 in 6a*): handles the six UI passes, in code order: `UI/Text` (0x674a59e), `UI/Glint` (0x674a708), `UI/Blit` (0x674bc15), `UI/Base` (0x674bd1f), `UI/Sprite` (0x674c147/0x674c283), `UI/Fill` (0x674cd0e); permutation key `FONT_TYPE` (0x674d2bf/0x674d6be); per-draw shader bindings written by name (hash-by-name like 6cb): `TintColor`, `MatTexture`, `GlyphTexture`, `GlyphSmoothRadius`, `OutlineCutoff`, `ShadowSmoothRadius`, `HalfTexelOffset`, `ShadowColor`, `ShadowOffset`, `GlyphHeight` (font SDF glyph rendering: smooth radius, outline cutoff, drop shadow), `GlintTexture`, `GlintColor`, `UVScale`, `UVOffset`, `UVRotation` (enchant-glint scrolling UVs). Sole caller **0x6664a00 (0x3b10 bytes)**, which itself also handles **`Debug/ImGui`** (0x6664ae9, 0x6668252; `ImGuiProj` 0x66683f6) and `UI/Blit` (0x6664d8f, 0x66682b7); it is called from 0x6664760 (0x218). So UI and the debug ImGui overlay share one frame function; ImGui draws after the UI passes.
- Material draw helpers (by string): `ui_text`/`ui_text_smooth` fn 0x158ef20 (0x13f), `ui_texture_and_color(_tint)` fn 0x6e6fcc0 (0xc74, callers 0x5934e40 0xcf12 bytes and 0x65a7490), `ui_textured_and_glcolor_sprite` / `_bilinear_sprite` / `ui_grayscale` / `ui_item_multicolor_tint` / `ui_inventory_item_glint` fn 0x1c3a790 (0x2c0, callers 0x4cb3430 0x55a6 bytes and 0x6c10370), `ui_invert_overlay` fn 0xe89e00 (0xbcf, caller 0x65a6f00), `ui_skinning_item.skinning_alphatest` fn 0x6b83390 (0x21b, caller 0x6a50290 0x914a bytes = item-in-UI skinned rendering). These are the legacy-UI (Ore UI-less) draw paths; the Ore UI/cohtml path (menu) draws through `cohtml` renderer classes (import names only; call site anchor `0x52d26a0` references the cohtml string at 0xf2424e0).
- Consequences: (I5a) the UI render context writes the font/sprite/fill uniforms listed above by name; UI hooks should target 0x674a3a0 (UI group extraction) or the per-material helpers; (I5c) the font pipeline is SDF glyph rendering (GlyphTexture + GlyphSmoothRadius + OutlineCutoff + ShadowOffset...), `FONT_TYPE` is a material permutation. Still pending: glyph atlas management, Ore UI cohtml texture upload path (I5b).

### 6cf. Ore UI / cohtml integration anchor (I5b)
Fn **0x52d26a0 (0x254e bytes; caller 0x4fc9650, 0xf6 bytes)** is the Ore UI view setup/render glue: it contains the JS bootstrap string `window.__bedrockProvidedCohtmljs = true;` (injected into every hbui page) and the material name `ui_textured_premultiplied_alpha` (the shader used to composite the cohtml-rendered surface, premultiplied alpha), and calls `0x1f4bdc0` (builds `ui://` resource URLs, e.g. `ui://./hbui/index.html`), plus view/texture helpers 0x52d1a10, 0x1f3a2c0, 0x1f383f0, 0xa01db70.. (cohtml wrapper functions are imports, names in the import table at 0xf9ac794+: `IAllocator@cohtml`, `IAsyncResourceHandler`, `IFileSystemReader`, `ILogHandler@Logging@cohtml`, `IInternationalizationManager`...: the engine implements these interfaces and gives cohtml a RenderDragon rendering backend `OreUI::RenderDragonRenderingBackend`). Earlier research (memory `bluey-menu-underui-plan`, 1.26.51): route `/main-menu` of the React bundle `data/gui/dist/hbui/index.html`, panorama is a separate native scene (`CubemapBackgroundScreen`, `InMenuCubeMapObject`) beneath the Ore UI scene; UnderUI injects a pass beneath it. For 1.26.62.01 only the anchor above is re-found statically; the backend's texture upload/draw functions (cohtml surface -> bgfx texture update, slot 55 upload path in 6bb) are not decoded. This is why the checklist keeps I5b partial.

### 6cg. RTX (ray-traced "Vibrant Visuals" / Graphics_Raytracing) pass anchors (I7)
- **0xbbe2d50 (0x1357f = 79,231 bytes) = RTX frame builder/main pass declaration function**: pass names `PreBlasSkinDispatch` (GPU skinning of BLAS vertex buffers before acceleration-structure build, matches slot 63 "RTX GPU skinning" in 6bb), `RTXStub`, 7x `DepthOnlyAlphaTest` (per-geometry-class alpha-tested depth passes), `Core/Screen/Blit`, `RTXPostFX.ToneMapping`, `RTXPostFX.Bloom`, `SceneGammaFallbackBlit` / `FullscreenSceneGammaPass`, `WholeFrameGammaFallbackBlit` / `FullscreenWholeFrameGammaPass`, light uniforms `LightAmbientColorAndIntensity`, `LightWorldSpaceDirection`, `LightDiffuseColorAndIlluminance`, `Color0`, and the acceleration-structure bind name **`RTXAccelerationStructure`** (0xbbf5bea; matches the `Bind::AccelerationStructure` kind in 6aq) — the whole RTX scene is one huge declaration function.
- **0xc28e330 (0xcc6 bytes; caller 0x66232b0, the 46 KB RTX extractor already seen in 6bb/6be)** = RTX resource/G-buffer declaration: `PrimaryHitT0`, `MotionVectors`, `TaaOutput`, `ToneMappingButNoBloom`, G-buffer: `Emissive`, `Metalness`, `Roughness`, `SurfaceOpacityAndObjectCategory`, `SurfaceNormals`, `GeometryNormal0`, `Position`, plus names `G-Buffer Normals/Albedo/Metalness/Emissive/Ambient/Roughness/Split Mask`; denoiser inputs/outputs: `Diffuse0Noisy`, `DiffuseChroma0Noisy`, `Diffuse3PreTemporal`, `DiffuseChroma3PreTemporal`, `Diffuse2Denoised`, `DiffuseChroma2Denoised`, `IncomingIrradianceCache`, `DiffuseMoments0`, `FirstIterationVarianceColor/SH`, `Specular0Noisy`, `Specular3PreTemporal`, `Specular2Denoised`, `SpecularMoments0`, `FirstIterationSpecularVariance`, `DesnoisedSpecularVariance`, `HistoryLength0`, `SunLightShadow0/1`, `AdaptiveDenoiserGradients`, `AdaptiveDenoiserReference`, `FinalOutput`. So the RTX path is a ReSTIR-like/SVGF-style denoiser with 3 stages (0 noisy, 2 denoised, 3 pre-temporal), and `PrimaryHitT0`/`MotionVectors` are the primary-hit buffers also used by DLSS (6bc).
- **0xbcc4b90 (0x189f bytes; caller 0xbc080c0)** = compute pass list of the RTX lighting/denoiser: `ClearVertexIrradianceCache`, `ClearFaceIrradianceCache`, `ResolveLightMeasurement`, `AccumulateInscatter`, `AccumulateGIInscatter`, `ReprojectSpecularOnly`, `BlendCheckerboardFields(SH/Shadow)`, `DiffuseFireflyFilter(SH)`, `SpecularFireflyFilter`, `CalculateFaceData`, `CheckerboardUpscale(SH)`, `TemporalDenoising`, `FinalizeDenoising`, `ToneMappingHistogram`, `CheckerboardInterleave`, `TileClassification` (+ the AdaptiveDenoiser*Inline compute shaders). Irradiance caches per vertex and per face = world-space radiance cache for GI.
- **0xe8403d0 (0xaa5b bytes)** = D3D12 RTX context init: `SuperSampling.NeedsUpdatedDriver`, `SuperSampling.MinDriverVersionMajor/Minor` (DLSS driver gates; see 6bc) and `RayTracingHitDistance` (0xe84a64b); `maxAccelerationStructures`, `avgAccelerationStructureSize` are caps limits (0x660ecd0 caps dump, 6bl). TLAS overflow assert string lives in submit slot 50 (0xe82e230: "TLAS isn't big enough...").
- Graphics-mode UI: `Graphics_Raytracing` (caps flag name), `DeferredRayTrace` (0xf4bc950 render-mode name), `RayTraced` used in the GraphicsMode option handlers 0x7ea7de0 (Simple/Fancy) and 0x88beee0 (Advanced/Fancy), `RayTracingHitDistance` material constant.
Consequence for the checklist (I7): pass list, resource names and denoiser stages are mapped statically; the declaration lambda bodies (inside 0xbbe2d50) and BLAS/TLAS build code (slot 50 / 63) remain undecoded.

### 6ch. Actor/skinning shader interface and bone-matrix anchors (I2b)
- **Batch-actor extractor 0x66d9af0 (0x8bb7 bytes)** writes its per-draw bindings by name (same name-hash mechanism as 6cb), in code order from 0x66df637: `TintedAlphaTestEnabled`, `MetalnessUniform`, `EmissiveUniform`, `RoughnessUniform`, `SubsurfaceUniform`, `PBRTextureFlags`, `ActorFPEpsilon`, `DitheringEnabledToggle`, `MultiplicativeTintColor`, `UVScale`, `GlintColor`, `UseAlphaRewrite`, **`PrevBones`** (0x66dfceb), **`PrevWorld`** (0x66dfd6c), textures `MERSTexture` (metalness/emissive/roughness packed), `NormalTexture`, `MatTexture1`, `MatTexture2`. The short-named current-frame bone uniform (`Bones`) is under the 6-char filter of the string tool and was not listed; `PrevBones`/`PrevWorld` exist so the shader can output **motion vectors from the previous frame's skinned pose** (VV/TAA/DLSS, 6bc).
- **Second actor-side extractor 0x66b2de0 (0x47bc bytes)** has the same `PrevBones` (0x66b7338) / `PrevWorld` (0x66b73b5) pair: it is the previous-pose variant (motion-vector/prepass-side) of the actor draw.
- **Skinned mesh creation**: 0x6b84200 (0x3f8 bytes) builds the geometry object named "Skinned Mesh" (0x6b844bd); 0x6b84680 (0x71c bytes) builds index buffers "Skinned Mesh Quads" / "Skinned Mesh Tris" (0x6b84ac2/0x6b84c54). Together with the skinned-mesh clone 0x6b84e60 (~28 calls/frame, 6 live table) these are the CPU-side meshes that the actor models are skinned into, in the non-RTX path. On the RTX path skinning is done on the GPU in `PreBlasSkinDispatch` before the BLAS build (6cg, slot 63).
- Not decoded: the exact layout of the bone matrix constant block (count/stride of `Bones`, how many bones per ShaderBuffer slot), the Molang-driven bone transform computation (done on CPU in the actor render controller before upload) and the upload call. Natural next step: find the `Bones` slot id in the Actor material uniform list (`tools/lce/matparse.py` on `data/renderer/materials/Actor.material.bin` / `ActorForwardPBR`) and trace its 0x5679db0 writer in 0x66d9af0.

Addendum 6ch (material check, `matparse.py` on 1.26.62.01 `data/renderer/materials`): every actor material declares **`Bones` = mat4 x 8** (type 4, array count 8, 64-byte default): **at most 8 bone matrices per draw/ShaderBuffer slot**; models with more bones are split into several draws (batches of 8), which matches the 0x66d9af0 extractor writing bones through 0x5679db0 (vec4 span = count*4). `ActorForwardPBR` (103 uniforms, 15 samplers) and `ActorPrepass` (30 uniforms, 4 samplers) add **`PrevBones` mat4 x 8** and **`PrevWorld` mat4 x 1** (previous-frame pose and world matrix for motion vectors); plain `Actor` (22 uniforms, 2 samplers) has `Bones` plus tint/fog/dither/UV-animation uniforms (`ChangeColor`, `ColorBased`, `HudOpacity`, `MatColor`, `MultiplicativeTintColor`, `OverlayColor`, `TileLightColor/Intensity`, `UVAnimation`, `UseAlphaRewrite`, `TintedAlphaTestEnabled`, `BlockLightColor`, `FogColor`, `FogControl`). Permutation axes of Actor: flag `DepthOnlyOpaque` (14 slots) and axis `Change_Color` {Off, On}. So bone layout is resolved: **mat4[8] column-major 64 B each = 0x200 bytes per draw**, slot order = file order of the uniform list.

### 6ci. Item-visitor jump table (0x6646980, jump table rva 0xf2bf2d0, u8 tag at `*(r8+8)`, 11 entries)
Tag -> handler entry (sorted by address gives each handler's extent): **0 -> 0x6646a10** (to 0x6646a89; MeshFallbackPosUVNormalColor), **4 -> 0x6646a89** (to 0x6646bc6; builds `ChunkOccluder`), **9 -> 0x6646bc6** (to 0x6646c8d; 0x18-byte item, passLookup 0x5aba5c0 at ctx+0x9d0), **2 -> 0x6646c8d** (to 0x6646d5f), **3 -> 0x6646d5f** (to 0x6646dc2, 0x63 bytes = forwarder), **7 -> 0x6646dc2** (to 0x6646f5b; contains `CracksBlockEntity` at 0x6646e6c = block-entity crack/skinned path, also the inline skinned-mesh extractor of E3), **1 -> 0x6646f5b** (to 0x66471de), **5 -> 0x66471de** (to 0x6647320; contains `Cracks` at 0x6647231), **6 -> 0x6647320** (to 0x6647383, 0x63 bytes forwarder), **10 -> 0x6647383** (to 0x66473e6, 0x63 bytes forwarder), **8 -> 0x66473e6** (largest tail; contains `GlowInkSignForwardPBR` 0x66476d2 and shared tail `MeshFallbackForwardPBR` 0x6647b9b). Default (tag > 10) -> 0x664ef12. Handlers share the epilogue/tail from 0x6647bb4 on (tag 0 jumps there). Mapping vs the checklist: E1b (tag 0/4) = 0x6646a10 / 0x6646a89 now with exact entries; E1c (1/5 Cracks, 2/3/7/9 CracksBlockEntity-path) = 0x6646f5b / 0x66471de and 0x6646c8d / 0x6646d5f / 0x6646dc2 / 0x6646bc6; E1d (6/8/10 forwarders, 8 = GlowInkSign) = 0x6647320 / 0x66473e6 / 0x6647383. Their internal field reads (item record layout, 0xC8-byte records from 6bg) are still to be decoded per handler.

### 6cj. Tag 7 of the item visitor (0x6646dc2) = hash-switch over skinned block-entity overlay materials
Tag 7 reads a pointer at `payload+0x140` (`rdi` = payload; if non-null uses `ptr+0x10`, else a static default), loads the item's **64-bit material-name hash** (`[that]`), and branches through a binary-search tree of FNV-1 64 hash constants: split at `0x416ae22cab22ea36` (jg -> 0x6647449), leaves at `0xd8ef928bc853553a` and **`0x2c8b246fd3eddc59` = `cracks_overlay_tile_entity.skinning`** (0x6646e00, rdata hash table in docs/data/render_hashes.txt). Right subtree (0x6647449): **`0x4b8edb299c93abb3` = `selection_overlay_block_entity.skinning`** and **`0x416ae22cab22ea37` = `selection_overlay_block_entity.skinning_color`**; other leaves `0x6ef4382226de1fd6`, `0x5857c29e32acef88` are not in the hash table (names unresolved). For a matching leaf it builds the pass-name string `CracksBlockEntity` (0x6646e6c, length 0x11) and resolves it with the material/pass resolver **0x675e440** (6bm; `this = [rip+0xc29018b]->+0x120->+0x40`, ctx+0x40/+0x68 passed along) and then continues through the tail (0x6647ff1 on success, 0x664cb91 on miss).
Corrections/identification: tag 7 is **not** a general skinned-mesh extractor; it handles the *skinned block-entity overlay* items (crack overlay on tile entities, selection box/overlay on block entities). The general skinned-mesh clone is 0x6b84e60 (6bm/6ch). The same function's later regions contain additional hash switches for other tags: hash constants found elsewhere in 0x6646980: `map`, `ui_texture_and_color`, `ui_texture_and_color_blur`, `fullscreen_cube_overlay_blend/opaque`, `selection_box`, `wireframe`, `water_hole`, `entity_flat_color_line`, `entity_alphatest` (0x6649e58..0x664c8df), i.e. the visitor decides per item **which pass name/material variant to draw with**, by material hash — the exact control point for a material-swap hook (G6): hook the resolver 0x675e440 (called with the pass-name string) or patch the hash switch inputs.

### 6ck. LIVE capture, 2026-11-12 (after the user restarted game + CE): hardware-breakpoint logging works and the game survives
Method: CE `debug_process(interface=1, Windows native)` + `set_breakpoint(address, capture_registers, capture_stack)` = **hardware execute breakpoint, logging only, NO code written into the game** (the earlier crashes all followed code-cave patches, see 6bd). Base of Minecraft.Windows.exe this run: `0x7FF7784A0000` (pid 20380); code bytes at base+0x6646980 verified `55 41 57 41 56 41 55 41 54 56 57 53 48 81 EC B8` = item visitor of 1.26.62.01. Breakpoint on the item visitor 0x6646980 collected ~16,000 hits in a few seconds and ~42 in a short second window; removed with `remove_breakpoint`, debugger detached, game kept running, code bytes intact.
Findings: (1) the visitor's 3rd argument `r8` points into the **caller's stack frame at RSP+0x48** (not a heap array): `{ void* payload @+0, u8 tag @+8 }`; `get_breakpoint_hits` stack capture starts at RSP+8 (skips the return address), so `stack[8]` = payload pointer and `stack[9] & 0xFF` = tag. (2) tag values seen live: **0, 1, 6, 7, 10** (all in the 0..10 jump-table range; payload pointers are low stack-region addresses 0x148fe0..0x14a148 on the render worker; `rcx`=0x4BDB9FF0F00 (this/visitor context) and `rsi`=0x4BDB8252800 constant across hits; `r9`=0x7FFEF000 constant). (3) Hit rate ~16k per few seconds confirms the earlier ~134 calls/frame figure (dozens of frames). (4) A single RIP breakpoint reports registers truncated for stack pointers (low 32 bits displayed) but heap/code pointers are full 64-bit.
Limits: logging mode cannot dereference payloads at hit time (payload lives in the caller stack and is gone afterwards), so item-layout decoding needs the stack-capture window to include the payload contents (stack_depth up to ~0x80 qwords) — next live step: capture `stack_depth=64` at the handler entries of 6ci to copy the 0xC8-byte item records. `get_breakpoint_hits` output is very large: always pass `limit` small and `clear=true`.

### 6cl. LIVE item payloads (tags 0, 1, 7) via a pause-and-read Lua breakpoint handler
Method that works (no code written, game keeps running): in CE Lua define `debugger_onBreakpoint()` that reads `RDI` (payload pointer at each handler entry, because 0x66469fd does `mov rdi,[r8]` before the jump table), copies 0x200 bytes with `readBytes`, stores them in a global table, removes the breakpoint after 2 captures, calls `debug_continueFromBreakpoint(co_run)` and returns 1; arm with `debug_setBreakpoint(base+handler, 1, bptExecute)` for handlers 0x6646a10 (tag 0), 0x6646dc2 (tag 7), 0x6646f5b (tag 1), 0x66471de (tag 5; no hit in the window). Raw dumps are saved in `docs/data/live_item_payloads_1.26.62.01.txt` (6 records). This beats logging-only breakpoints, which cannot read the payload because it is a long-lived local on the worker's stack that is overwritten between hits (payload addresses are stack addresses like 0x149620 / 0x14A148 / 0x149E40, one stable stack slot per tag handler call chain).
Observations (little-endian floats unless stated):
- **Tag 7 payload (rdi=0x149620)**: starts with two 0x20-byte-ish blocks of floats that repeat (`0.00898, -0.0, 0.00898, -0.0, -0.0, -0.0125, ...`: a decomposed transform/scale block, twice), then a bounds box **(-12,-12,-24)-(12,24,48)** at +0x68..0x7F (`0xC1400000, 0xC1400000, 0xC1C00000, 0x41400000, 0x41C00000, 0x42400000`; changes between samples: (-9,-9,-20)-(9,12,..) i.e. per-model local AABB), three `0x7F7FFFFF` (FLT_MAX, an initial min-bounds), then pointers to heap objects (0x4BE…, e.g. +0x90..: 0x04BE30CCCD65BE…), a vtable-looking qword and so on. **`[rdi+0x140]` = pointer P (0x4BE197C5BF0), `[P+0x10]` = 64-bit material-name hash = `0x0694BEA7E37055A4`** (FNV-1 64, constant across both samples; not in docs/data/render_hashes.txt, so the hash of an actor material name not yet in the table; confirms that tag 7 selects the pass by this hash, 6cj). `[P]` points to itself (P is a list/hash-node head).
- **Tag 1 payload (rdi=0x14A148)**: begins with heap pointer (0x4BE6A654390), then float matrix data (`0.9x, ...` rotation-like entries and a scale of ~0.88/-0.2), colours `E5116D3D` x4 = (0.0572)x4, `0x3F01C056` etc.; a self-referential stack pointer 0x14A148 at +0xC0 and neighbours 0x14A501, 0x14A230, 0x14A580 (stack object graph), then a second copy of the matrix block at +0x120..: payload = {matrix block, flags, self-pointers, matrix copy, 4 colour floats, 2 more floats}.
- **Tag 0 payload (rdi=0x149E40)**: begins with three heap pointers (0x4BE6D0F7B00, 0x4BE6401FC40, 0x4BE6401FC30), then floats (`0.0128, ...`, `1.0 1.0 1.0 1.0` colour at +0x60..0x6F, a 4-float group `0.6641 0.6 0.9 ...`) and more heap pointers at +0x70..; `rbx` = context object 0x4BDB9FF0F00 (constant).
These are raw (field meanings not named yet); each handler's field reads should now be matched against these samples (E1b-d). Hash 0x694BEA7E37055A4 is an open lead: brute-forcing material names from `data/renderer/materials/*.material.bin` (their `name` string) with FNV-1 64 would resolve it.
Update 6cl: FNV-1 64 of all 1,048 material-file names and header strings in `data/renderer/materials/*.material.bin` does NOT produce `0x0694BEA7E37055A4`, so the tag-7 live material is named by a runtime string (likely a render-controller/variant name such as `<material>.skinning` or a resource-pack material key from `*.material` JSON in actor definitions), not a stock material file name. To resolve: capture the string at `P` neighbours (the node at `[rdi+0x140]` probably holds the name string right after the hash, read `P+0x18..+0x40` at the same pause) in the next capture.

### 6cm. Live sample of tag 2 and which tags occur in the current scene
Second capture run (handlers for tags 4 = 0x6646a89, 9 = 0x6646bc6, 2 = 0x6646c8d, 8 = 0x66473e6): **only tag 2 hit** (2 captures, rdi = 0x149900), tags 4 (ChunkOccluder), 9 and 8 (GlowInkSign tail) did not occur in the capture window (current scene: in-world, no occluder geometry or glow ink signs in view). Tag 2 payload layout (rdi = 0x149900, sample appended to `docs/data/live_item_payloads_1.26.62.01.txt`): +0x00 three stack pointers (0x149E98, 0x149EE8, 0x149E98: begin/end/cap of a local vector), +0x18 heap pointer 0x4BE180ACF00, +0x20 code pointer (0x7FF77D8B958C = a function/vtable entry in the exe at rva 0x5415 8C… i.e. base+0x5…: a callback), then floats from +0x28: several identity-pattern 4-wide groups (`1,0,0,0 / 0,1,0,0 / ...`, i.e. 4x4 identity matrices and a 2.0 scale at +0x38/+0x44), and at +0x1F0.. the tint `(0.8, 1.0, 1.0, 1.0)`.
