### 6dk. B3c caution: what the draw emission actually reads (static + live cross-check, 62.01)
Branch B emission (0xe829b7f..0xe829c90) reads its arguments through `r8 = [rbx+0x1b0]`: u16 `[r8+0xC]` (0xFFFF = non-indexed; else `[rbx+0x588] + (idx<<7)` = 0x80-byte buffer record at ctx+0x9B2178, `[rec+0x60]>>5` = whole-buffer index count, `[rec]` = buffer address), u16 `[r8+0x1E]` (index count, 0xFFFF = all), u16 `[r8+0x1C]<<5` (start index), non-indexed args `{[r8+0x10],[r8+0x14],[r8+0x18]}` via cmdlist vtbl `+0x70`, indexed via vtbl `+0x1D8` with `[ctx+0x19F40]` (the command-list array head) and an xmm9 zero pair. **But `[rbx+0x1b0]` is not always the Frame header**: it is set to the header at 0xe8223c6 and then re-written at 0xe821f84-area as `value ^ [rbx+0x1f0]` (a state-diff word) in other paths; live headers have `+0x0C = 0`, `+0x1C/+0x1E = 0` for real draws (their index data is at header `+0x40..+0x5A` per Frame.h), so these fields belong to the *transformed record* the executor builds from header + the 0x540 RenderDraw record, not to the raw header. Treat the argument map above as the shape of the emission, not as header offsets; the exact (header -> record) transform sits between 0xe822480 (branch A, 6df) and 0xe829b50 and is the remaining part of B3c (needs a breakpoint on the vtbl +0x1D8 call at 0xe829c60 to read the final argument registers: index count/start/base vertex and compare them with the header's `+0x40/+0x44/+0x4C`).

**6dk live result (negative):** a read-only execute breakpoint (Lua pause-and-read, auto-removing) on the indexed-draw call `0xe829c60` (62.01) in a running world frame got **0 hits** over several seconds with ~600 items per frame. So the branch-B "indexed draw via cmdlist vtbl +0x1D8" at 0xe829b7f..0xe829c60 is NOT the path normal terrain/actor draws take. Those draws go through another emission site (the instanced/multi-draw or the branch-A continuation after 0xe822480 -> 0xe822a40, or the executor's second class of item). Next step for B3c: place the same breakpoint on the other `call r11`/`call [rip+...]` draw emitters of the executor (candidates: the `0xe822a40` hit path and the cmdlist-wrapper calls listed by `grep "call qword ptr \[rip" exec62.asm` after 0xe822480) and read their arguments. 6cs's "regular path" description therefore applies only to the rare byte3 != 0 && != 1 items that reach 0xe829b50 with the draw record. Debugger detached, bp removed.

### 6dl. LIVE hit counts on the executor (62.01, read-only breakpoints, world frame) [V live, negative result recorded]
- Executor entry `0xe821120`, wrapper `0xe83c390` and submit slot 50 `0xe82e230` are all hit: over the same window **executor 133, wrapper 133, slot 50 = 34** -> about **4 executor jobs per submit** (the job slices of 6cp; one command list each).
- Draw-looking call sites inside the executor are NOT hit: `0xe829be1` (renderer-context virtual `+0x200`, `rbx+0xb0` object, args `(rdx=[rbx+0x90], r8=[rbx+0x658], r9=[rbx+0x660], stack: record [rbx+0x1f0], per-draw 0x40 slot `[rbx+0x90a0]+([rbx+0x1e8]<<6)`, `[rbx+0x318]`)), `0xe829c60` (indexed `+0x1D8`), `0xe829c7d` (non-indexed `+0x70`), `0xe82c9bb` and `0xe82caaf` (second and third `+0x1D8` sites with `[r10+0x5A]<<5` start offsets): **0 hits each**, even though ~600 items per frame are consumed.
Therefore the normal item class does not draw through any of those virtual calls. Candidate explanations: (a) normal items draw through the many calls using vtable offsets `0xD0` (19 sites), `0x90`, `0x98`, `0xF8`, `0x100`, `0x130`, `0xE0` (these are the other cmdlist wrapper methods; one of them may be a multi-draw/`DrawIndexedInstanced` wrapper taking the transformed record), or (b) the items are batched into indirect/"multi draw" buffers consumed by slot 50 after the executor (the executor only records, slot 50 submits). Next: sample the `0xD0`-offset call sites (first: the site at 0xe82c952..0xe82c96b `mov rax,[rax+0xd0]; call` with `r8=&rbx+0xc0` request struct built just before) with the same hit-count method, then read the struct to learn the real draw record.
Used 5 hardware breakpoints at most per run; debugger detached after each run, none left set.

### 6dm. LIVE: the executor makes no indirect (virtual) calls in a normal world frame [V live, negative, 2 samples]
All executor virtual calls go through the CFG/guard dispatch thunk (`[rip+0x11763f1]` -> ntdll `0x7FF9A8CDCD00`, `mov r11,[..]; mov r10,rax; shr r10,9 ...`). A read-only execute breakpoint on that thunk (Lua handler counting `rax` targets whose return address `[rsp]` lies in the executor `0xe821120..0xe82e230`; self-removing after N hits) saw **4 000 and then 17 470 guard-dispatched calls process-wide with ZERO coming from the executor**, while the executor itself ran ~133 times in the previous window (4 jobs per submit). So in a normal world frame the executor walks the ~600 sorted items (state tracking, program/variant resolve, the per-draw table writes seen at 0xe821fa1-0xe822090) **without issuing any command-list call**: it is the *record/validate* pass, and the actual draw/dispatch API calls happen elsewhere (a later stage of submit slot 50, or the RenderDragon-side command encoder the frame graph tasks feed). Consequence for hooks: `Encoder::submit` (6cq) and the frame task list (6cx/6dg) remain the reliable interception points; do not look for D3D12 draw calls in 0xe821120. B3c is kept partial: the remaining step is to hit-count the same thunk filtered to slot 50's range `0xe82e230..0xe836000` (which contains the other `+0x1e0/+0x1e8/+0x1f8` vtable calls listed in 6dl) to find the real draw emitter.

**6dm note (safety, user confirmed the game crashed):** after the 17k-hit sampling on the shared guard thunk the game process was no longer the same one (restarted; CE reattached to a new pid, same image base 0x7FF7784A0000). Do not repeat sampling on process-wide hot addresses; use per-function breakpoints with small hit caps. The slot-50-range sampling planned above was NOT run.

### 6dn. A11 reset entry found: submit prologue + RendererContext slot 60 (+0x1E0) [V static, 62.01]
Submit slot 50 `0xe82e230(this=rcx, Frame*=rdx, ?, r9)` begins with: `cmp dword [this+0x19F88], 5 ; jl 0xe832cee` (command-list/queue state guard), then **if `[this+0x2D8] != 0` (swapchain exists) it calls virtual `+0x1E0` = slot 60 with `rdx = Frame + 0xDC5808`** (the backbuffer size pair; the reset flags u32 sits at `+8` = `Frame+0xDC5810`, FrameOffsets::kResetFlags) and aborts the submit if it returns true (resize/reset happened this frame). **Slot 60 = `0xe81a610` (base D3D12 context; RTX override `0xe83ec70`)**: first flushes the deferred-release ring (`this+0x3C55288` = pending count; `this+0x3C54A08[]` = pending objects, a 0x110-entry ring at `+0x3C55B10/+0x3C55B18`; each is moved to a per-frame list, its `[obj+0x2CBD0] = [this+0x3C563B0]+0x28` stamp written and `[obj+0x2CBD8]=1`, then released through the device object `[this+0xCC9268]` vtable `+0x50`), then applies the **reset flags**: `flags & 0x100` -> `[this+0x19FD8] = 0x10` else `1` (present sync interval/vsync mode), `(flags>>17)&1` -> byte `[this+0x19FDC]` (second present option; on change it walks the command-list arrays `[this+0x19F60]`/`[this+0x19F68]` to rebuild them) followed by swapchain resize code. So `bgfx::reset(width,height,flags)` = writing `Frame+0xDC5808/0xDC5810` on the game thread; the render thread applies it at the top of the next submit via slot 60. Hook point for forcing vsync/resize: slot 60 or the Frame fields.

### 6do. Safe live method (no debugger) and the frame double-buffer, corrected [V live polling]
After two game crashes during breakpoint sessions (user-confirmed, see memory `feedback-ce-multi-cave-crash`), live work now uses **plain memory polling only (no `debugProcess`, no breakpoints)**: a Lua loop reading `ctx = [base+0x12AE1748]`, the two frame pointers `[ctx+0x1CFD0C0]` / `[ctx+0x1CFD0C8]`, their item count `u32 [F+0xDC57E8]` and counter `u64 [F+0xE746D8]`, change-logged. Result in a menu/loading scene (53 items/frame): the frame at **`ctx+0x1CFD0C8` is the one being FILLED by the game thread** (its counter is the newest, N counts up 0,1,2,3,10,13,... to 53 during the frame build), and the frame at **`ctx+0x1CFD0C0` holds the previous, COMPLETE frame (N=53 constant, counter one lower) = the frame the render thread consumes**. At each frame boundary the two pointers swap (counter 7679/7680 -> 7680/7681 ...). This corrects the naming in 6cp ("API frame 0x1CFD0C0 / submit frame 0x1CFD0C8"): `0x1CFD0C0` = submit/consume side, `0x1CFD0C8` = API/fill side (that is why a read of the "submit" pointer at random times often shows the old data, and why my earlier "N=0 at submit entry" reads were on the fill frame right after reset). A complete frame can therefore be snapshotted safely by waiting until `[F0+0xDC57E8]` is stable and the counter of F1 is one higher, then `write_region_to_file(F0, 0xE749C0)` plus the per-draw record array at `[F0+0x8B4700]` (separate heap block, 0x540 bytes per item) for offline decoding of headers/keys/records.
Practical rule for hooks: read items from `[ctx+0x1CFD0C0]`, never from the `0x...C8` frame while the game thread is running.

### 6dp. LIVE (polling only): the per-draw RenderBind record, 0x540 bytes (62.01, menu scene, complete frame of 1130 items) [V live, field meaning inferred]
Complete frame found by polling (counter of the fill frame = consume frame + 1, N stable): `[F0+0x8B4700]` = heap array base (0x67F0000 here), item `i` at `+i*0x540`. Layout seen in items 0, 1, 5 and 1129: **64 binding slots of 0x14 bytes** (`0x500` bytes; slot `k` at `k*0x14`), the default (empty) slot pattern is `{+0x00 u32 0, +0x04 u32 0, +0x08 u32 0xFFFF0000, +0x0C u32 0xFFFFFFFF, +0x10 u32 0}`; a bound texture/sampler slot is e.g. `{+0 = 0x0000056A (handle in the low 16 bits), +4 = 0, +8 = 0x07A00000 (item 5) / 0x021C0000 (item 1129) (flags: high bits sampler/state), +0xC = 0xFFFFFFFF, +0x10 = 0x03000000}`; item 1129 has slots 0 and 1 both bound to handle 0x56A (`+0x14 = 0x56A`, `+0x1C = 0x70000`, `+0x24 = 0x3000000`). The tail after the 64 slots holds the extra fields seen earlier: `+0x500..+0x513` misc, **`+0x514` u32 = murmur bind hash** (6cs), up to `+0x540`. Item 1 also has `+0x60 = 0x200` (a slot-3 field: size/stride). This matches the encoder side `Encoder+0x140 .. +0x653` (0x514 bytes = 64 x 0x14 + 4, hashed with `0x5bd1e995`, 6cq) copied by `memcpy 0x518`. So `setTexture(stage, handle, flags)` writes slot `stage` of 0x14 bytes in the encoder (`+0x140 + stage*0x14`).
Method note: this was read with plain `readBytes` while the game ran; the dump is ~0.7 KB of non-zero dwords per item, small enough to analyse offline.

## 6dq. Executor per-draw matrix cache (static, 62.01)

In executor 0xe821120 (rbx = executor state): `[rbx+0x1e8]` = current matrix-cache index, `[rbx+0x90a0]` = base of a 64-byte-stride matrix array
(`index << 6`, used at 0xe8282c3/0xe828359/0xe829b86/0xe82a87f). The block at 0xe828359..0xe8284xx loads a 4x4 matrix with movaps x4 and
transposes/inverts it with shufps (cofactor math) into rbx+0xF0/0x110/0x1C0 scratch, i.e. per-draw model matrix -> inverse (normal/world-to-model)
computed lazily when the cached matrix handle `word [rbx+0x10e]` differs from last (`word [rbx+0xe174]`). `[rbx+0x130]` = per-draw uniform
range table (8-byte entries: +0x10 offset, +0x14 count, clamped to 4 vec4 per range). Real draw emitter still not located; this is constant-upload side.

## 6dr. Material loading (G1b, static, 62.01)

- Loader: `0xc26caa0` (size 0x277a) reads `materials.index.json` (string 0xf5831eb) and each `<name>.material.bin` (0xf593224, ref at 0xc26d241). It is a
  resumable state machine, not a plain function: rcx=rdi = output holder, rdx = arg stored at frame+0x1d8, r8 = task/coroutine frame whose byte at
  `[r8+0x1c8]` (0..3) is the state (jump table at 0xc26cb4b+0x320c21e). Task-frame fields used: +0x30, +0x58 (r15) / +0x60 (byte), +0x68, +0xb0, +0x118, +0x170.
- State 0 allocates a 0xF0-byte object (allocator = vtbl+8 of the global at 0x60d1646 rip), zeroes it, ctor `0xeec5870(obj, 2)`, sets `+0x50`=0 byte,
  a std::string at +0x58 (SSO cap 0xF at +0x70), size cap 0x200000 at +0x88, 0x2540be400 (10e9) at +0x90, builds a container at +0x98 via `0x24af560`,
  zeroes +0xd8/+0xe0, then stores it into `[rdi]` (the previous object is destroyed with `0xe8fc640` then freed `0xe935700(obj,0xF0)`). So holder[0] = the
  0xF0 "material set" object; holder[+0x20] = next member (rbx). It then allocates a second 0x258-byte object (probably the group/repository).
- Callers: `0x66117f0` (size 0x3fc0, big setup function) and `0xc269790` (0xb48). Neither references useful strings directly.
- MaterialPtr (SDK) = shared_ptr<void> (16B); the control block target is RenderMaterialInfo. The material objects and the runtime lookup (name -> info) are
  still not decoded; G1b stays partial. Next static step: disassemble state 0/1 beyond 0xc26ccc5 to find the 0x258 object's map, then its find-by-name accessor.

### 6dr addendum: the 0x258 object (static)
Built at 0xc26ccad..0xc26ced0, stored into `holder[+8]` (old one released via its vtable slot 0, arg 1). It is three consecutive 0xB8-ish sub-records at +0x0/+0xb0/+0x170,
each: vptr at +0, an rb-tree/list sentinel (self-pointer pair at +0x48/+0x50, +0xf0/+0xf8, +0x1b0/+0x1b8; owner back-pointer at +0x40/+0xe8/+0x1a8),
a small counter dword (+0x58/+0x100/+0x1c0), 0x40 bytes of zeroed state, a sub-init `0xeec5870(sub,2)` and a mutex-like init `0x9bd2e0` at +0xa8/+0x150/+0x210.
Presumably one record per material list (by shader stage / platform file set). Also stored: +0x158=holder[+0x20], +0x160=holder (rdi), +0x168=material-set (0xF0 object), +0x250=0.
After the build, holder[+0x28]=task+0x14, holder[+0x2c]=task+0x18, holder[+0x30]=0x80 (flags/size). The file name list comes from `materials.index.json`
(std::string built at 0xc26d0f0 with 0x9d6d0 = string assign). Still no name->MaterialInfo accessor identified.

## 6ds. F3: the "legacy / other" render path (static + earlier live traces)
Conclusion: 1.26.62.01 has no separate legacy forward frame renderer. Evidence: (1) the Fancy-mode live trace (6ab) runs the shared Frame renderer 0x67c9c20 and the shared
material-draw functions 0x66xxxxx-0x67xxxxx, with none of the VV/RTX lambdas of 0xbb3..0xbcd entered; (2) the only frame-graph *builder* with named pass declarations is the
giant 0x55f1450 (VV, 6p); no "FancyFrame*" / "LegacyFrame*" strings exist (searched); callers of 0x55f1450 and 0x67c9c20 are indirect (vtable / std::function), so the mode switch is a
data choice (GraphicsMode component, string 0xf5e49cc used at 0x3a10dc8, 0x3a10ed1, 0x7ea7e17, 0x88bef36) rather than separate call chains; (3) "legacy" survives only as
(a) LegacyCubemap sky pass (RenderLegacyCubemapDescription 0x660b1a0; LegacyCubemapForwardPBR in 0x66560f0) and (b) the legacy-UI material draw helpers listed in 6cz (ui_* fns 0x158ef20, 0x6e6fcc0, 0x1c3a790, 0xe89e00, 0x6b83390).
Remaining unknown (not needed for hooks): which function reads GraphicsMode to choose builder vs Fancy task list. Start from 0x3a10d20/0x3a10e40/0x7ea7de0/0x88beee0.

## 6dt. E5a: component vocabulary (static, 62.01)
The exe keeps one `entt::internal::pretty_function() [Type = X]` string per registered ECS type. Extracted all of them: **12,836 type names**
(`docs/data/entt_types_1.26.62.01.txt`), of which 758 are plain `*Component` names; the 94 render/camera/anim/client-related ones are in
`docs/data/entt_render_components_1.26.62.01.txt`. Render-relevant ones: `RenderPositionComponent`, `RenderRotationComponent`, `RenderOffsetsItemComponent`,
`DynamicRenderOffsetComponent`, `GlobalActorRenderComponent`, `AtomicEntityShouldRenderComponent`, `VisibilityCacheComponent`, `TintColorComponent`, `GlintItemComponent`,
`RelativeShadowOffsetComponent`, `PassengerRenderingRidingOffsetComponent`, `VehicleRenderingRidingOffsetComponent`, `MovementInterpolatorComponent`,
`QueuedMovementInterpolationComponent`, `UpdateAndRenderThrottleComponent`, `FogCommandComponent`, `GlobalTextureGroupStateComponent`, `CameraShakeComponent`,
`CameraClientInstanceComponent`, `ServerActiveCameraComponent`, `ClientParticle{Initialization,Termination,Tracking}Component`, `EmitParticleRequestComponent`,
`AtomicEntityAnimStateComponent` / `AtomicEntityAnimationContextComponent`, `AtomicEntityParticleTrackingComponent`, `PlayerInteractionModelComponent`. The CameraAimAssist* components
(frustum cache etc.) are gameplay, not rendering. Framebuilder description types stay in docs/RENDER_CATALOG.md. Where each component's storage lives (E5b) is still open.

## 6du. E5b: entt storage layout (already implemented in the SDK; summarised here)
`EntityContext` (0x18): {registry-holder ptr, `mRegistry` = entt::basic_registry<EntityId>*, EntityId}. Pools are found by the component's entt type hash (`poolFor(registry, hash)`),
type name read from the pool's `info` (type_info { u32 seq, u32 id, string_view alias }; alias e.g. "struct StateVectorComponent"). `EnttSparseSet` (0x50): vtable, sparse
GameVector<u32*> pages of 2048, packed GameVector<u32>, info ptr, mode, head (free-list). `EnttStorage<T>` adds payload GameVector<T*> pages of 128 elements. Entity id: low bits index
(`entityMask`), high bits version; a packed slot whose index bits == entityMask is a tombstone. Lookup = sparse[page][pos%2048] -> packed index -> payload[idx/128][idx%128]
(`tryGet`). Files: `src/sdk/mc/deps/ecs/EnttStorage.h`, `gamerefs_entity/EntityContext.{h,cpp}`. Reading frame-object components from the render registry uses the same layout with
the render registry pointer (the dragon::frameobject registry, 6aa) in place of the actor registry.

## 6dv. C6: Deferred Texture/Buffer Transactions (static, 62.01)
- Flush function `0x6632bf0(rcx = DeferredTransactions*)` (strings "Deferred Texture Transactions" and "Deferred Buffer Transactions" at 0x6632c19/0x6632c42): the argument holds
  two labelled `std::vector<Transaction*>` ({begin,end,cap} at +0x00 and +0x18; each followed by its trace-scope name pointer). Both are moved out (source zeroed), passed to `0x12893d0(pair, &outVec)`
  which produces one merged vector of job pointers, then each job is run through **vtable slot 1** (`[vtbl+8]`) and then destroyed through **slot 0** (`[vtbl+0]`, edx=1 = deleting dtor);
  the vector storage is freed (size >= 0x1000 takes the aligned-free path). So texture and buffer creation/upload requests recorded during extraction are queued and executed in one go.
- Also referenced from `BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame` 0x66232b0 at 0x66244e7 and 0x66264e6 (two exits of endFrame, with the "Persistent UI Color/Depth" scopes), i.e. the flush runs
  at the end of the frame builder before the frame renderer 0x67c9c20.
- "RenderDragon Deferred Textures" string at 0xf5573d6 is referenced only by 0x661a770 (+0x661c51e): the deferred-texture resource owner (name given to the texture group); not decoded further.
- Aliasing/lifetime: no code in this path aliases memory; resource lifetime is by `shared_ptr` handles in the pass dependency records (6cz) plus Bind* declarations (6aq). Marked partial.

## 6dw. H3a: where the camera / projection / view live (consolidated)
Three consistent sources, all already readable:
1. **bgfx per-view record** (authoritative for what is drawn): `ctx = [base+0x12AE1748]`, `V = ctx + 0x1EAB090 + viewId*0x140`; `V+0x10` view (identity: camera-relative rendering; camera translation sits in per-draw transforms),
   `V+0x50` projection (live: FOV ~76.9 deg, aspect 1920/1137, 6dd), `V+0x90/+0xD0` previous view/proj for TAA/motion, rect at V+0 (6dd, `bgfx/View.h`). Written each frame by the task body `0x568CD90`.
2. **ClientInstance** (game side): `getViewMatrix()` = CI+0x418 world view mat4; projection found by shape via `findProjectionMatrix(aspect)` (SDK `ClientInstance.h`).
3. **LevelRendererPlayer**: `cameraPos()` (OffsetProvider LevelRendererPlayer_mCameraPos) and `fovScale()` (mFovX/mFovY); mFov at +0x1238 is not the live FOV, which comes from the first-person CameraComponent (CameraHook).
Setup order: CameraComponent/LevelRendererPlayer update on the game thread -> frame-builder inserts copy matrices into the Scene/HUD task description -> `0x568CD90` copies them into the per-view record -> executor reads them via the view table.
For world-to-screen use source 2/3 on the game thread; for hooks inside the render thread use source 1.

## 6dx. D5c: ChunkOccluders description (static, 62.01)
- Clone `0x5369920` is a **thin** insert: it does not copy the description. It builds a visitor variant on the stack, `{ptr = description at +0x10, tag byte = 4 at +0x18}`, wrapped in the
  "FrameBuilder - Insert" trace scope (string 0xa1c4680, once-init guard), and calls **builder vtable slot 0x378/8 (Item group insert)** with it. So the Item group receives a pointer to the live description.
- Visitor tag 4 handler `0x6646a89`: reads per-frame context `ctx = [rbp+0x8a0]`, registry-ish `ctx+0x68`, `ctx+0x40`, then calls `0x67d6d70(out, ..., "ChunkOccluder" (len 0xd), ...)` to find/create the render
  object named "ChunkOccluder" (the same string is also referenced at 0x664ee5f), checks `byte [obj+0x10] == 1` (object valid/enabled), takes a shared_ptr copy (`lock inc [ctrl+8]`) and proceeds to fill it.
  It never calls the terrain extractor 0x66ea3d0 (that is tag 3). Description fields read by the handler: `+0x30 +0x38 +0x40 +0x48` (four 8-byte values: pointers/spans for occluder boxes; exact types pending).
- Use: the occluder list feeds the visibility/occlusion pass (RenderChunk occlusion strings, 6bf). Same thin-clone pattern (pointer + tag) applies to ItemInHand 0x531b180 (tag 6) and Crack 0x539fff0 (tag 5, fat 0x996: copies fields); field offsets for tags 0/1/2/7 are in 6cj (line "Descriptor object field offsets").

## 6dy. J1: frame-level takeover design (written against A-F, untested in game)
Seams, from earliest (game thread) to latest (GPU submit), each with the data it gives and what it can change:
1. **FrameBuilder::insert clones** (D, 6dx, DescriptionClones.h; FrameBuilderHook): see/alter/drop every description (actors, chunks, particles, UI) before it enters the frame. Drop = skip the original; alter = edit the description in place (thin clones pass a live pointer).
2. **BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame 0x66232b0 / Frame renderer 0x67c9c20**: whole-frame boundary; the deferred texture/buffer flush (6dv) runs here. Good place for per-frame setup/teardown of custom resources.
3. **BgfxContext::endFrame 0xc270460 -> frame-graph generate 0xc273420 -> task dispatch 0xe9002e0**: the task list (0x80-byte entries, name hash at task common vtable layout, 6cx/6da): pass enable/disable (swap the std::function impl at task+0x70 for a no-op stub), reorder (swap raw entries), insert a custom task (clone an entry and point +0x70 at our callable). Pass identity by FNV-1a 64 name hash (PassNames.h). This is the primary "takeover" seam: one hook, whole pass list.
4. **bgfx Encoder::submit 0xbc60740 family**: per-draw program/state/view/bindings (6cq/6ct); veto or rewrite a draw (state bits, program, textures via the RenderBind record, 6dp).
5. **Frame::sort 0xbc61e20 / sort keys**: view-order control (viewId in bits 54-63, mode tag 48-53); reorder views without touching passes.
6. **D3D12 executor 0xe821120 / submit slot 50 0xe82e230 / PSO builder 0xe81e860**: GPU-side: replace PSO (key mask 0x0707007FFFFFF0FF), per-draw constants (matrix cache 6dq).
Camera data: 6dw. Materials: G1a file format + 6dr loader. Component data: 6dt/6du.
Validation status: seams 3-6 are anchored by static analysis + live counters (6cx/6da/6dp) and are consistent with each other (same pass names appear in the task list, in the sort keys' viewIds and in the executor loop); none has been exercised with a patch in game (user will test "big bang"; debugger-free methods only). Risks: task-list entries are shared_ptr<Task> (disable by stub, never free); frame pointers alternate each frame (fill vs consume, 6do); RVAs must be re-checked with SelfTest sigs (6dj) at startup. 

## 6dz. D5e: ItemInHand description (static, 62.01)
Path: thin clone `0x531b180` -> Item group vslot 0x378 -> visitor tag 6 -> forwarder `0x6647320` (restores xmm6-13, `jmp`) -> **extractor `0x6698e30(rcx=builder state, rdx=per-frame ctx, r8=description)`** (size 0x5fb4;
strings ItemInHandForwardPBR/Prepass/Glint/Textured). It keeps the description pointer in r14. Fields it reads (`desc+`):
- `+0x0C` shared_ptr control-block refcount slot (`lock dec [desc+0xc]` after copies: the description holds a shared_ptr at +0x08/+0x10 pair, refcount+0x0C weak/strong).
- `+0x18` u8 kind (compared with 2 at 0x669b419 -> then `[desc+8]` used as the object pointer).
- `+0x28` qword pointer (item/mesh data; fed to the first processing block at 0x6698ede, `[rax+0x40]` follows it).
- `+0x38..+0x77` **16 floats = 4x4 transform** (copied float by float into the draw's constant block at 0x669b04a..0x669b0ee; `[r15+0x28]` is the destination).
- `+0x78` u8 flags (bit 3 (0x08) selects the second variant: `test al,8; sete cl`), `+0x7A` u8 flag (OR'd into the first-person/overlay bool at 0x6698ead; also in the pass flags at 0x669b13d).
- `+0x11C` u16 (stored to the draw record at +0x19f8, with a valid byte 1 at +0x19fa), `+0x158` 16 bytes, `+0x168` 8 bytes, `+0x170` 16 bytes (copied together to record +0x19c8/+0x19d8/+0x19e4: tint/glint colour blocks, written with a valid byte at +0x19f4).
- `+0x1D0` u32 and `+0x1D4` u8 (u8 flag gates the u32 use at 0x6699805..0x669980f: variant/override id).
- `+0x58/+0x60` are also used as begin/end of a vector at 0x66997ba (`[+0x60]-[+0x58]`): the matrix block and this overlap, so one of the two readers sees a different pointer in r14 (not resolved).
Types of the +0x158.. blocks (colour vs UV) and the meaning of +0x1D0 remain open; the description (+0x38 matrix, +0x28 pointer, +0x78/+0x7A flags) is enough to identify and transform held items.

## 6ea. D5d: Crack description clone 0x539fff0 (static, 62.01; interpretation partial)
Unlike the thin clones, `0x539fff0` (fat, 0x996 bytes of code, frame 0x1c8) **builds** the crack description. Args: rcx = builder (rdi), rdx = source object A (`[rdx+0x28]` -> r14, a big object with `+0x18`, `+0x74` (u8), `+0xac8` (pointer -> `[x+0x24]` float scale used for the crack stage: `cvttss2si(float * xmm7)`), `[rdx+0xc]`), r8 = source object B (rbx, a large manager: fields at **+0x3280 / +0x3288** (container begin/end or head/sentinel), **+0x3298, +0x32a0, +0x32a8, +0x32b0** (further pointer pairs, null-checked at 0x53a0367)).
It walks the intrusive list rooted at B+0x3280 (`rbx = [rbx]` next link at 0x53a07d0, stops at the sentinel rdi) and for every node with `float [node+0x20] > 0` (progress) and a live block lookup (vcall slot +0x10 of `[r15+0x978]` with key `node+0x10`, then `0x5391130(r15, block)` validity, then vcall slot +0x140 returning the block pointer):
`node+0x10` u8 (low nibble) and `node+0x18` u8 (low nibble) and `node+0x14` u16 (minus `[region+0x3a]`) are packed into a small record at [rbp+0xf0..0xf2] (x/z low nibble, y delta relative to the region's min section = a block position inside a chunk/section), then the crack stage `progress*scale` is computed. The result is inserted through the Item group (tag 5, "FrameBuilder - Insert" scope at 0x53a0601). So: crack records come from a per-level list of "destroy progress" nodes (position + progress float at +0x20), one description insert per frame, not per block. Exact node struct (the position is a BlockPos packed into +0x10..+0x18) and the final description fields stay open.

## 6eb. D5i: EnvironmentalText / UIText clone 0xe71330 (static, 62.01; shared clone, partial)
One clone (size ~0x900 bytes, frame 0x308) serves both `RenderEnvironmentalTextDescription` and `RenderUITextDescription`: args rcx = builder (r12/[rbp+0x228]), rdx = description, r8 = pass/context ([rbp+0x250]), r9 = r13 extra. It ends in two different group inserts: **Item group vslot 0x378** (tail at 0xe71c51; EnvironmentalText = in-world text such as signs/hanging signs) and **UI group vslot 0x368** (tail at 0xe72187; variant tag byte 3 written at [rbp+0x30]+8; UIText), each wrapped in "FrameBuilder - Insert" (0xe7228c / 0xe72322).
Description fields read: `+0x0C` shared_ptr refcount slot; `+0x60` embedded sub-struct (address taken at 0xe71543); `+0x75` u8 (==1 selects a branch at 0xe71708); **`+0x90` string pointer, `+0x98` its shared_ptr control block (`lock inc [ctrl+8]` copy), `+0xA0` length/size word** (the text payload is a shared string view: ptr, control, size), `+0xB0` pointer to a settings/font object whose byte `[+0x256]` disables the whole insert when non-zero (0xe713d7: when set the string copy is released and the insert skips). Empty text (`ptr == null` or first byte 0) returns early (0xe713c6).
Use: world/UI text strings can be read or replaced via the shared string at desc+0x90 before the clone runs (hook the clone, edit desc+0x90/+0xa0). Types of the remaining +0x60 sub-struct (colour/size/anchor) are not decoded.

## 6ec. D5f: SkinnedMesh description clones (static, 62.01; partial)
Two clones insert `RenderSkinnedMeshDescription` into the Item group (tag 7, vslot 0x378, "FrameBuilder - Insert" scopes at 0x4e3a737 / 0x6b869de):
- **`0x6b84e60` (general clone, 0x1800 bytes of code, frame 0x3d8)**: rcx = this (first byte must be 1 or the clone returns at 0x6b84e86), rdx = description (rsi), r9 = extra (r15). Description fields: `+0x0C` shared refcount slot (`lock dec [+0xc]` at the end);
  `+0x10` pointer (arg to the 0x128-offset resolver, repeated for 5 render-state variants at 0x6b85581/..697/..7aa/..8bd/..9d0: `0x128 + [desc+0x20]` computes a per-variant sub-object address); `+0x18` pointer (-> `[x+0x60]`); `+0x20` pointer (base of that variant array); 
  `+0x30` and `+0x38` pointers to small objects whose `u32 [obj+0x10]` is read (two ids/sizes, e.g. geometry and material handles; they go into the draw records at +0x10/+0x18 of r13/r15 entries); `+0x60` embedded struct (address passed on at 0x6b86581: transform/params); flag bytes **`+0x74`, `+0x75`, `+0x76`** (0x6b86575..0x6b8657d, packed into the pass flags); **`+0x108` ptr, `+0x110` control block (refcount at +8), `+0x118` third word** (a shared_ptr + extra copied to a local at 0x6b84ec6: probably the bone/animation data or mesh handle).
- **`0x4e39f20`** (0x1e0 lines, frame 0x508, xmm6-15 saved): the second SkinnedMesh producer (same Item tag 7); reads the description only through `[desc+0xc]` and the output slot; it builds from the game's own structures instead (`[r12+0xc0]` 0x60-stride array base, `r10*3<<5` = 0x60 stride entries) and stores the result back into the description at `[desc]` (+8 dword). Not decoded further.
Combined with 6cv (tag 7 live: model matrix pair, AABB, material node at +0x140 of the *item* payload) this means: skinned entity/block-entity geometry is identified by the descriptor's +0x30/+0x38/+0x108 data, and the item payload carries the per-draw transforms.

## 6ed. D6b: EndSky / CelestialBody / Cubemap / LegacyCubemap clones (static, 62.01; partial)
All four insert into the **Sky group, builder vslot 0x370**, with a variant-tag byte written at `[rbp+0x18]` (thin) / `[rbp-0x48]` (fat):
| Description | Clone | Kind | Sky tag |
|---|---|---|---|
| RenderEndSkyDescription | `0x53707a0` | thin (70 lines, pointer pass-through) | **3** |
| RenderCelestialBodyDescription | `0x5370b20` | thin | **7** |
| RenderCubemapDescription | `0x660b720` | thin | **2** |
| RenderLegacyCubemapDescription | `0x660b1a0` | fat (0x390), 217 lines | **4** (matches 6bs: Sky 0 Ambient/Atmosphere, 1 Clouds, 2 Cubemap, 3 EndSky, 4 LegacyCubemap, 5 Sky/Stars, 7 CelestialBody, 8 VolumetricFog, 9 Weather, 10 SkyAmbientSamples) |
Thin clones: identical 70-line body, trace scope "FrameBuilder - Insert", variant `{ptr = description, tag}` passed to the group's insert; the description is read later by the Sky visitor (so a hook on the clone sees the live description pointer in rdx). 
LegacyCubemap fat clone `0x660b1a0`: its arguments are (rcx = description-like object with `+8` ptr, `+0x10`/`+0x11` u8 flags, `+0x58` u16), (rdx = context whose `+0x18` points to a record that gets byte `+0x78 = 1` written: "sky drawn" flag, and `+0x74` u8 read at 0x660b337), and a stack argument (`[rbp+0x140]`, a 4-way variant selected by byte `+0x20` through a jump table at 0x660b24a: 0 = nothing, 1/2/3 = pick a texture pair from the object's `+0x0/+0x8` shared pointers). It copies the selected texture handles ([rbp+0x80]/[rbp+0x88] shared_ptr, refcounted) into a local record at [rbp+0x10..], then inserts it with tag 4. The texture selection (cubemap faces) is the only per-frame data; the rest is flags. Field names beyond that are open.
Next lead for exact fields: the Sky visitor handlers for tags 2, 3, 4, 7 in the 0x6646980 jump table (case targets listed in 6ci).

## 6ef. E8: typed ExtractionObserver (implemented, not built into the DLL yet)
`src/client/features/hooks/hooks/render/ExtractionObserver.h`: header-only registry keyed by (Group, variant tag): `add(Group, tag-or-kAnyTag, Listener, user)` before hooks are enabled (max 16 entries, lock-free dispatch), `Listener(const Event{group, tag, description*}, user) -> bool drop`.
Tag enums for the Sky (11), Item (11) and UI (4) groups come from 6w/6ed. `FrameFilterHook::ffSlot` (Item, vslot 0x378), `ffSlotSky` (Sky, 0x370) and `ffSlot340` (UI, 0x368) call `ExtractionObserver::dispatch` first and return 0 (drop) when a listener vetoes. The Effect (0x360), Light (0x380) and Editor (0x358) group slots are not hooked yet (their types: 6ee); add a detour per slot the same way when needed.
Checked: the header compiles standalone (MSVC /Zs). FrameFilterHook.cpp not rebuilt (the user builds and tests later).

## 6eg. B11 and C9: SDK headers written (compile-checked with MSVC /Zs, not built into the DLL)
Correction first: the "MSVC syntax check" of ExtractionObserver.h reported in 6ef did not actually run (`cl` was not on the PATH in that shell). It was redone with vcvars64 (`C:\Program Files\Microsoft Visual Studio\18\Community\VC\Auxiliary\Build\vcvars64.bat`) and a deliberately broken control file that did fail; ExtractionObserver.h, D3D12Context.h and Tasks.h all compile clean.
- `src/sdk/mc/deps/bgfx/d3d12/D3D12Context.h` (B11): D3D12Rvas (global 0x12BB4910, vtables, submit 0xE82E230, executor 0xE821120, PSO builder 0xE81E860, reset 0xE81A610, device-lost 0xE8102B0, init 0xE807FF0), D3D12Offsets (+0x19F58 resource tables, +0xCC9268 queue, +0x19F88 state ...), D3D12Tables (six resource tables with base and stride), D3D12Slots. Constants only, each tagged [V]/[I].
- `src/sdk/mc/deps/minecraft_renderer/dragon/Tasks.h` (C9): TaskRvas (dispatch 0xE9002E0, five task class vtables), `TaskListEntry` (0x80 stride, static_assert), `TaskList` (begin/end), `TaskOffsets` (functor +0x70, args +0x78, name offsets per class) and `taskName(task, imageBase)` (reads the class's embedded std::string). Pass-name hashes stay in dragon/PassNames.h. The detour that walks the list on every frame (the pass enumeration hook) is not written yet; the recipe is 6dg/6dy.

## 6eh. D5h: BlockSelectionOverlayBlockEntity clone 0x539b7c0 (static, 62.01; partial)
Fat clone (1474 lines, frame 0x638), Item group tag 8 (vslot 0x378 at 0x539c927, "FrameBuilder - Insert" at 0x539d34a); same shape as the Crack clone (6ea). Args: rcx = builder (rbx), rdx = source A (`[rdx+0x28]` -> r14, `[r14+0xb0]` -> r12: the client-side holder of the selection data), r8 = BlockSource/region (rsi), r9 saved at [rbp+0x598], and a **stack argument at [rbp+0x620] = pointer to a block position** (rdi): x low nibble at `[pos+0]`, z low nibble at `[pos+8]` (byte), y word at `[pos+4]` minus `[region+0x3a]` (section min y). It asks the region for the block entity at that position (vcall slot `+0x140` of r8, 0x539b816) and, when it exists, packs `{x&0xf, z&0xf, y - minY}` into a 4-byte local at [rbp+0x50..0x52] and calls `0x3853770(blockEntity, &packed)` (get the block-entity render data for that sub-chunk position). The rest builds the overlay description (matrix floats at +0x378.., 1.0f constants at 0x539cc08) and inserts it with tag 8. So the overlay exists only for the currently selected block when it is a block entity (chests, signs ... "selection_overlay_block_entity.skinning" material, 6bk). Field-level layout of the produced description is open.

## 6ei. A12 fork additions + B10c: what the exe shows (static, 62.01; all partial)
Tool: `scratchpad/fork_strs.py` style scan (one pass indexing every rip-relative `lea`, then filtering string refs to the bgfx / D3D12 / frame-graph regions).
- **A12a dynamic texture handles** (`bgfx::Context::init` 0xbc66de0): after the handle allocators are set up, `test byte [rva 0x12ADFA54], 0x10` (a global caps/feature byte, bit 4 = renderer supports dynamic texture handles) selects the branch: when clear and the byte `ctx+0x1ebf2f3` (guarded-allocation flag) is 0 it logs *"Texture handle allocation must be guarded if renderer context does not support dynamic texture handles."* (0xbc67296). When set it compares reset flag **0x400000** in `ctx+0x1ebf19c` (requested) against the same bit in the consume frame's flags `[[ctx+0x1cfd0c8]+0xdc5810]`: turning it off after it was on logs *"Disabling dynamic texture handles is not supported."* (0xbc672cb); otherwise it clears bytes `ctx+0x1df7480` and `ctx+0xe62610`. So dynamic handles = reset flag 0x400000, with a free-list in the handle allocator (word counters at ctx+0x1dfbe94, ids at +0x1dfbe98, 4096 entries; same layout as 6dm). Allocation path itself (createDynamicTexture, 0xbc74a80 region, 6am) already listed.
- **A12b ranged resources / buffer bind offset**: the only string evidence is in the RTX skinning slot 63 (`0xe83f1d0`, ref 0xe83fbb0): *"Our skinning vertex buffer is asking for a range that will extend past the end of the buffer."*, i.e. the fork's vertex-buffer bind takes (offset, size) ranges and validates offset+size against the buffer. The draw header already carries a vertex byte offset (+0x50) and counts (6ct), consistent with ranged binds. The setter API (encoder-side overloads) is not isolated.
- **A12c/B10c ray tracing**: RTX frame builder `0xbbe2d50` references the strings `PreBlasSkinDispatch` (0xbbe4387, pass body 0xbbe4399), `RTXAccelerationStructure` (0xbbf5bea) and RTX resources (6cg); `PrepareTLAS` pass body 0xbbe5c43, `PreBlasSkinning` 0xbcc5aa7, `RayTracingHitDistance` (0xe84a64b in 0xe8403d0). The RTX submit override 0xe83d440 does the TLAS/BLAS update before the base submit (6bi). Handle/call API of the fork is not isolated; BLAS/TLAS build calls are not located (needs the D3D12 `BuildRaytracingAccelerationStructure` vtable call sites in 0xe83d440 / 0xe83f1d0).
- **A12d upscaling API**: `0xe83ec70` (RTX slot 60 reset handler) handles "Upscaling Mode: DLSS / Fallback / Off" (0xe83eed0 / 0xe83ef58 / 0xe83ef61), `DLSSOptimalSettingsCallback` (0xe83ee17), `DLSS.Get.Dynamic.{Min,Max}.Render.{Width,Height}` (0xe83efe4..0xe83f020), `DLSS.Feature.Create.Flags` (0xe83f154), `DLSS.Enable.Output.Subrects` (0xe83f169). `0xe8403d0` (slot 64, per-frame super-sampling) holds the evaluate parameters: `SuperSampling.Available / NeedsUpdatedDriver / MinDriverVersionMajor / Minor` (0xe840615..0xe840677), `DLSS.Input.{Color,Depth,MV,Translucency,Bias.Current.Color}.Subrect.Base.{X,Y}`, `DLSS.Output.Subrect.Base.{X,Y}`, `DLSS.Render.Subrect.Dimensions.{Width,Height}`, `DLSS.Pre.Exposure`, `DLSS.Exposure.Scale`, `DLSS.Indicator.Invert.{X,Y}.Axis`, `DLSS.Input.Bias.Current.Color.Mask`, `DLSS.Checkerboard.Jitter.Hack` (0xe84a37f..0xe84a7a1). These are NVSDK parameter names (NGX), set in a long run of SetI/SetF calls; the pass-side upscalers are TAAU (0xbc8cfd0 `TAAUpscalingParameters`), FSR1 (0xbcb3e08 / 0xbcb441c) and the `Upscaling` pass (0xbc8a380, 0xbc3dd40).

## 6ei. A12a-d fork additions and B10c TLAS/BLAS (static, 62.01; partial)
**Where the fork additions are visible:** the Caps serializer `0x660ecd0` (6j) names them as capability flags (bit positions in `bgfx/Caps.h`): `Ray_Tracing` (31, ON in the live RTX path), `Upscaling` (32, OFF live), `Buffer_Bind_Offset` (34, ON), `Ranged_Resource` (35, ON), `Dynamic_Texturehandles` (36, ON). Everything below is what the code around them shows; the API-level semantics (new bgfx:: calls) are not individually decoded.
- **A12a dynamic texture handles:** `Context::init 0xbc66de0` contains "Disabling dynamic texture handles is not supported." (so the flag is mandatory); `createDynamic` textures `0xbc74a80` (0x5c5), destroy `0xbc750b0`, update `0xbc752f0` use the u16 ref arrays at ctx+0x1dfb48c..90; the D3D12 texture table is `this+0xBDE178` (stride 0xB8, 4096 inline then chained pages, handle>>12 = page, 6am/6z). A dynamic handle is a normal texture-table slot allocated through the guarded paged array, not a fixed small table.
- **A12b ranged resources / buffer bind offset:** flags only. No string or distinct function was found for them (searched "Ranged", "BufferRange", "bind offset"; only the Caps names). Consequence from the executor/encoder layouts: a bind slot's record is 0x14 bytes (6dp: handle, offset?, flags, ...) and the second dword of the bound slot was 0 in live data, which is where a buffer bind offset/range would sit: unverified.
- **A12c / B10c ray tracing, RTX submit wrapper `0xe83d440` (slot 50 of the RTX class, 0x381 bytes) decoded:** (1) lazy init: when byte `[rtx+0x4165e78] == 1` and `frameNo ([rtx+0x50]) - [rtx+0x4165e7c] >= 0xB` and the D3D12 device/queue pointer `[rtx+0x19f58]` is set, it clears the flag, stores the frame number, **idles the queue** (`0xe80ecb0(rtx+0xcc9268, -1)`) and calls **`0xe84ba30(rtx, Frame+0xe748c8)`** which builds the RT compute pipeline objects (strings "failed to serialize root signature!", "failed to create root signature!", "failed to create compute PSO!"). So RT pipelines are created 11 frames after startup, on the render thread, with a full GPU idle. (2) Per frame it copies 16 bytes `Frame+0xe746e0` and a dword `Frame+0xe74844` into `rtx+0x3c5642c / +0x3c56454` (per-frame camera/scene parameters for the RT passes). (3) Calls the base submit `0xe82e230`. (4) After it, if the serial `[rtx+0x4165e70]` changed: QueryInterface on the command list/queue object `[[rtx+0x19f58]+0x30]` (IID at rip+0xcb006c, an ID3D12 interface with RT support) then `0xe84c560(rtx, ctxPtr, iface)` and `0xe84c7d0(rtx)` (acceleration-structure update/build for the frame), `Release` (vtbl+0x10), queue idle again, then a loop over **all 0x1000 handles calling `0xe84b980(rtx, i)`** (per-handle BLAS refresh/cleanup), resets four state dwords `rtx+0x415e6c8/6bc/690/684`, and clears two big tables at `rtx+0x41564d8..` (an unordered container being rebuilt: node size 0x48). 0xe84b980/0xe84c560/0xe84c7d0 have no strings (the TLAS/BLAS builders proper are below them; not decoded). Thin RTX overrides of slots 10,14,15,16,19,21 (6g table) register each created resource with this BLAS bookkeeping.
- **A12d upscaling:** cap flag 32 is OFF on this machine; RTX slot 64 `0xe8403d0` is the DLSS/"SuperSampling.*" setup; FSR1/TAAU passes and the VV upscaling options are in 6x/6bc/6bh (checklist I6b/I6c). Whether an `upscale()` bgfx call exists is not decoded.

## 6ej. B3c SOLVED (static): the draw emitter is `0xe81b720`, the executor's batched draw flush (1.26.62.01)
How it was found: scanning 0xe800000-0xe850000 for vtable loads of ID3D12GraphicsCommandList slots 12/13/14/59 (`[rax+0x60]` DrawInstanced, `[rax+0x68]` DrawIndexedInstanced, `[rax+0x70]` Dispatch, `[rax+0x1d8]` ExecuteIndirect; many hits are false positives = struct fields). The executor 0xe821120 itself only calls **ExecuteIndirect (0xe829c46, 0xe82c99c, 0xe82ca92) and Dispatch (0xe829c79)** directly (indirect/compute items; the earlier "0 hits" on 0xe829be1/c60/c7d were those compute branches, which a normal scene does not run). All normal draws go through **`0xe81b720(rcx = DrawBatcher* [state+0x250], rdx = [[state+0xb0]+0x238], r8 = JobContext* [state+0x90], r9d = 0 non-indexed / 1 indexed)`**; `[r8+0x30]` = the ID3D12GraphicsCommandList*. Call sites in the executor: 0xe822e4e/0xe822e6b (flush both batches when pipeline/root state changed), 0xe82cfc1/0xe82cfde, 0xe82d0f6 (single kind), 0xe82d12b/0xe82d148 (both). Sig in Sigs.json: `D3D12Context.drawFlush` (unique).
Batcher (rdi): pending count per kind `u32 [rdi + kind*4 + 0x10]` (zeroed when consumed), record array `[rdi + kind*8 + 0x18]` (r15), per-kind **command signature** `[rdi + kind*8]` (used by ExecuteIndirect), flag byte `[rdi+0x1164]` (indirect path enabled), bound-state cache at `[rdi+0x1120]/[rdi+0x1128]` (last two CBV addresses), `rdi+0x10c0` (VB views cache, 0x50 bytes) and `rdi+0x1110` (IB view cache, 0x10 bytes).
Record layouts (all D3D12 structures pre-built, i.e. the executor has already resolved handles to GPU addresses):
- **indexed (kind 1), stride 0x88**: `+0x00..0x4F` 5 x D3D12_VERTEX_BUFFER_VIEW (0x10 each: address, size, stride) -> `IASetVertexBuffers(0, 5, views)` (slot 44, 0x160) only when different from the cache (0xebce440 = 16/80-byte memcmp, 0xebce390 = copy); `+0x50` D3D12_INDEX_BUFFER_VIEW (0x10) -> `IASetIndexBuffer` (slot 43, 0x158); `+0x60`, `+0x68` two GPU addresses -> `SetGraphicsRootConstantBufferView(2, addr)` and `(3, addr)` (slot 38, 0x130; per-draw constants / matrices, cf. 6dq) when changed; `+0x70` IndexCountPerInstance, `+0x74` InstanceCount, `+0x78` StartIndexLocation, `+0x7C` BaseVertexLocation, `+0x80` StartInstanceLocation -> **DrawIndexedInstanced** (slot 13, 0x68, at 0xe81b866 / 0xe81b8ce).
- **non-indexed (kind 0), stride 0x70**: `+0x00..0x4F` 5 VBVs, `+0x50`, `+0x58` the two CBV addresses (root params 2, 3), `+0x60` VertexCountPerInstance, `+0x64` InstanceCount, `+0x68` StartVertexLocation, `+0x6C` StartInstanceLocation -> **DrawInstanced** (slot 12, 0x60, at 0xe81ba74 / 0xe81bac7).
- Path choice: if `n == 1` or the indirect flag `[rdi+0x1164]` is 0 (or kind==0 with the flag clear) the records are emitted one by one with the direct draws above (loop at 0xe81b8b0 / 0xe81bab0, `esi` = remaining count); otherwise (0xe81bc43..0xe81bf08) the records are copied into an upload ring (alloc `0xe8360f0`, copy `0xeec6ec0`, per-kind byte size = `[0xcd19b0 + kind*4] * n`) and issued with **one ExecuteIndirect** (slot 59, 0x1d8) using the per-kind command signature (`[rdi + kind*8]`), MaxCommandCount = n, ArgumentBuffer = the ring copy; the ring buffers get a resource-state transition (slot 0xd0/8 = 26 ResourceBarrier with state 0x400/0x200 = INDIRECT_ARGUMENT/COPY_DEST toggles) tracked in a 32-entry table at rdi+0xf28..0x1040.
Hook use: (a) hook `0xe81b720` to skip a whole batch (set the count at `[rdi+kind*4+0x10]` to 0 and return) or to inspect/modify records before they go out; (b) edit a record in place (stride 0x88/0x70): zero the count/index fields to skip a single draw, swap the VBV/IB views or the CBV addresses to substitute geometry/constants; (c) per-draw identity is NOT in the record (it is built from the frame headers: PSO, textures and root constants are bound earlier in the executor loop), so identify a draw by the batcher state at flush time (`[state+0x250]` + the PSO/root state set just before) or hook earlier at the Encoder::submit seam (6cq). Not run in game.

## 0p. Version Update Strategy (1.26.60 -> 1.26.62 drift)

### What version drift looks like [I]

Between 1.26.60 and 1.26.62: every RVA changed, vtable slots shifted (+8 on group slots, +1/+4 around `startFrame`/`_insert`), some struct field offsets moved (±0x10). The `RENDER_REVERSAL_PLAN.md` rule: "Slot constants are named once, in the SDK header, and a startup check must fail loudly if a slot does not point into .text or a sig is missing." The per-version drift itself is verified in section 6cw (encoder/executor RVAs shifted between the two builds; field offsets identical) [I].

### What breaks easily (treat as disposable, regenerate per patch)

- Raw RVAs (every function address) [I]
- Vtable slot **indices** (they shift between patches — the +8 group-slot shift and the +1/+4 shift around `startFrame`/`_insert` are live-verified for this pair [V by strings, section 3])
- Exact struct field offsets (BatchActor `+0xE0` etc. — re-verify per patch) [I]
- Inline pass-name hash immediates in the giant 0x55f1450 function — rescan per version [I]
- Thin clone RVAs (0x139-byte stamps) — relocate via `srcloc.py` per patch [I]

### What survives reasonably well (worth fully reversing now)

- Render stack architecture and layer boundaries (game → FrameBuilder → dragon → bgfx → D3D12) [V]
- Pass name hash **algorithm** (FNV-1 64, offset basis 0xcbf29ce484222325, prime 0x100000001b3) [V]
- entt component vocabulary and rendering component names (survive via `__FUNCSIG__` strings) [V]
- bgfx core concepts (Frame, View, RenderItem, Caps) — Mojang's fork changes slowly [I]
- D3D12 backend interface (65 vtable slots, `submit` signature) [V]
- Function **behavior** and **semantics** (submit takes `Frame*` in `rdx`, endFrame takes `BgfxFrameBuilder` in `rcx`) — version-stable [V]

### Practical update workflow

1. Keep an RVA/slot database per version (`OffsetProvider.h`, `Sigs.json`) — one file set per build, never merged across versions.
2. Use string-based discovery (`srcloc.py` finds functions by `__FUNCSIG__` substring — survives RVA moves).
3. Startup validation: fail loudly if a slot doesn't point into `.text` or a sig is missing (the plan-doc rule above; sigs live in `Sigs.json`, section 6dj).
4. Separate "what" from "where": document behavior independently of RVAs; behavior is version-stable, address is not.

### Prioritization rule

Focus on version-stable, high-value targets first. Don't over-invest in version-fragile details (exact offsets, inline immediates) — re-derive those per patch. Fully reverse the structural and behavioral parts that survive across versions; that makes re-mapping after a patch take hours instead of weeks.

## 6ek. AUDIT of the "Full RE" material added by another agent (checklist section K, RENDER_REVERSAL_PLAN, the pasted pseudocode) against the disassembly
Rule: reversing is the ground truth; their text is a hypothesis list. Verdicts (V = confirmed by this map's evidence, X = contradicted, ? = no evidence either way yet):
| Claim | Verdict | Evidence / correction |
|---|---|---|

| `+0xDC57E0` pointer to per-view block | ? | not seen; the known tail is `+0xDC57E8` (item count, CAS) `+0xDC57F0..` transient IB/VB (Frame.h); needs a re-check |
| `+0xDC57E8` u32 draw count, `+0xDC5808` backbuffer size | V | 6de live (611 items), 6e |

| Draw record `+0x38` program handle | ? | the program is a field of the **sort key** (`(key>>0x15)&0x7FF`, 6ao), not (yet) a header field; `+0x38` is undecoded |
| `+0x40` start index, `+0x44` count (0xFFFFFFFF = computed), `+0x48` index stride, `+0x5A` IB handle, `+0x76` VB handle | V (+0x40 is [I]) | Frame.h RenderDrawHeader |
| submit signature "rdx = Frame*" | ? [I] | submit prologue reads the consume frame through the context; 6do corrected which pointer is fill/consume, so say "reads the frame via ctx+0x1CFD0C0/0xC8" until the prologue is re-read |
| Context `+0x19F58` resource tables, `+0x19F88` state (<5 skip), `+0x93A178` 0x78-stride records | V/V/[I] | 6z, 6ai, 6aj |
| Layer list L1-L18 and 7-step pipeline diagram | V (as an outline) | matches 6a/6aa/6ab/6n/6ej; names such as `dragon::frameobject` are from LeviLamina, unverified in the exe |
| Version drift: vtable group slots shifted, every RVA changed | V | FrameFilterHook slot comments (0x350 -> 0x370 -> 0x378), 6cw |
| "Survives versions": FNV-1 64 pass hashes, entt names, bgfx concepts, 65 vtable slots | V | 6o/6p/6dt/6f; vtable slot *numbers* did change in the group part of the FrameBuilder table, not in the RendererContextD3D12 table (65 slots both versions) |
**Correction of 6ao ("Descriptor flush" 0xe81b720)**: wrong. `0xe81b720` is the draw-batch flush (6ej). The command-list slots in it decode as (D3D12 vtable index = offset/8): 0x60 = 12 DrawInstanced, 0x68 = 13 DrawIndexedInstanced, 0xd0 = 26 ResourceBarrier, 0x130 = 38 **SetGraphicsRootConstantBufferView** (root params 2 and 3, 64-bit GPU addresses), 0x158 = 43 IASetIndexBuffer, **0x160 = 44 IASetVertexBuffers(0, 5, views)** (6ao's "root parameter 5" is this), 0x1d8 = 59 ExecuteIndirect. The 0x50-byte copy at rdi+0x10C0 is the vertex-buffer view cache (5 x 0x10), not "descriptor state". Root params 2/3 are CBVs. The root signature does have >= 6 parameters (6ak), but this function does not touch descriptor tables.
Method lesson: a vtable-slot guess needs the interface's real index table (offset/8), not just "a call through [rax+0x130]". `tools/sigs/dossier.py` (below) prints the D3D12 interface slot names for every vtable call so this check is mechanical.

### 6el. M1b inventories for L4/L5 and L6 (static, 62.01) [V mechanical, [I] region meaning]
Generated with `tools/sigs/inventory.py`: `docs/data/inventory_L4L5_builder.tsv` (rva range 0x6600000-0x67e0000: 6391 functions, 30 big, 2189 indirect-entry = vtable/std::function only) and `docs/data/inventory_L6_graph.tsv` (0x5500000-0x5700000: 5567 functions, 16 big). Counts exceed the earlier estimates (~2,300 / ~3,600) because the ranges include unrelated code: in L6 the functions below ~0x55f0000 are command/JSON parsing (strings "commands.hasitem.fail.noItem", "format_version", "Missing rule derivation"), NOT render passes; the VV/Fancy pass declarations start at the giant 0x55f1450 (0x4e144 bytes, "Shadow Cascades") and run to ~0x5700000. Treat the first ~0xF0000 bytes of the L6 file as noise (filter by address >= 0x55f0000) [I].
Render-relevant big functions found (D0, candidates for dossiers): L4/L5: 0x66232b0 BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame (0xb3d0), 0x6646980 Item group visitor (0x9425), 0x6698e30 ItemInHand extractor (0x5fb4), 0x66d9af0 batch-actor extractor (0x8bb7), 0x66d0a00 bodyActor pass (0x738b), 0x66ea3d0 terrain/chunk draw builder (0xf430), RenderChunkFogAlpha family 0x670b6e0/0x6722cb0/0x67762e0/0x6792d90/0x67aa700, 0x66560f0 cubemap (0x86be), 0x666aca0 flipbook/UI base (0x6c7c), 0x674a3a0 UI text/glint (0x4c66), 0x67375a0 clouds, 0x673d150 ActorPrepass/FlameBillboard, 0x67417f0 particles, 0x6745440 weather, 0x66a6830 shadow volume, 0x66742f0/0x66a1860 editor block volume. L6: 0x55f1450 VV graph builder, 0x56a06a0 SkyProbeDeferredShading/DoDeferredShading, 0x56e1990 Upscaling.
Note: the L4/L5 file mixes the FrameBuilder layer (extractors/clones) with the frame-renderer pass bodies that consume it (material draw functions); splitting into L4/L5/L6 proper needs the call graph, not an address range [I].

### 6em. Dossier pass: init, handoff, GraphicsTasks (static, 62.01) [V mechanical]
Dossiers in `docs/functions/`: bc63690 (Context init tail + "bgfx - renderer backend thread"), bc65800 (handoff), c267200 (Render lambda, D1), c25fc50 (Execute). Correction/caution on 6as/753: the handoff 0xbc65800 is described as "called from frame()", but the 62.01 call lists show 0xbc65800 CALLS Context::frame 0xbc66de0 and is called from 0xbc63c80, 0xbc660a0 and GraphicsTasks Execute 0xc25fc50 (this agrees with the chain in 6ac). The earlier "frame() calls handoff" wording is unreliable; where the swap sits inside handoff is not pinned [?]. Also 6ek's view-struct matrix offsets (+0x10/+0x50 relative to the Frame struct) vs 6q's initialiser (+0x20/+0x60/+0xa0/+0xe0 at ctx+0x1eab080) disagree by 0x10: one of them has a different base (struct start vs a +0x10 header); needs a live poll of one view struct before trusting either [?].
Addendum 6em (view-struct offsets): 6ek/Frame.h cite "view mtx +0x10, proj +0x50 (6dd live)", 6q's initialiser writes identity matrices at +0x20/+0x60/+0xa0/+0xe0 and 6q's live read matched fields at +0x00/+0x08. A Frame copy is a straight memcpy of the Context array, so both cannot describe the same base. Not resolved: Minecraft.Windows.exe was not running when I tried (CE bridge alive, process lookup NOT_FOUND). Poll `*(base+0x12AE1748)+0x1eab080+viewId*0x140` (64 bytes at +0x10 and +0x20) next time the game is up; until then Frame.h's comment is [?], not [V].
New D1 dossiers this pass: c25fe20 (task runner), c280010 (generate final step), bc660a0 (frame core); D2: bc65720, bc63150.

### 6en. Task runner and the generator's "final step" decoded (static disassembly, 62.01) [V]
- **0xc25fe20** = drain the GraphicsTasks queue (0xae500 try-pop into an MSVC std::function, small-buffer copy or pointer steal into a local vector of 0x40-byte slots, vector growth 0x1c3170), then call every impl's `_Do_call` (vtbl+0x10) in FIFO order and `_Delete_this` (vtbl+0x20). Called by Execute 0xc25fc50 x2, BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame 0x66232b0 and 0xc260050. See docs/functions/c25fe20.md.
- **0xc280010 is not a task-list builder**: it is `_Insertion_sort_unchecked` over 0x80-byte records keyed by the signed u64 at +0x78; **0xc280ca0** is the stable_sort chunk driver (runs of 0x20 elements). 6cz called 0xc280010 "final step producing the task list"; correct to: the generator's last visible step is a stable sort of its per-pass records by an order key, after which the records become the task list [I for the second half]. The key's producer is still unknown. See docs/functions/c280010.md.

### 6eo. Frame pass order is driven by endFrame's `vector<HashedString>&&` (static, 62.01) [V mechanical, [I] naming]
Correction/extension of 6cz and 6en. In 0xc273420 (offsets 0xc27ab07..0xc27ac5f) each pass record's task object (`rec+0x00`) is matched against the names vector passed in r9 (element 0x30 bytes: hash +0x00, std::string +0x08, owner back-pointer +0x28) by name hash at `obj+0x08` plus string compare; the matched index (`byte distance / 0x30`) is stored as the sort key at `rec+0x78`, and the records are stable-sorted by it (0xc280010 insertion sort for <= 0x20 elements, 0xc280ca0 chunk+merge otherwise). Unmatched records keep key 0 (initialised at 0xc2792ff). Consequences: (1) the pass order the dispatcher sees = declaration order refined by this list; (2) the producer of the list is the caller (Frame renderer 0x67c9c20), so changing the list = reordering passes without touching task entries; (3) names are HashedStrings, so they can be built with PassNames.h. Untested in game. Producer decode: open (K-P3/F5).

### 6ep. Frame renderer 0x67c9c20: the names vector it passes to endFrame is EMPTY (static, 62.01) [V mechanical]
Correction to 6eo's "producer not decoded". The call to `BgfxContext::endFrame` at 0x67ca3cd is set up at 0x67ca399..0x67ca3cd as: `rcx = [[rsi+0x20]]` (BgfxContext), `rdx = [rsi+0x10] + 0x268` (DragonFrameResources&), `r8 = rbp+0x38` (Declarator&, filled earlier from the closure: copies of [rsi+0x48..0x58], shared_ptr pair, flag byte at rbp+0x118), `r9 = [rsi+8]` (IntraFrameAllocator&), stack arg `[rsp+0x20] = rbx = &rbp+0xb0`, and immediately before the call `rbp+0xb0..0xc0` is zeroed (`movdqa [rbp+0xb0], xmm0 ; mov [rbp+0xc0], 0`) = **an empty `vector<HashedString>`**. endFrame has only this caller (dossier), and 0xc273420 skips its ordering stage when `names.begin == names.end` (0xc27ab19..0xc27ab27 -> jmp 0xc27ae44). So in 1.26.62.01 the 6eo ordering stage is inert: **pass order = pure declaration order**; the names-vector mechanism exists but nothing feeds it. To make use of it one would have to hook endFrame (0xc270460) and substitute a non-empty vector (stack arg 5 at [rsp+0x28] on entry), or patch the zeroing here [I, untested; needs a std::vector<HashedString> with 0x30-byte elements {hash, std::string, owner ptr}]. Frame renderer prologue facts: lambda whose closure `rcx+0x20 -> [..]+0x18` gives the Context base used for offsets +0x4c8/+0x4f8/+0x710/+0x958 (7 resource sets via 0xa784c0), trace scopes "Frame renderer - Frame" and "BgfxContext...", and the callee order 0xe8feb10 (PipelineDiskCache::tick), 0xc267b90, 0xc270330, 0xc270460, 0x4305e0 (destroys the temp vector), 0x67ca960, 0x67cab00.

### 6eq. 0xbc660a0 = Context::shutdown sequence; 0xbc65800 = Context::frame(capture) (static, 62.01) [V mechanical, [I] naming]
Reading 0xbc660a0 end to end: it writes command bytes into two Frame command buffers (`Frame+0xDC5818` pos / `+0xDC5820` data = pre-commands, byte 1 first; `Frame+0xE05820` pos / `+0xE05828` data = post-commands, bytes 0x1f/0x1a) and calls 0xbc65800 five times with edx = 0, three times in a row at the end, then stamps the present record. That is the shape of bgfx's shutdown (RendererShutdownBegin, destroy everything, flush frames). Therefore **0xbc65800 is the API-side `Context::frame(bool capture=false)`** (it is also what `GraphicsTasks - Execute` calls once per frame, 6ac), and 6as/6em's "handoff" is that frame(). This resolves the 6em direction puzzle: 0xbc660a0 (shutdown) -> frame 0xbc65800 -> swap 0xbc66de0 -> (single-threaded) renderFrame 0xbc63150. New Frame facts [I]: pre/post command buffers (stock bgfx CommandBuffer m_cmdPre/m_cmdPost) at +0xDC5818/+0xDC5820 and +0xE05820/+0xE05828 (0x40000 apart); command enum numbering unverified. These are the buffers through which the API thread creates/destroys GPU resources for the render thread: the place to observe every texture/buffer/program creation (checklist A12/B10 follow-up).

### 6er. Render-thread command-buffer interpreter 0xbc68e40 and the command byte table (static, 62.01) [V mechanical, [?] enum names]
`0xbc68e40(ctx rcx, buf rdx)` (0x1b83 bytes, only caller `renderFrame` 0xbc63150; string "MemoryReader: read truncated.") reads commands from a command buffer object (`rsi`: `[rsi]` = read position u32, data from `rsi+8`; fields read with memcpy 0xebce390, 2-byte aligned: `pos = (pos + 5) & ~1` pattern) and dispatches the byte through a **40-entry jump table at rva 0xf449638 (`movsxd rax,[rbp+rax*4]; add rax,rbp; jmp rax`, index = byte - 1; bytes outside 1..40 loop)**. Each handler decodes its arguments and calls one `RendererContext` virtual (`[ctx+0x1ebf2d0]` = renderer object, slot = offset/8). Table dumped to `docs/data/bgfx_cmdbuf_1.26.62.01.tsv` (byte -> handler rva -> vtbl slots). Findings:
- Slot numbers match the RendererContextI order of the 6k table (createVertexLayout 12, createIndexBuffer 10, createVertexBuffer 14, createDynamic IB/VB 16/19, update dynamic 17/20, createShader 22, createProgram 24, destroyFrameBuffer 36, createUniform 37, requestScreenShot 39, ...). So **a hook on the renderer's slot N sees every API-thread resource command of that kind** (slots 10-45 are the resource create/destroy/update API), already validated static against the table.
- byte 1 (written first by shutdown, 6eq) = handler at 0xbc68f62: `mov byte [ctx+0x1ebf2ec], 0` (clears a renderer-active flag): RendererShutdownBegin-like [I]. Post-command bytes 0x1f (31) -> slot 21 handler 0xbc6a113 (a destroy*), 0x1a (26) -> slot 47 + the callee 0xbc68840 (0xbc6a838/0xbc6a87b) [V which slots, [?] semantic names]. byte 9 and byte 10 have no virtual call (no-op / local handling), the enum numbering is NOT stock bgfx (stock CreateShader=9 would call slot 22; here that is byte 12) [V mismatch].
- 0xbc68840 (0x5f7 bytes; callers renderFrame and 0xbc68e40; constant 0x78666762 "bgfx" TLS magic) is the render-thread init/begin-frame part [I].
Use: the command stream is how Minecraft's renderer creates every texture/buffer/program on the render thread; decoding the argument layouts of bytes 11/12/14/19 (createTexture / createShader / ...) gives full resource-creation observability (A12/B10/G-series follow-up) [I].

### 6es. Command argument layouts (static, 62.01) [V reads, [I] names]
Extracted with a script over each handler of 6er (`docs/data/bgfx_cmdbuf_args_1.26.62.01.txt`: per byte the sequence of `size@scratch (alignment)` memcpy reads from the command stream, then the renderer virtual slots called). Stream reads are aligned (u16: 2, u32: 4, u64/pointers: 8) relative to the buffer start, command byte first. Fully readable ones:
- **byte 2** = [u16 handle][0x54-byte VertexLayout record (4-aligned)] -> slot 12 `createVertexLayout(handle, const VertexLayoutRecord*)`; 0x54 matches the layout table stride (`layoutTable + handle*0x54`, 6ct) [V].
- **byte 12** = [u16 handle][u64 Memory* (8-aligned)] -> slot 22 `createShader(handle, Memory*)`; afterwards the handler releases the Memory object (frees its out-of-line storage when `[mem] != mem+0x10`, then calls a release through a global object) [V].
- **byte 13** = [u16 handle][u16 vsh][u16 fsh] -> slot 24 `createProgram(handle, vsh, fsh)` [V].
- **destroy commands** bytes 27..34 = [u16 handle] -> slots 13, 11, 15, 18, 21, 23, 25, 34 (destroy VertexLayout, IndexBuffer, VertexBuffer, DynamicIB, DynamicVB, Shader, Program, Texture, in the order of the stock enum shifted by +6) [V slots, [I] names]; bytes 35..38 and 40 are further one/two-u16 commands (slots 38, 41, 43, 57, 44).
- **byte 3/4** (index / vertex buffer create): [u16 handle][u64 Memory*][u16 flags]... then slot 10 / 14; **byte 5/7** dynamic buffer creates: [u16][u32 size][u16 flags] -> slots 16/19; **byte 6/8** dynamic updates: [u16][u32 offset][u32 size][u64 Memory*] -> slots 17/20 [V reads, [I] field names].
- **byte 14** = createTexture, see 6et (the slot-56 reading here is wrong);  **byte 10** reads a 64-byte block plus ~12 small fields without any virtual call: the texture-create-style commands (field order known, meaning [?]). **bytes 25/26** read a 64-byte block + {u8,u8,u32} and call slots 47/46 (+0xbc68840): readTexture/screenshot-style [?].
Enum numbering: bytes 1..8 equal stock bgfx (ShutdownBegin, CreateVertexLayout, CreateIB, CreateVB, CreateDynIB, UpdateDynIB, CreateDynVB, UpdateDynVB); bytes 9..11 are extra/unknown commands; CreateShader/CreateProgram sit at 12/13 (stock 9/10: +3); the destroy block is stock +6 (21..30 -> 27..36). This is an inference from the matching slot order, not a decoded enum [I]. Note the slot names for slots >= 35 in the 6k table may be shifted by a couple of positions (byte 35's single-handle destroy hits slot 38, not 36), so treat 6k's names beyond slot 34 as [?].

### 6et. Command byte 14 = createTexture (static, 62.01) [V mechanical, [I] field names]; correction to 6es
Handler 0xbc69487 reads `[u16 handle][u64 Memory* mem (8-aligned)][u32 flags (4-aligned)][u8 skip]`, then reads the first 4 bytes of the Memory block through a MemoryReader (strings "MemoryReader: read truncated.") and requires the magic **0x00584554 = bgfx `BGFX_CHUNK_MAGIC_TEX` ('T','E','X',0)**, parses the 0x18-byte TextureCreate header, and calls **renderer vtable slot 26 (`[rax+0xd0]`) = createTexture(handle, mem, flags, skip, header*)**, which returns the created texture object pointer [V: slot 26 is createTexture in the 6k table, this is the stock `createTexture(_handle, _mem, _flags, _skip)`]. 6es's "byte 14 -> slot 56" was a script artefact (the 0x1c0 hit is the MemoryReader's own virtual, at 0xbc695fa) and is corrected here; byte 11 ([u16][u32][u64]) is the one that calls slot 56 (0x1c0).
After the call the returned pointer is stored in the **Context's per-handle texture table**: `ctx+0x1e55ab8` = paged array, **element 0x28 bytes, 0x1000 elements per 0x28000-byte page** (`imul 5, shl 3`), pages chained by a pointer at `page+0x28000`, texture object pointer at **element+0x10**; handles >= 0x1000 are "dynamic texture handles" and only allowed when the flag byte `ctx+0x1e7dad0` is set (else it fails through 0xbc5f110 with a guarded-array message), which ties to the dynamic-texture-handle caps bit found earlier (6ek). [V code, [I] meaning]. Observation points: renderer slot 26 sees every texture creation with its encoded TEX chunk; `ctx+0x1e55ab8` lets a poll enumerate textures by handle (element+0x10 = backend object).

### 6eu. Command byte 18 (0xbc69031) and byte 11 (0xbc6960d) (static, 62.01) [V reads, [?] semantics]
- **byte 18** = `[u16 handle][u32 a][u64 b][u64 c]` -> renderer slot 35 (`[rax+0x118]`) called as `slot35(handle, a, b, c)`; afterwards the handler stores the u64 `b` itself at **element+0x10 of the same per-handle table as createTexture** (`ctx+0x1e55ab8`, 0x28-byte elements, same dynamic-handle >= 0x1000 rule). So `b` is an object pointer supplied by the command producer, not created by the renderer: this is the "register an externally created/wrapped object under a handle" command [I]. In the 6k table slot 35 is named createFrameBuffer; if that name is right, byte 18 = createFrameBuffer(handle, count/flags, attachment-array ptr, ...) and the table at ctx+0x1e55ab8 is shared between texture and framebuffer records [?: slot names >= 35 are unreliable, see 6es].
- **byte 11** = `[u16 handle][u32 a][u64 ptr]` -> slot 56 (`[rax+0x1c0]`) called as `slot56(handle, a, ptr)`, no table store [V]; slot 56 is a Mojang extra (not in stock RendererContextI).
- Both are in `docs/data/bgfx_cmdbuf_args_1.26.62.01.txt` form. Remaining unmapped command bytes: 10, 15, 16, 17, 19..26, 35..40 (arguments known, semantics [?]).

### 6ev. BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame 0x66232b0 stage map (static, 62.01) [V order]
Full call/string order recorded in docs/functions/66232b0.md ("Stage map"). Highlights: two flushes of the deferred-transaction object (0x6632bf0) bracket the body; the queued GraphicsTasks are run at 0x662358a (0xc25fe20) and again via 0xc260050 at 0x662d8d9; "Wait for previous frame" (0xc25f960, which reaches Execute 0xc25fc50 -> bgfx frame() 0xbc65800) is called at 0x6623881 and 0x662da86; the 9 deferred transaction kinds (Acceleration Structure, Dragon Image/Material/Texture/ShaderBuffer/IndexBuffer/VertexBuffer, Texture, Buffer) are walked between 0x6624389 and 0x6627d28; "Persistent UI Color/Depth" targets are created at 0x6624000/0x6624107; the scope "FrameBuilder - End frame wait for render" closes the function (0x662e4e1). So the Execute -> frame() handoff of 6ac happens INSIDE BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame (through Wait for previous frame), not after it; 6ac's linear chain `endFrame -> Frame renderer 0x67c9c20 -> BgfxContext::endFrame ...` is a data-flow order, the call nesting is different [I].

### 6ew. Deferred flush correction and Wait-for-previous-frame (static, 62.01) [V]
0x6632bf0 runs the BUFFER vector first and the TEXTURE vector second, each through 0x12893d0 then job->vtbl[1] (run) and vtbl[0](1) (delete) (corrects 6dv's "one merged vector"). 0xc25f960 holds two pending-frame shared_ptr slots (+0x88/+0x90 and +0x98/+0xa0) and a mode word (+0x08): it joins them (0xc25f700), runs Execute 0xc25fc50 only when mode == 1 and slot A was set, then clears both. Dossiers: docs/functions/6632bf0.md, c25f960.md.

### 6ex. renderFrame call order pinned (static, 62.01) [V]
In 0xbc63150 the calls after the semaphore wait are: `0xbc68840` (0xbc63340, prep), command interpreter `0xbc68e40` (0xbc6337b = cmdPre), **renderer vtable `[rax+0x190]` = slot 50 submit (0xbc633a8)**, `0xbc68e40` again (0xbc633c4 = cmdPost), then the "frame consumed" semaphore post (0xebdbc50 at 0xbc633da). This is the stock bgfx renderFrame shape and the first direct evidence of how the render thread reaches submit slot 50. Whole-frame table: docs/FRAME_CALLGRAPH.md.

### 6ey. Port check of the render sigs across versions (tools/sigs/audit.py, static) [V]
`docs/data/port_check_render_sigs.tsv`: each render sig of Sigs.json run against 1.26.50.04 / 1.26.51.01 / 1.26.60.05 / 1.26.62.01 (rva/matches): bgfx core (Encoder::submit 0xb721460 -> 0xb7225d0 -> 0xbc5fb40 -> 0xbc60740, reset, frameSort) and the draw flush match uniquely in ALL four versions, so those sigs are version-stable; the other sigs (drawExecutor, psoBuilder, endFrame, generate, taskDispatch, viewSetupTask) are DEAD on 50.04/51.01 (code differs) but live on 60.05 and 62.01. Between 60.05 and 62.01 almost every one moved by exactly +0xC00 (executor 0xe820520 -> 0xe821120, psoBuilder 0xe81dc60 -> 0xe81e860, endFrame 0xc26f860 -> 0xc270460, generate 0xc272820 -> 0xc273420, taskDispatch 0xe8ff6e0 -> 0xe9002e0, Encoder.* +0xC00, flush 0xe81ab20 -> 0xe81b720 (+0xC00 too)); viewSetupTask moved +0x7C0 (0x568c5d0 -> 0x568cd90).
**Correction to the 6cw addendum:** it claimed the executor entry 0xe821120, wrapper 0xe83c390 and slot 50 0xe82e230 are "NOT shifted" between 60.05 and 62.01. The audit shows the executor sig matching at 0xe820520 in 60.05, i.e. it IS shifted by +0xC00 like its neighbours; the "same bytes at 0xe821120 in 60.05" observation came from a prologue-byte coincidence. (Slot 50 / wrapper: not re-checked here; treat as shifted by the same amount until verified.) Also: the sig for taskDispatch was raised from "low" to "high" priority so the TaskDispatchHook (a HOOK needing a high-priority entry) passes tools/sigs/sigs.py; it matches exactly once on 62.01, 60.05, but it is a startup-scanned sig now [V].

### 6ez. Hash families re-checked: 0x66a02d0 is FNV-1a 64 of a string (static, 62.01) [V]
The helper called 72 times by the ItemInHand extractor (and in many extractors/handlers) computes `h = 0xcbf29ce484222325; for each byte: h ^= b; h *= 0x100000001b3` over a `std::string` {char*, len}: **FNV-1a 64**, whereas the pass-name hash (6p) was verified as FNV-1 64 (multiply, then xor). Both are in use: names of passes use FNV-1 (compile-time folded immediates), runtime string keys (material/uniform/texture names) use FNV-1a. Use `docs/data/render_hashes.txt` for FNV-1 pass names and hash uniform names with FNV-1a when matching task kinds (6ad). ItemInHand extractor dossier: docs/functions/6698e30.md (D1+).

### 6fa. Command bytes 15, 17, 19 (static, 62.01) [V reads and call shapes, [I] names]
- **byte 17** (0xbc697bb) = `[u16 handle][u16 a][u16 b][u8 c][u16 d]` -> renderer slot 31 (`[rax+0xf8]`) called as `slot31(handle, a, b, c, d)` with c and d on the stack: exactly the shape of stock bgfx **`resizeTexture(handle, width, height, numMips, numLayers)`**, and slot 31 is resizeTexture in the 6k table. So byte 17 = ResizeTexture [V shape, name by match]. (Byte 3's second virtual, slot 31, is the same call: a texture resize inside the index-buffer-create handler's sibling branch is unlikely, so byte 3's listing mixes two handlers that share code; treat the byte 3 row of the args file as unreliable [?].)
- **byte 19** (0xbc69a6d) = `[u16 handle][u32 a][u64 Memory*]` -> slot 37 (`[rax+0x128]`) as `slot37(handle, a, mem)`, then jumps to the shared tail 0xbc69f6f which releases the Memory object (same tail as createShader, byte 12). A create/update that takes a data blob [V]; name [?] (6k calls slot 37 createUniform, which does not take a blob, so 6k's names from slot 35 on are off).
- **byte 15** (0xbc69c6b) = `[u16 handle][u32 a][u32 b][u16 c]` -> slot 36 (`[rax+0x120]`) as `slot36(handle, b', a', c)` (c on the stack) [V]; name [?].
Net: confirmed names so far by argument shape: 2 createVertexLayout, 12 createShader, 13 createProgram, 14 createTexture (slot 26), 17 resizeTexture (slot 31), 27-34 destroys. Slots 35..45 need their D3D12 implementations read to be named (the 6k names there are positional guesses).

### 6fb. RendererContextD3D12 base vtable slots 10-34 named with evidence (static, 62.01) [V]
Dump: `docs/data/d3d12_vtable_1.26.62.01.txt` (vtable rva 0xF4ED2D0, slots 0-64 with size and strings). Three independent checks now agree on the stock RendererContextI order for slots 10..34:
1. **No-op and alias pattern** identical to stock bgfx's D3D12 backend: slot 13 (destroyVertexLayout), 27 (updateTextureBegin) and 29 (updateTextureEnd) point at the shared empty stub 0x768f0; slot 11 and 18 are the same function 0xe812b90 (destroyIndexBuffer == destroyDynamicIndexBuffer); slot 15 and 21 are the same function 0xe812e80 (destroyVertexBuffer == destroyDynamicVertexBuffer).
2. **Strings**: slot 22 "Unknown shader format %x." (createShader).
3. **Command argument shapes** (6es/6et/6fa): createVertexLayout(handle, 0x54 record) -> 12; createShader(handle, Memory*) -> 22; createProgram(handle, vsh, fsh) -> 24; createTexture(handle, mem, flags, skip) -> 26; resizeTexture(handle, w, h, mips, layers) -> 31; one-handle destroys -> 13, 11, 15, 18, 21, 23, 25, 34.
