### 6fc. D3D12 resource tables re-derived from the destroy/create virtuals (static, 62.01) [V]; corrects earlier table labels
Reading which table each named virtual indexes (`this + handle*stride + base`):
| table base (this+) | stride | records | evidence | resource |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0x93A178 | 0x78 | 4096 | slot 11/18 destroy(Dynamic)IndexBuffer `imul 0x78; add 0x93a178` | **index buffers** |
| 0x9B2178 | 0x80 | 4096 | slot 15/21 destroy(Dynamic)VertexBuffer | **vertex buffers** |
| 0xA32178 | 0x78 | 4096 | slots 36, 37 (create) and 38 (`imul 0x78; add 0xa32178; jmp release`) | **frame buffers** (slot 38 = destroyFrameBuffer, bytes 15/19 -> the two creates) |
| 0xAAA178 | 0xB8 | 4096 | slot 23 destroyShader | **shaders** (new) |
| 0xB62178 | 0xF8 | 512 | slot 25 destroyProgram | programs |
| 0xBDE178 | 0xB8 | 4096 | slot 34 destroyTexture, 26 createTexture, slot 35 | textures |
| 0xC97698 | 0x68 | ? | slots 39, 41 | uniforms / per-window records |
The sizes divide exactly (each base = previous base + records*stride), which independently confirms strides and counts. Corrections: (1) the header and the summary had **index/vertex swapped** (0x93A178 was called vertex, 0x9B2178 index) and the **framebuffer stride as 0x130**; fixed in `D3D12Context.h`. (2) Slot 35 (0xe8146f0) works on the TEXTURE table, so it is a Mojang texture virtual (command byte 18: handle, u32, object pointer, u64; stores the pointer in the Context texture record), not createFrameBuffer; the framebuffer creates are slots 36 and 37 and the destroy is 38. (3) 6cs/6de described `[rbx+0x588] = ctx+0x9B2178` as the "index buffer record table" with `h+0x0C` as the index-buffer handle: by this table it is the VERTEX buffer table, so the 6cs reading of header +0x0C/+0x1C/+0x1E (indexed vs non-indexed decision) must be re-read before use; 6e-2's version (+0x5A index-buffer handle into the 0x93A178 table, +0x76 vertex-buffer handle with `<<7`) is the consistent one [?: 6cs field names].
Names now [V] for 35..38 by table: 35 texture extra (Mojang), 36/37 createFrameBuffer variants, 38 destroyFrameBuffer; 39 and 41 touch the 0xC97698 table (createUniform / destroyUniform by the 0x68 stride [I]).

### 6fd. Full reversals, frame-path census and structure assertions (static, 62.01) [V]
**Fully reversed (every instruction mapped to a source line):** `Frame::sort` (docs/functions/bc61e20.md) and `EncoderImpl::submit` (docs/functions/bc60740.md). Both are stock bgfx bodies with Mojang additions. Corrections they force:
- submit's signature is `(this, u16 view, u16 program, u16 occlusionQuery /*r9w*/, u32 depth /*5th*/, u8 preserveState /*6th*/)`; 6cq had r9 as the depth.
- `Frame+0xDC57E0` is the **UniformBuffer pointer table** (`UniformBuffer {u32 size; u32 pos; u8 data[]}`), indexed by the encoder's `m_uniformIdx` (encoder +0x6AC); earlier notes called it "per-view tail block" and "view slot". The "present record u32 1" written by frame()/endFrame is the uniform buffer's End marker.
- The 0xC03FFFFFFFFFFFFF mask in Frame::sort is the view REMAP (view id -> view order through the inverse of `m_viewRemap`), not boundary patching; `Frame+0xDC57EC` = u16 m_numBlitItems, `Frame+0x934778` = u32 blit keys (view in the top byte); Context `+0x1CFD0D0` / `+0x1D7D0C8` = radix sort scratch keys/values.
- RenderBind = 65 bindings x 0x14 bytes (idx u16 at +0x0A, type u8 at +0x13) + u32 hash; sort-key tags: bit 52 draw, bits 50-51 type.
**Census (tools/sigs/closure.py -> docs/data/frame_path_functions.tsv):** the direct-call closure of 13 indirect entry points inside the render regions is 838 functions / 0x1CCC6F bytes; 22 have a D2 dossier (2.6% by count, 8.3% by bytes), 20 are D1, 796 are D0. Pass lambdas, task bodies and job classes reached only through vtables/std::function are not in the closure until they are added as roots, so the true number is higher.
**Structure assertions:** `docs/data/layout_asserts.json` + `tools/sigs/layoutcheck.py` (offline, any exe) and `src/sdk/mc/deps/bgfx/RenderLayoutAsserts.h` + `SelfTest` world phase (in the client). 55 assertions pass on 62.01 and 60.05; on 51.01 the checker reports 16 failures with the moved values (e.g. item count 0xDC57E8 -> 0xDD17E8, RenderDraw array 0x8B4700 -> 0x8C0700), which is the update-day output wanted. Method for every further function: reverse fully -> list its layout constants -> add them to both lists.

### 6fe. Fully reversed: encoder discard, bind-array grow, per-draw side table (static, 62.01) [V]
Three more instruction-complete reversals, all in the submit path:
- **0xbc60d10 = `EncoderImpl::discard()`** (docs/functions/bc60d10.md): clears `m_discard`, resets the 0x80-byte draw, a second 0x24-byte block at encoder +0xC0 (not touched by submit's own clear), the 65 bindings and the side table, then sets `m_bindDirty`. Called by pass task bodies directly as well as by the submit forms.
- **0xbc7a7c0 = grow of the Frame's RenderBind storage** (docs/functions/bc7a7c0.md): `Frame+0x8B4700` is `{base, u32 maxCount, reservedBytes, zeroedCount, committedBytes, mutex}`; records are committed in 64 KiB steps and zeroed on demand. **Readers of live memory must bound themselves by the item count (Frame+0xDC57E8): records past `zeroedCount` are not committed memory.** This is a concrete reason earlier wide polls of that region were unsafe.
- **0xbc60f10 = per-draw side table copy** (docs/functions/bc60f10.md): `Frame+0x8B4778 + idx*8` is a `SideTable*` per render item: 0x41 entries of `{Binding* data, u64 count}` plus a u32 `used` at +0x410, each data array `count * 0x14` bytes. This is the storage for more than one binding per stage ("ranged binds", checklist A12b: the storage side is now known, the setter that fills the encoder-side table at encoder +0x680 is still open). The allocator is the bgfx `bx::AllocatorI` global, slot 1 = realloc(ptr, size, align, file, line).
- **0xbc76610 = `bx::HashMurmur2A::add`**; submit's inline tail is `end()`, so `m_bindHash = bx::hash<bx::HashMurmur2A>(&m_bind, 0x514)` with seed 0.
Assertions: 68 offline (55 of them also in the client list); all pass on 62.01.

### 6ff. The three sibling submit forms (static, 62.01) [V]; corrects 6cr's labels
docs/functions/submit_variants.md, reversed by diff against the fully reversed submit:
- **0xbc61140 = `EncoderImpl::dispatch(view, program, numX, numY, numZ)`**: the compute dispatch. It fills the 0x24-byte RenderCompute at encoder +0xC0 and copies it into the same 0x80-byte Frame slot a draw uses (the slot is a union). (6cr called this "occlusion-query / indirect-count form": wrong.)
- **0xbc61560 = a Mojang custom item**: two 64-bit payload words (r8, r9) stored at encoder +0x100, 16 bytes copied to the Frame slot, key bit 53 set. (6cr called this "dispatch-style": wrong.) What the two words are is open.
- **0xbc618e0 = a compute item with subtype 1 that also writes the frame-global parameter blocks**: 0x1E8 bytes to `Frame+0xE746DC` and 0x38 bytes plus a shared_ptr to `Frame+0xE748C8`. That region is the "0x230 bytes of float parameters" seen in the live Frame dump (6e), and the subtype byte is what the executor tests as "item kind 1". (6cr called this "indirect/blit-like": wrong.)
All three always use the per-view sequence counter and never preserve state. **Sort key classes:** bit 52 = draw (types program/depth/sequence in bits 50..51), neither bit = compute, bit 53 = custom; so inside a view the order is compute items, draws, custom items. Sigs added: Encoder::dispatch, submitCustom, submitFrameParams. Assertions: 83 offline, all pass on 62.01.

### 6fg. Executor item classification by sort-key bits; custom item = render-thread callback (static, 62.01) [V]; corrects 6cs/6de/6df
Executor 0xe821120, item loop head 0xe8221f5..0xe82225f: `key = Frame.m_sortKeys[pos]` (the key array itself, +0x14700), `cls = key & 0x0030000000000000`:
- **cls == 0 (neither bit) -> compute item, path 0xe8222c0.** This is the path 6cs/6de/6df described as "the draw path with branch A / branch B on header byte +0x23". It is the COMPUTE path: stat `lock inc [stats+0x4c]`, RenderBind and side table loaded, command list `SetComputeRootSignature` (vtable +0xE8 = index 29) and `SetDescriptorHeaps` (+0xE0 = index 28) on a bind change, then **slot byte +0x23 = RenderCompute.subtype** (encoder +0xE3): 0 -> 0xe822480 (program = `(key >> 21) & 0x7FF`, shader-variant resolve, Dispatch), non-zero -> 0xe829b50 (the frame-params / ray-tracing compute, subtype 1 from 0xbc618e0). So "branch A/B" = plain compute vs frame-params compute, not two kinds of draws, and the "306 / 302 live items" counted in 6de were compute items of the two subtypes [reinterpretation; counts not re-measured].
- **bit 52 set -> draw, path 0xe82226d**, which first switches on the sort type in bits 50..51: 2 (sequence) -> 0xe822650, 1 (depth) -> program = `u16(key) >> 5` -> 0xe822661, 0 (program) -> 0xe822656; the draw body is the code around 0xe822673 (loads `m_sortValues`, side table, header with state at +0x20, scissor at +0x60 ...), i.e. what Frame.h's RenderDrawHeader comments already cite.
- **bit 53 only -> custom item, path 0xe822400**: stat `lock inc [stats + 0x4c + cls*4]`, slot = `Frame+0xB4700 + idx*0x80`; `fn = slot[0]`, `user = slot[1]`; if `fn != 0` it builds a 0x18-byte context on the job state `{const ptr (rip-relative, rva near 0xe822441+0xccb9b8), job field [rbx+0xb0], commandList = [[rbx+0x90]+0x30]}` and calls **`fn(user, &ctx)`** through the CFG dispatch thunk. So the Mojang custom item (0xbc61560, `submitCustom(view, a, b)`) is a **render-thread callback: `a` = function pointer, `b` = user data, invoked in sort order inside its view with the live D3D12 command list.** This is the engine's own seam for injecting arbitrary D3D12 work at a chosen point of the frame: for checklist M3f (whole-frame replacement / our own renderer) and for overlays it is the native route, far safer than patching the executor. Untested in game; the context struct's first field and the exact callback signature need the game's own callers of 0xbc61560 read (callers: see dossier) [?].
Per-class executed-item counters live at stats +0x4C (compute), +0x50 (draw), +0x54 (custom) [V for +0x4c and the cls*4 form, [I] for +0x50 being draws].

### 6fh. Who submits custom items: frame-graph pass tasks queue a render-thread callback (static, 62.01) [V]
0xbc61560 has no direct `call` anywhere; it is reached by six tail jumps (`jmp 0xbc61560` at 0x568ba70, 0x5699c70, 0x56c8650, 0x56ca2f0, 0x56d0d60, 0xbc04300). Four were read and are the same shape, each a virtual method of a pass-task class (vtable entries at rdata 0xf25c798, 0xf25c838, 0xf25c948, 0xf43ea78):
```cpp
void PassTask::encode() {            // 0x568b9e0 / 0x5699be0 / 0x56c85c0 / 0xbc04270
    ... release of a temporary shared_ptr ...
    ViewRef* v = this->viewRef;      // this+0x2E8 (0x568b9e0), +0xB0 (0x5699be0), +0x108 (0x56c85c0), +0x3B0 (0xbc04270)
    v->encoder->submitCustom(v->viewId /*u16 at +8*/, &PassTask::renderThreadBody /*static fn, rip-relative*/, this);   // encoder at v+0x10
}
```
Callback targets: 0x568b9e0 -> 0x568bcd0, 0x5699be0 -> 0x5699e30, 0x56c85c0 -> 0x56c8810, 0xbc04270 -> 0xbc04530 (lea target = next instruction + displacement). So **each frame-graph pass does not draw on the prep thread: it queues ONE custom item into its view carrying `{static function, task object}`, and the D3D12 executor calls that function on the render thread, in sort order, with the command list (6fg).** The pass's real GPU work therefore lives in those static functions (0x568bcd0 for the common pass class of the 14 "Scene"-type tasks), which are new roots for the frame-path census. This also explains the layering: `Encoder::submit` draws issued by extractors go to the Frame as draw items; pass-level work (clears, view setup, blits) rides as custom items.
Use: to run our own code at an exact point of the frame the native way, queue a custom item on the wanted view (`submitCustom(encoder, viewId, ourFn, ourData)`), order inside the view = sequence counter. Untested in game; the callback's second argument layout (0x18-byte context) still needs 0x568bcd0 read.

### 6fi. Executor draw path head and the pass-class template (static, 62.01) [V]
- Draw path head transcribed in docs/functions/e821120.md ("Item loop head and draw path head"): program extraction per sort type, `++stats[+0x50]`, occlusion-query visibility test (`Frame+0x14300` = int32 m_occlusion[256] by query handle; draw +0x7A = query, draw +0x62 sign bit inverts the test; state bit 62 = the draw IS a query), scissor intersection against the view scissor using `Frame+0xD457D0` rects, then current/previous state tracking. Frame.h corrected: 0x14300 is occlusion results, not "per-scissor flags". The remainder of the draw path (from 0xe822830) is still untranscribed.
- All pass-task render-thread bodies are one template (docs/functions/568bcd0.md): four bodies differ only in three displacements; every task class ends `{name std::string, arg0, ViewRef*, control block}`. Tasks.h has TaskTail with static_asserts tying it to the name offsets.

### 6fj. RenderDraw as a struct; draw path second part (static, 62.01) [V]
`bgfx::RenderDraw` (0x80), `RenderStream` (8) and `RenderCompute` (0x24) are now typed structs in `src/sdk/mc/deps/bgfx/Frame.h` with a static_assert per field; they are compiled by `SelfTest.cpp` (which now includes Encoder.h, Frame.h, D3D12Context.h and Tasks.h so their compile-time checks run in every build). Layout: 4 streams of `{u32 startVertex, u16 vertexBuffer, u16 layout}` at +0x00, state +0x20, stencil +0x28, uniformBegin/End +0x34/+0x38, startMatrix +0x3C, startIndex +0x40, numIndices +0x44, indexStride +0x48, numVertices +0x4C, instanceDataOffset +0x50, numInstances +0x54, instanceDataStride +0x58, indexBuffer +0x5A, numMatrices +0x5E, scissor +0x60, submitFlags +0x62, streamMask +0x63, uniformIdx +0x64, instanceDataBuffer +0x76, occlusionQuery +0x7A. Correction: +0x50/+0x54/+0x58/+0x76 are the instance-data view, not the mesh vertex buffer (6e-2 and the old header names said vertex buffer).
Draw path second part (docs/functions/e821120.md): on a view change or after a compute item the executor sets the graphics root signature (command list index 30) and the two descriptor heaps (index 28) and resets its `currentState` (a RenderDraw at job+0x4C0); otherwise it computes `changedFlags = previous ^ new` state. Primitive type = state bits 48..50. Then it copies the stream mask, instance-data fields and each bound stream. Still untranscribed: per-stream buffer/layout lookup, PSO selection, constant upload, the draw call.

### 6fk. Draw path third part: PSO call and batch-compatibility test (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e821120.md, third part. Per stream the executor takes the layout from the vertex buffer record (`ctx+0x9B2178 + handle*0x80`, u16 layout handle at record +0x78) or, when that is invalid, from the draw's stream; layouts are 0x54-byte records. The PSO is fetched per draw with `(state, stencil, three Mojang draw fields at +0x68/+0x6C/+0x70, streams, layouts, program, instanceDataStride/16, a framebuffer/format word)`. A draw joins the open batch only if bind hash, stencil, program, blend factor (when used), primitive type, topology, scissor and PSO all match and it is not an occlusion query; otherwise the batcher is flushed twice (0xe81b720 with 0 and 1). Bind changes go through a cache keyed by the RenderBind hash (hash map at job+0x678 with an LRU list): this is what 6df mis-called a "shader-variant hash map". RenderDraw gained `rgba` (+0x30, blend factor) and the three PSO parameter fields.

### 6fl. Executor draw path parts four to six: the draw path is transcribed end to end (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e821120.md parts four, five and six: descriptor tables (root 0 samplers, 1 SRV, 4 UAV), stencil ref, blend factor, topology, scissor rect, pipeline state; uniform update and per-shader constant commit (root 2 and 3 = vertex / fragment constant buffer views); then either an ExecuteIndirect (draws with an indirect buffer) or an append to the batcher (0x88 indexed / 0x70 non-indexed records). Corrections: draw **+0x74 is the index buffer handle** (the old +0x5A is `m_startIndirect`, +0x5C `m_numIndirect`, +0x78 `m_indirectBuffer`); 0xe81e1c0 is the constant-buffer commit, not descriptor creation; index/vertex buffer records carry the GPU address at +0x58 and size at +0x60. New typed structs with static_asserts: `d3d12::BatchDraw` (0x70), `BatchDrawIndexed` (0x88), buffer views, and record field constants (D3D12Context.h). Remaining holes in the draw path: bind-cache miss (descriptor build), setPredefined, and the helper bodies.

### 6fm. Two executor helpers reversed (static, 62.01) [V]
- **0xe81b4d0 = setVertexBuffers** (fully reversed, docs/functions/e81b4d0.md): per stream of the draw it transitions the vertex buffer to GENERIC_READ at most once per frame (per-context touched bitmap at +0xA258, touched list at +0x12458, state pairs at +0xA458), takes the stride from the layout record (table ctx+0xC96178, 0x54 bytes, stride u16 at +0x24) and writes the D3D12 vertex buffer view; the draw's vertex count becomes the minimum over the streams. New Sigs.json entry `D3D12Context::setVertexBuffers`.
- **0xe81e1c0 = commit(UniformBuffer)** (structure reversed, case bodies by shape, docs/functions/e81e1c0.md): the uniform opcode is stock bgfx (copy bit 0, num bits 1..10, loc bits 11..26, type bits 27..31 with the fragment bit), values come inline or from `ctx+0x1250 + handle*8`, and each is copied into the shader's constant scratch vector at its byte location; Mat3 is widened to three vec4 rows.
Assertions: 123 offline (all pass on 62.01).

### 6fn. Uniform update reversed; renderer slots 46/47 named (static, 62.01) [V]
0xbc6a9d0 = bgfx `rendererUpdateUniforms(renderer, UniformBuffer*, begin, end, touched*, ctx)` (fully reversed, docs/functions/bc6a9d0.md): replays the draw's uniform range; for each value opcode it records the handle (sorted, unique) in the caller's vector and calls renderer slot 46 `updateUniform(ctx, handle, data, size)` = `memcpy(ctx->m_uniforms[handle] /*ctx+0x1250*/, data, size)`; marker opcodes (type >= 5) go to slot 47 `setMarker` (empty in D3D12). Together with the constant commit (6fm) the full uniform path of a draw is now known: game setUniform -> Frame UniformBuffer -> updateUniforms -> per-handle value storage -> commit into each shader's constant scratch -> root CBVs 2/3. Slot names 46/47 from their bodies replace the positional guesses of 6k. Sigs.json: `Encoder::updateUniforms`. Assertions: 127 offline.

### 6fo. Predefined uniforms (static, 62.01) [V dispatch]
Executor draw path, docs/functions/e821120.md "predefined uniforms": the program record carries up to 28 `{u32 loc, u16 count, u8 type}` slots at prog+0x10 (used count u8 at prog+0xF0); a 14-entry jump table (rva 0xF4EE7F8) writes ViewRect, ViewTexel, View, InvView, Proj, InvProj, ViewProj, InvViewProj, (8: unidentified), Model, ModelView, ModelViewProj, (12, 13: unidentified) into the vertex or fragment constant scratch (type bit 0x10). Per-job view data: view matrices at job+0x90A0 indexed by job+0x1E8, view rect u16x4 at job+0x90A8, current viewProj pointer at job+0x2F0, model matrix cache at job+0x2F8 (64 bytes per matrix, indexed by draw.startMatrix), cached inverses flagged at job+0x10E / +0xE174..+0xE178. This is where a camera/projection change for all draws of a view would be applied natively [I].

### 6fp. Bind-cache miss = descriptor table build per binding type (static, 62.01) [V structure]
On a RenderBind hash miss the executor walks the 65 binding stages and, per stage, dispatches on `Binding.type` (u8 at +0x13; jump table rva 0xF4EE7D0, 10 types): unbound (idx 0xFFFF) and type 0 get a null/reserved descriptor slot; types 1 and 2 bind an index / vertex buffer as a shader buffer (SRV, or UAV with a transition to UNORDERED_ACCESS when `access` at +0x11 is non-zero); type 8 is the ranged binding that pulls every entry of the draw's side table (6fe) with per-texture state tracking at ctx+0x224A8 / +0x226AC (records at ctx+0x22498, stride 0x78); 7 is ignored; 3, 4, 5, 6, 9 recorded by target only. The stage count is a runtime global (rva 0x12AE0D80). Descriptor slots come from the heap object at job+0xB8 `{cpu +8, gpu +0x10, increment +0x18}`. Detail in docs/functions/e821120.md.

### 6fq. All ten binding types named; the 0xA32178 table is shader buffers, not framebuffers (static, 62.01) [V use, I names]
Reading the remaining cases of the descriptor build (jump table rva 0xF4EE7D0):
| type | target | what it reads | name |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0xe825ba6 | reserves a descriptor slot only | Reserved [?] |
| 1, 2 | 0xe825b15 | index (ctx+0x93A178) / vertex (ctx+0x9B2178) buffer record, UAV transition when access | IndexBuffer, VertexBuffer |
| 3 | 0xe82719f | texture record (ctx+0xBDE178, 0xB8), guard 0xe83abb0, dynamic-handle rule (>= 0x1000 and guard byte ctx+0xC96190) | Texture |
| 4 | 0xe8270ee | record of the ctx+0xA32178 table (0x78) with state tracking at ctx+0x224A8 / +0x226AC (UAV state 8) | ShaderBuffer |
| 5 | 0xe82609e | only when a global byte (rva 0xe82609e+7+0x42b99ae) has bit 0x80, else a reserved slot | feature-gated [?] |
| 6 | 0xe8260d8 | every side-table entry for the stage, as textures (dynamic-handle rule) | RangedTexture |
| 7 | - | skipped | Ignored |
| 8 | 0xe825be1 | every side-table entry, as ctx+0xA32178 records | RangedShaderBuffer |
| 9 | 0xe8271f3 | records `binding+0x00 & 0x1F0F07FF` (sampler flags) for the stage, slot reserved | Sampler |
**Correction to 6fc:** the job keeps `ctx+0xA32178` at job+0x3A0 and binds its records as shader resources and UAVs with buffer-style state tracking, and command byte 19 creates them from a data blob. So that table is **Mojang's shader buffers**, not framebuffers; `D3D12Tables::kFrameBuffers` was renamed `kShaderBuffers`. Where bgfx framebuffers live in this build is open again. Job resource table pointers (set at 0xe82187a..0xe8218ab): +0x588 vertex buffers, +0x5D8 index buffers, +0x3A0 shader buffers, +0x1F8 textures.

### 6fr. PSO key reversed; framebuffers found; vertex layout table base fixed (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e81e860.md "PSO key": the 13-argument signature and the full Murmur2A key (masked state, pass key, masked stencil, three Mojang draw fields, shader hashes and attribute masks, vertex layout hashes and the shader-filtered attribute list, framebuffer handle, instance data count, colour attachment format keys and the depth DSV format).
Corrections this forces:
- **Framebuffers are the table at ctx+0xC97698 (stride 0x68)**: per record u16 colour texture handles at +0x00, u16 depth handle at +0x10, a window/swap-chain pointer at +0x18, u8 colour count at +0x32. The header called this table `kUniforms`; renamed `kFrameBuffers` (FrameBufferRecord added). This answers the "where are framebuffers" question 6fq reopened.
- **Vertex layouts start at ctx+0xC96198**, not 0xC96178: 0xC96178 is the pointer to the overflow pages for texture handles >= 4096 (the inline texture array of 4096 x 0xB8 ends exactly there) and 0xC96190 is the dynamic-texture guard byte. Layout record = stock VertexLayout with 19 attributes: hash +0, stride +4, offsets +6, attributes +0x2C. setVertexBuffers' stride read (ctx + layout*0x54 + 0xC9619C) is record +4, so its dossier's "+0x24" was relative to the wrong base.
- Texture record +0x40 = format key hashed for render targets, +0xB0 = format index into a 16-byte format table (DXGI DSV format at +8).
Assertions: 149 offline, all pass on 62.01.
Addendum 6fr: the header's compile-time check caught that there are **64 vertex layouts, not 4096**: 0xC97698 - 0xC96198 = 0x1500 = 64 x 0x54, the stock BGFX_CONFIG_MAX_VERTEX_LAYOUTS. The table layout of RendererContextD3D12 between the texture array and the framebuffers is therefore exact: textures (4096 x 0xB8) | overflow page pointer, guard byte | 64 vertex layouts | framebuffers (0x68 each).

### 6fs. PSO description: the full state-word bit map (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e81e860.md "PSO description build, first part", from the code that fills D3D12_GRAPHICS_PIPELINE_STATE_DESC. State word: 0-3 write mask, 4-7 depth func (0 = off), 12-27 four blend factors, 28-30 rgb equation, 31-33 alpha equation, 34 independent blend, 35 alpha-to-coverage, 36-37 cull, 38 depth write, 40-47 alpha ref (dynamic), 48-50 primitive, 56 MSAA, 57 line AA, 58 conservative raster, 62 occlusion query. Stencil words: ref 0-7, read mask 8-15, func 16-19, fail 20-23, depth-fail 24-27, pass 28-31 (back = high word, or front when it is 0). FillMode is always SOLID, stencil write mask always 0xFF, DepthClipEnable = !(u8 at renderer +0x19FDC).
Game enum tables (all dumped; stock bgfx order): blend factor {rgb, alpha} at rva 0xF4ECC00 (1 ZERO .. 13 INV_FACTOR; SRC_COLOR/INV_SRC_COLOR/DST_COLOR/INV_DST_COLOR/SRC_ALPHA_SAT map to alpha factors for the alpha channel), blend equation 0xF4ECC70 (ADD SUB REVSUB MIN MAX), cull 0xF4ECCB0 (NONE FRONT BACK), compare 0xF4ECBD0 (off LESS LEQUAL EQUAL GEQUAL GREATER NOTEQUAL NEVER ALWAYS), stencil op 0xF4ECC90 (ZERO KEEP REPLACE INCR INCRSAT DECR DECRSAT INVERT).
**RenderDraw +0x68 / +0x6C / +0x70 = depth bias (int), slope-scaled depth bias (float), depth bias clamp (float)**: Mojang's per-draw depth bias, which goes into the PSO (so changing it creates a new pipeline). Encoder.h DrawState / StencilState carry the whole map.

### 6ft. PSO builder transcribed end to end (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e81e860.md, second part: input elements are built stock-bgfx style (streams claim the shader's attributes in order; instance data become float4 TEXCOORD elements after the highest TEXCOORD used, per-instance class); topology type from the primitive table (+4); render targets: no framebuffer = 1 target with the backbuffer format (u32 renderer+0x19F98) and D24S8 depth, window framebuffer = backbuffer colour and no depth, otherwise the framebuffer's texture formats and its depth texture's DSV format; SampleDesc = the 13th argument (so the "per-pass key" the executor passes from job+0x650 is the MSAA sample desc); `CreateGraphicsPipelineState` through the device at renderer+0x400 (ID3D12Device index 10); on failure the device-lost state at renderer+0x19F88 / +0x19F8C is set; on success the cache at cache+0x40 is updated. With 6fr/6fs this covers the whole function.

### 6gq. Three shared helpers fully reversed; "passLookup" is a thread-local getter (static, 62.01) [V]
- **0x4def080 = Mesh::hasDrawableData**: true when the mesh has CPU parts (12-byte elements at +0x58..+0x60), or non-empty GPU buffer records (+0x1D8 / +0x1FC) together with a ready resource (+0x1B0 / +0x1C0, ready bit at resource+0x12, touch counter +0x10), or a non-empty list at +0x248.
- **0x4dfb570 = resolve loaded texture**: handle -> slot (+0x18) -> resource with state byte +0x68 == 2 -> GPU texture with ready bit +0x12; returns the texture's shared ref or empty. Descriptions built before a texture is ready carry an empty texture.
- **0x5aba5c0 = ThreadLocalObject<T>::get** (previously called "passLookup"): returns the calling thread's 0x8B8-byte data block, creating it through a factory std::function and registering it in the owner's locked list on first use. The "queue into the pass" visitor cases (6gi) therefore append to per-thread blocks; extraction runs on several threads without contention.
Dossiers docs/functions/4def080.md, 4dfb570.md, 5aba5c0.md.

### 6gr. The shader-buffer slot writer, fully reversed (static, 62.01) [V]
0x5679db0 (docs/functions/5679db0.md) = set one uniform in a material instance's CPU uniform block: look up the 16-byte slot (+0x30 table; u16 byte offset at +0x08), bounds-check against the block size (+0x20), memcpy count*16 bytes into the data (+0x28), set the slot's dirty byte (+0x0C) and fold the slot index (FNV-1a) and the value's XXH64 into the block hash at +0x70 with boost::hash_combine. This is the write every extractor uses for its named uniforms; a hook here sees every per-draw uniform value by slot.

### 6gs. 0x675e440 is a resource cache, not a material resolver (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/675e440.md. `get(out, key16, loader, name, defines)`: lock the recursive mutex at +0x98; if the key is in the stale set (+0x40) retire the old holder into the vector at +0x80 and erase it; try_emplace the entry in the map at +0x00, create its holder if empty, mark it used (+0x30), and if the resource is missing or not ready build a LoadRequest {name string, defines map, reloaded flag +0x78} and load it through 0xbcbf730. Both containers hash the 16-byte key as hash_combine(lo, FNV-1a(hi)). Corrections: 6bm's "material resolver" and its "+0x98 loaded state / +0xe4 sentinel" are the mutex and its owner count; and line "0x5679db0 = entt-style sparse-set access" in the terrain note is wrong, it is the uniform writer (6gr).

### 6gt. The resource loader behind the cache (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/bcbf730.md. 0xbcbf730 = loadAsync: make the Resource now (its +0x10 byte is the cache's "already requested" flag, set on return), wrap the LoadRequest in a 0xE0-byte job (vtable 0xF44BED0) holding a weak_ptr to it, register the weak_ptr (0xbcc0960), then queue {weak_ptr, job}: owner thread (function-static thread id) -> vector at loader+0x18; worker -> its ThreadLocalObject block (list +0x118); a thread with no role drops the job. loader+0x270 refuses all loads. The job body 0xbcc06a0 runs an optional callback, locks the weak_ptr and calls 0xe86b730 to load. 0xbcc0ee0 is the synchronous twin used by the twin cache 0x567c8b0. Still open: the owner-thread drain of +0x18 / +0x118, and 0xe86b730.

### 6gu. The material load itself, fully reversed (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e86b730.md. Job::run -> 0xe86b730: material = ctx->materials->load(name, key, reloaded) (0xc2919d0, key = the base64 literal "dGhvc2Ug...ISA=", which decodes to "those are not the shaders you are looking for! "); MaterialInstance (0x470, ctor 0xbcd1be0) = material + a copy of the request's defines map; each instance dependency node of kind 1 (list at instance+0x3F0) is resolved through the context's std::function at ctx+0x40 with "<name>.material.bin" and stored as {FNV-1a(name), shared_ptr} in the instance's vector at +0x00. So the whole chain cache -> loader -> job -> material.bin is closed except the file loader 0xc2919d0, the instance ctor 0xbcd1be0 and the owner-thread drain of the job queue. Drain search so far: not in 0xbca0000..0xbcd0000 and not in 0x1700000..0x1800000 (no function there walks +0x18 with +0x118 and calls a job's slot 1); 0x1771500 is the out-of-line enqueue shared with core resource loaders (callers 0x174a0e0, 0x174e7c0, 0x1757110, 0x1f3ee50).


### 6gv. Material repository, file load and MaterialInstance layout (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/c2919d0.md, c292a20.md, bcd1be0.md. get(name, key, reload): locked unordered_map<string, shared_ptr<Material>>; on a miss or reload it calls loadFile with the primary source (+0x178), falling back to a built-in source. loadFile: for each shader platform in a global std::map (the first supported one whose file the source returns wins) path = platformDir(name) + extension(+0x90); bytes -> make_shared Material (0x190, ctor 0xbcd2790) -> deserialize(stream, key, true, 0x0D) at 0xe870350; rejected if any pass variant (0x88 bytes, shader at +0x58) is empty. MaterialInstance (0x470): +0x00 resolved deps, +0x18 global id, +0x20 0x108-byte state block, +0x1A0 uniform object (0x67bfa60, 0x40), +0x2A8 defines copy, +0x3E8 dependency map, then initFromMaterial 0xbcc7f20. Next: 0xe870350 (the material.bin parser that takes the key) and 0xbcc7f20.

### 6gw. material.bin header and encryption (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e870350.md. Header: u64 0x0A11DA1A, string "RenderDragon.CompiledMaterialDefinition", u64 version 0x1A, u32 FourCC NONE / SMPL / KYPR. NONE parses the body (0xe870870) directly. SMPL (string key): string keyHash, iv, ciphertext; keyHash must equal hash-alg-2(key) and is used as the cipher key, cipher made by 0xae3210(1, 5). KYPR (key pair): wrapped secret, iv, ciphertext; the secret is unwrapped, resized to 86 bytes and hashed into the cipher key. The built-in base64 key from 0xe86b730 is the SMPL key. Next: the body parser 0xe870870.

### 6gx. material.bin body format and shader selection (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e870870.md. Body: name, optional parent, u8 buffer count (name, reg, access, precision, unorderedAccess, type, texture format, 3 unknowns, 4 optional fields), u16 uniforms (name, type 0..3, count + optional default of 16/0x24/0x40 bytes), u16 string properties, u16 passes (name, platform bit string, fallback, optional blend mode, flag definitions, u32, u16 variants (supported, flags, shaders: stage, platform, inputs, u64 hash, bytecode blob)), trailing u64 0x0A11DA1A. Per variant the loader keeps one shader per stage: the highest platform <= min(0x0D, device max from 0xc25a1c0). The 0x0D argument (loadFile -> deserialize) is that cap.

### 6gy. MaterialInstance::initFromMaterial (static, 62.01) [V phases]
docs/functions/bcc7f20.md. name <- material name; uniform block created at instance+0x170 (0xe86a1b0); every uniform default written through 0x5679db0 (types: 1 vec4, 2 mat3, 3 mat4); defines applied as flags (0xe86ff10); per variant the bytecode is copied into a bgfx Memory, createShader 0xbc71f60, setName 0xbc729e0, then createProgram(vsh, fsh, true) 0xbc72c80 or createProgram(sh, true) 0xbc73000, handle stored at variant+0x1C as {u16, valid bit 16}. So bgfx programs for materials are created at load, not per frame. Corrects 6gv: +0x1A0 is not the uniform block, +0x170 is.

### 6gz. Pass lambdas: the ScreenDraw path, and the VV pass-group table (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/fullscreen_pass.md. Fullscreen post passes are declared by a group builder (ColorPostProcess 0xbbbcb20) through declareScreenPass 0xbbbd3d0 -> buildScreenPass 0xbc37320 -> registerPass 0xbc37ca0, with an execute std::function (vtable 0xF440710) whose _Do_call tail-jumps to ONE shared body, ScreenDraw 0xbc04dc0: it writes the pass rect and four mat4 straight into s_ctx->views[viewId] (+0x10/+0x14/+0x20/+0x60/+0xA0/+0xE0/+0x120/+0x132), emits the debug marker "ScreenDraw:<pass name>", binds the material (0xe86d7f0), state, uniforms, textures and buffers, then submits through 0xbcc38e0 (geometry variant: none / 64-byte item list / mesh). So every ScreenDraw pass can be intercepted at one address (0xbc04dc0) with the pass record in r8 (name std::string at +0x268).
VV pass-group builders (callee of 0x55f1450 -> passes whose FNV-1 hash they hold): 0xbb3e2b0 voxel (UploadDirtyVolumeKeys, UploadPerGroupLerps, CombineVoxelLighting); 0xbb4c2e0 depth (DepthOnlyOpaque/AlphaTest); 0xbb6b350 deferred lighting (CausticsMultiplierPass, DoDeferredLighting, DirectionalLightingPass, DiscreteIndirectCombinedLightingPass); 0xbb74040 (SurfaceRadianceUpscalePass, DoDeferredShading); 0xbb78240 DepthAndNormal; 0xbb7b210 DoWaterSurfaceBuffer; 0xbb7efb0 WaterExtinction; 0xbb81880 WaterSurface; 0xbb84ee0 forward (ForwardPBRAlphaTest/Transparent, HighlightTextureEdge); 0xbb8eb70 DoIndirectSpecularUpscale; 0xbbbcb20 ColorPostProcess; 0xbbbfc50 NothingTask; 0xbc57460 LightClustering; 0xbc7c580 VolumeScattering; 0xbc7f6e0 MinMax init/downsample; 0xbc82ad0 SSR (RayMarch, RayMarchHZB, FillGaps, GetReflectedColor); 0xbc8a380 TAAUpscaling; 0xbc8eb90 LuminanceHistogram/HistogramBuild; 0xbc98310 bloom down (DFDownSample, ThresholdedDownSample); 0xbc9ada0 bloom up (DFUpSample, BloomBlend); 0xbc9d7a0 local exposure (Clear/Build/FilterBilateralGrid, ApplyLocalExposure); 0xbcd77b0 CubemapToOffscreen; 0xbcdba70 Convolve. Indirect (no direct caller from 0x55f1450): 0xbbe2d50 RTX prep (PreBlasSkinDispatch, PrepareTLAS, AlphaTestCracks), 0xbcc4b90 RTX denoise/raygen (43 passes, from 0xbc080c0), 0xbcb3cf0 FSR1 (from 0xbcb64f0).

### 6ha. VV pass execute bodies, and a correction to 6gz (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/pass_bodies.md. Three ways a pass gets its body: a pass object (vtable slot 2: deferred lighting/shading 0xbc2c590/0xbc2cd20/0xbc2d550/0xbc2dd20, water 0xbc2eb50/0xbc2f4a0, voxel combine 0xbc0b240, ClusterLights 0xbc5bad0), the ScreenDraw std::function (0xbc04dc0), or the generic draw-list task 0x568cd90 (depth prepasses, DepthAndNormal, water surface buffer, forward PBR, RTX prep). DirectionalLightingPass 0xbc2cd20: same view writes as ScreenDraw, marker "DoDeferredLighting", material bound from resources+0x90, textures/buffers from +0x38, pass looked up by FNV-1 hash 0x993692FCF6FC1544 in the material's pass table (+0x2F8), identity transform, submit 0xbcc38e0. Correction: 0xbcc38e0's variant is the transform (0 = one inline mat4, 1 = list of mat4 = instancing, 2 = pointer form) and its 5th argument is the material pass, not a mesh.

### 6hb. How SSR, voxel upload and RTX denoise register their bodies (static, 62.01) [V]
SSR: through 0xbc88560, a ScreenDraw std::function (vtable 0xF44B280, slot 2 = 0xbc04dc0), so all four SSR passes run the shared ScreenDraw body. Voxel upload: pass object (vtable 0xF43EF40) from 0xbc09b10, body 0xbc0ac20. RTX denoise/raygen: pass object vtable 0xF43EC30 installed by the RTX prep builder at 0xbbe77f3; body 0xbc080c0 -> 0xbcc4b90, which records its 43 passes straight into the encoder (submit 0xbc60740, dispatch variant 0xbc60d10, submitFrameParams 0xbc618e0). With 6ha this gives an execute body for every VV pass group.

### 6hc. The 0xC8 item record is an index record (static, 62.01) [V]
src/sdk/mc/deps/minecraft_renderer/framebuilder/DrawItem.h (static_asserts, used by RenderMarkHook's kItemSize). The two writers called from RenderMark::bodyActor, 0x6765020 (read in full) and 0x6768c70 (same sequence), look up the pass object (0x5aba5c0), append each argument to a per-pass pool and store the new pool index (size-1) as a u16 in a zeroed 0xC8 record, then push the record onto the pass's item vector (+0x00/+0x08/+0x10; grow 0x5aba6d0). The record holds no data of its own: +0x00 u32 valid mask (1 -> 0x4400001 -> 0x7407001), +0x10 index into u16 pool +0x8A0, +0x28 pool +0x780 (0x58), +0x2A pool +0x768 (0x88 shader-state blocks), +0x2C pool +0x750 (0x58 state), +0x3C u16 pool +0x690, +0x40 pool +0x660 (0x2C), +0x42 pool +0x648 (0x48), +0x44 u32 pool +0x630; writer A also sets +0x04 = 2 and +0x52 -> pool +0x588 (0x30 records), writer B sets u16 +0x0A = 2 and +0xB2 -> pool +0x108 (0x38 records). So the 56-bit item hash RenderMarkHook uses (6bg) is a hash of pool indices, stable within a frame because pools only grow. The batch instance (0x48) is now a typed struct in FrameBuilder.h from the 6d capture.

### 6hd. DrawItem pool element layouts (static, 62.01) [V sizes and copy shapes, I roles]
DrawItem.h now types every pool element: +0x108 DrawItemShared (0x38: two shared_ptrs, 16 bytes, u64), +0x588 DrawItemSmallVector (0x30: data, u32 size, u32 cap, one inline 0x20 element), +0x660 DrawItemBounds (0x2C: optional 6-float box with index byte +0x18, 4 floats), +0x648 DrawItemTransform (0x48: the same transform variant submitPass 0xbcc38e0 takes, index +0x40), +0x750 DrawItemState (0x58 render state, same block DirectionalLightingPass copies from resources+0x23C), +0x768 DrawItemShaderState (0x88: u64, 0x78 block via 0xe869ff0, owned pointer), +0x780 DrawItemMaterial (0x58: u64, vector of 0x28 texture bindings, u64, 4-way material reference variant with index +0x38, 24 bytes: the same record buildScreenPass takes). So an actor body item references the same transform / material / state records the pass bodies submit, which ties M2d to M1e: a DrawItem is one draw call's arguments, stored by pool index.

### 6hf. Material holder, uniform slot lookup and the material-reference variant (static, 62.01) [V]
The "material" argument of pass builders and of bindMaterial 0xe86d7f0 is `{MaterialInstance* +0x00, UniformBlock +0x08}`: the block is the one 0x5679db0 writes (slot table block+0x30, u16 slot count block+0x3A, 16-byte entries pointing at uniform nodes with the FNV-1a name hash at node+0x28 and a flag at +0x34). bindMaterial walks those entries and, for flag == 1, resolves the hash in the MaterialInstance map at +0x368. The 4-way material reference variant (index byte +0x38 of the 0x58 material record and of buildScreenPass's argument; jump tables 0xF440DD0 / 0xF2BFD94): 0 = empty, 1 = an 8-byte value, 2 = a 16-byte plain value, 3 = shared_ptr (AddRef'd on copy); concrete types of 1 and 2 not resolved [?].
M1f note: the live (D3) pass needs Cheat Engine attached read-only to the running game; the CE bridge was not reachable on 2026-11-14, so no D3 reads were taken.

### 6hg. material.bin crypto resolved: SHA-256 + AES-256-GCM (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/e870350.md addendum. OpenSSL-backed helpers: hash alg 2 = SHA-256 (NID table 64/672/673/674 for ids 1..4, id 0 = built-in MD5); makeCipher(type, mode): type 0/1 = AES-128/256, modes 0..6 = ECB, CBC, CFB8, CFB, OFB, GCM, CTR. SMPL decrypts with AES-256-GCM keyed by SHA-256(key string) (checked against the stored hash); KYPR also uses AES-256-GCM, keyed by SHA-256 of its 86-byte unwrapped secret.

### 6hh. Resource loader lifecycle: per-frame merge found, job drain still open (static, 62.01) [V]
docs/functions/bcbf730.md addendum. The loader lives at owner+0x1308 in a RenderDragon object. Per frame, 0xc288990 (from BgfxFrameBuilder::endFrame) merges the per-thread tracked weak_ptr blocks into the owner-thread vector. At shutdown 0xc287b30 -> 0xc28bcf0 unloads every live tracked resource and drops pending jobs unrun; 0xc28b100 is the destructor. The per-frame runner of queued jobs (slot 1 = Job::run 0xbcc06a0) is still not found; the ranges and shapes ruled out are listed in the addendum.

### 6hi. Per-frame pending-job merge (static, 62.01) [V]
0xc288880 (from endFrame) runs 0x67591e0 for the seven loaders embedded in the RenderDragon owner (+0x4C8, +0x710, +0x958, +0xBE8, +0xE60, +0x10C0, +0x1308 = materials): per-thread pending {weak_ptr, job} entries whose resource is still alive move into loader+0x18; the rest are deleted. With 6hh both per-thread queues are now accounted for; only the consumer of loader+0x18 that runs the jobs remains.

### 6hj. The resource job runner (static, 62.01) [V]
0x6622610 processPending(loader), called from endFrame and six other sites: merge per-thread pending (0x67591e0), swap out loader+0x18, run them with 0x12893d0 (lock each weak_ptr, Job::run via slot 1, delete, collect continuations, count), run and delete continuations. This closes the async load path from ResourceCache::get to material.bin; loads complete on the processPending thread, one frame after the request at most.

### 6hm. Stencil face encoding (static, 62.01) [V]
0xe86d770(face, ref, readMask) = ref | readMask << 8 | testTable[face.func-1] (bits 16..19, same order as depth) | failS[face+0x0C] (bits 20..23) | failZ[face+0x04] (bits 24..27) | passZ[face+0x08] (bits 28..31). Op enum 0..7 = Keep, Zero, Replace, IncrSat, DecrSat, Invert, Incr, Decr; the game tables map IncrSat, DecrSat and Invert to bgfx ZERO. The two bytes after the stencil flag (+0x55/+0x56) are the reference and read mask. DrawItemState::StencilFace typed.

### 6hp. Correction: the "material record" is the GEOMETRY reference (static, 62.01) [V]
The 0x58-byte record (DrawItem pool +0x780; buildScreenPass's 2nd argument; ScreenPass+0x88; resources+0x38 in DirectionalLightingPass) is what the draw binds as geometry. 0xbcc2b00 (previously named bindTextures) binds vertex streams: for each 0x28-byte stream reference in the vector at +0x08..+0x18 it resolves {handle, static/dynamic, offset} (0xbcc2820; dynamic VB table s_ctx+0x1DBD0C8, stride 0x1C) and writes the RenderStream {startVertex, handle, layout} into the encoder draw (encoder+0x40 + i*8) and the stream-mask bit (draw+0x63); +0x20 = vertex count. 0xbcc2eb0 (previously bindBuffers) binds the index buffer from the 4-way variant at +0x28 (index +0x38): 1 = pointer to {u16 handle, u8 dynamic, u32 size, u8 32-bit}, 2 = {u64 id, u8} via 0xe32e310, 3 = shared_ptr (object +0x18 = the buffer record); then +0x40 u32 start index and +0x48 u64 index count go to RenderDraw startIndex/numIndices/indexBuffer (encoder +0x80/+0x84/+0xB4 = draw +0x40/+0x44/+0x74, matching Frame.h). So 6gz/6hd/6hf's "material reference variant" is the index buffer reference, and the "texture bindings" are vertex streams. DrawItemMaterial is renamed DrawItemGeometry (DrawItem.h) with the tail typed.

### 6hq. Vertex stream reference (static, 62.01) [V]
The 0x28-byte elements of the geometry reference are resolved by 0xbcc2820: variant index +0x18: 0 = pointer to a vertex buffer object (u16 handle +0x0C, layout +0x10), 1 = {u64 id, u8} via 0xe327b30, 2 = owner pointer whose +0x18 is the buffer object; for kind 1 a u32 at +0x10 with bit 16 set picks a vertex-layout override (low 16 bits FNV-1a hashed into a mutex-guarded map: buckets +0x60, mask +0x78); +0x20 u32 start vertex. Typed as DrawItemVertexStream.

### 6hr. The inline-transform submit and the Frame matrix cache (static, 62.01) [V]
0xbcc3b90 (submitPass alternative 0, read in full): program = 0xbcc7ce0(materialPass) ({u16 handle, valid}); if valid, the inlined bgfx::setTransform reserves a slot with an atomic increment of Frame+0xD457C0 (cap 0xFFFF), copies the 64-byte matrix to Frame+0x9457C0 + slot*0x40, sets the draw's startMatrix (encoder+0x7C = draw+0x3C) to the slot and numMatrices (draw+0x5E) to 1, then Encoder::submit(encoder, viewId, program, occlusion 0xFFFF, depth, preserveState). Frame.h gains kMatrixCache 0x9457C0 / kMatrixCount 0xD457C0 with a static_assert that the 65536-entry cache ends at the counter.
M2d closed: every byte of DrawItem, its pool elements, the batch Instance, RenderControllerMesh, MeshData, the state block, stencil faces, the transform variant, the geometry reference and the vertex stream reference is typed with static_asserts. Fields whose meaning is still unknown are marked [?] in the headers (geometry +0x50 is copied by the packet constructor but read by no bind or submit path).

### 6hu. Index/vertex buffer records field by field (static, 62.01) [V]
BufferD3D12::create 0xe812740 (read in full; 10 callers) initialises the 0x78-byte buffer record: +0x00 ID3D12Resource*, +0x08..+0x2F the SRV description (format, dimension 1 = buffer or 0xB = raytracing acceleration structure, mapping 0x1688, first element, numElements = size/stride, stride, flags), +0x30..+0x57 the UAV description, +0x58 GPU VA, +0x60 size, +0x64 bgfx buffer flags, +0x66 dynamic, +0x68 persistent CPU mapping (Map when flags & 0x2000), +0x70 element stride. Index records are exactly this; vertex records add the layout handle at +0x78 (stride 0x80). Typed as d3d12::BufferRecord with static_asserts tying it to the table strides and the existing executor offsets.
The RTX renderer's createIndexBuffer / createVertexBuffer overrides (0xe83d210 / 0xe83d260, vtable slots 10 / 14) call the base create and, on an RTX frame, append the new handle to RTX lists (ctx+0x41638C8 / +0x41618C4, 4096 entries); vertex buffers are also registered as geometry with vertexCount = size / layout stride (0xe84b620).

### 6hv. Shader record field by field (static, 62.01) [V]
createShader 0xe8137b0 (vtable slot 22) checks the bgfx binary magic (CSH 0x03485343, FSH 0x05485346, VSH 0x05485356; else "Unknown shader format %x.") and fills the 0xB8 record in stock bgfx ShaderD3D12 order: +0x00 code Memory, +0x08 UniformBuffer* (capacity 0x400), +0x10 PredefinedUniform[14] (fragment shaders OR 0x10 into the type), +0x80 u16 attribute mask[19] (0xFFFF per attribute read), +0xA8 MurmurHash2A hash, +0xAC numUniforms, +0xAE constant size, +0xB0 numPredefined. Typed as d3d12::ShaderRecordLayout, asserted against the table stride and the executor offsets.

### 6hw. Program record complete (static, 62.01) [V]
createProgram 0xe8142c0 (slot 24, read in full): program+0x00 = &shaders[vsh], +0x08 = &shaders[fsh] (left unset for fsh 0xFFFF), +0x10 = vsh predefined uniforms then fsh ones (memcpy numPredefined*8 each), +0xF0 = the sum; +0xF1..+0xF7 never written. Typed as d3d12::ProgramRecordLayout.

### 6hx. Framebuffer and vertex layout records (static, 62.01) [V]
Vertex layouts: createVertexLayout (slot 12, 0xe812bb0) is a 0x54-byte memcpy of bgfx::VertexLayout into ctx+0xC96198+h*0x54, so the record is that struct verbatim. Framebuffers: the functions indexing ctx+0xC97698 are slots 39/40/41 (plus the PSO builder and executor); slot 39 0xe817880 = createFrameBuffer(handle, nwh, ...): it first releases any framebuffer already on that window (Release() its swap chain at +0x18, clear +0x20 window, +0x33, +0x64, set +0x10 and +0x30 to 0xFFFF), appends the handle to the window list (ctx+0x2F8 count, u16 list +0x2FA), creates the swap chain (0xe9241b0) into +0x18 and an RTV per back buffer, then sets +0x20 = window, +0x30 = window index, +0x32 = 1. Typed as d3d12::FrameBufferRecordLayout (color[8], depth, swap chain, window, window index, num color, dirty +0x64). Mojang inserted extra vtable slots before the framebuffer API, so stock bgfx slot numbering does not apply past 34.

### 6hy. Framebuffer record complete (static, 62.01) [V]
Slot 40 0xe8176a0 = createFrameBuffer(handle, u8 num, const Attachment*): +0x30 = 0xFFFF, +0x33 = num, num x 6-byte attachment records memcpy into +0x34 (8 x 6 = +0x34..+0x63), then 0xe819b50(record) derives the color/depth handles and each attached texture state is updated (0xe82d960). Slot 41 0xe817c00 = destroyFrameBuffer: Release the swap chain (+0x18), clear +0x20/+0x33/+0x64, +0x10 = 0xFFFF, and swap-remove the record from the window list (ctx+0x2F8 count, +0x2FA list), patching the moved record +0x30. FrameBufferRecordLayout now covers all 0x68 bytes.

### 6hz. Texture record (static, 62.01) [V tail, ? views]
createTexture (slot 26: RTX thunk 0xe83d400 tracks the handle, base 0xe8143c0) locates the record (handles >= 0x1000 walk the overflow pages at ctx+0xC96178: 4096 records = 0xB8000 bytes per page, next-page pointer after them) and calls TextureD3D12::create 0xe81bf30. Its direct writes, from the bimg::ImageContainer: +0x98 flags (low 32), +0x9C/+0xA0 width/height after skipped mips (clamped to the block size), +0xA4 depth, +0xA8 numLayers, +0xAE type (0 2D, 1 3D, 2 cube), +0xAF requested format, +0xB0 actual format (0x42 fallback when caps lack the type), +0xB1 numMips - skip; +0x00..+0x1F stored as one block (resource pointer first). +0x20..+0x97 (the view descriptions, holding the PSO format key at +0x40) are filled by helpers not yet read. Typed as d3d12::TextureRecordLayout.
M2e closed: all seven record types (buffer = index/vertex, shader, program, framebuffer, vertex layout, texture) are typed with static_asserts tied to the table strides and the executor offsets; the only untyped range is the texture view block.

### 6ia. DrawBatcher as a header; M2b closed (static, 62.01) [V]
The batcher fields from the flush dossier (e81b720.md) are now d3d12::DrawBatcherOffsets (command signatures +0, pending +0x10, records +0x18, ring slot +0xF28, barrier table +0xF30..+0x1040, VB cache +0x10C0 (5 views), IB cache +0x1110, last CBVs +0x1120, stats +0x1148/+0x1150, capacity +0x115C, indirect flag +0x1164) with static_asserts that the cache sizes chain exactly, plus JobContextOffsets::kCommandList 0x30. The eight batcher offsets are added to both assertion lists (layout_asserts.json: 168 assertions pass on 62.01; RenderLayoutAsserts.h kDrawFlush, with SelfTest static_asserts tying its literals to the header). With BatchDraw / BatchDrawIndexed already typed, M2b is closed.

### 6ib. RenderWeatherDescription, and how to name extractor uniforms (static, 62.01) [V mapping]
Every description read in the Weather extractor 0x6745440 gives fields up to +0x12A (size 0x130 assumed, aligned [I]). The ten vec4 blocks are material uniforms, named by matching each block's load to the FNV-1a hash static it is compared against: +0x88 FogColor, +0x98 FogAndDistanceControl, +0xA8 PositionBaseOffset, +0xB8 PrevPositionForwardOffset, +0xC8 PrevPositionBaseOffset, +0xD8 PositionForwardOffset, +0xE8 ViewPosition, +0xF8 UVOffsetAndScale, +0x108 Velocity, +0x118 Dimensions. +0x50/+0x60/+0x70 are texture references passed to the binder 0xbcaba00 (+0x50 = WeatherTexture; the other two are among Occlusion/Height/Lighting) [I]; +0x80 int becomes the OcclusionHeightOffset value [I]. Typed in Descriptions.h.
Method (reusable for every extractor): (1) hash statics initialised inside the extractor are hashed at runtime in cold blocks; pair each static with its string through the function-local guard: the guard acquire 0xe938cb0 precedes the string's `lea rdx` + std::string ctor 0x79580, the guard release 0xe938d70 follows the static store. (2) Statics shared with other renderers are constant-folded in an initialiser (here 0x67ed710: one movabs + store per uniform); match the constant against FNV-1a 64 of candidate names. That shared table at rva 0x129F8310.. holds the actor-material uniform hashes: Bones, MatTexture, TileLightColor, TileLightIntensity, ChangeColor, ColorBased, FogColor, FogControl, HudOpacity, OverlayColor, GlintTexture, UVScale, GlintColor (3 entries 0x1ED33D4EC79302E9, 0x0979B878DF736140, 0x0D178ABB47341E4F unnamed). A hook on these statics' consumers would see every actor overlay/tint uniform by name.

### 6ic. RenderParticleDescription; RenderControllerMesh is mce::Mesh (static, 62.01) [V]
descscan (the 6ib method as a script: follows the description from r8 through register copies and its spill slot, lists every field read, names uniform blocks through guarded and constant-folded FNV-1a statics) on the Particle extractor 0x67417f0: +0x00 mce::Mesh* (gated by Mesh::hasDrawableData 0x4def080), +0x18 / +0x30 optional texture pointers [I], +0x20/+0x28 shared_ptr kept alive on the pass (+0x80/+0x88), +0x6C FogColor, +0x7C FogAndDistanceControl, +0x8C/+0x8D enums, +0x91 flag, +0x93 0/1/2, +0x98 MERSUniforms with its enable byte +0xA8. Typed in Descriptions.h (size 0xB0 [I]).
Mesh::hasDrawableData reads the RenderControllerMesh fields one for one (MeshData +0x50, handle groups +0x1D8/+0x1FC/+0x220, vector +0x248), so the batch instance's render object is an mce::Mesh and the three 15-handle groups are its GPU buffer handles. Noted in FrameBuilder.h.

### 6id. FlameBillboard and Gameface descriptions; descscan tool (static, 62.01) [V reads]
tools/sigs/descscan.py <extractor rva> [maxoff] automates 6ib: description field reads plus uniform names. FlameBillboard / ActorPrepass extractor 0x673d150: +0x00 mesh, +0x48/+0x58/+0x70 textures (FlameAtlas, MERSTexture, NormalTexture [I]) with +0x80 flag, PBR scalars Metalness +0xA8 / Emissive +0xAC / Roughness +0xB0 / Subsurface +0xB4 enabled by +0xB8, UVOffset +0xC0, FogColor +0xC8, FogAndDistanceControl +0xD8, TileLightIntensity +0xE8, DitheringEnabledToggle +0xF0 (RenderFlameBillboardDescription, 0xF8 [I]). Gameface/Renoir extractor 0x66ade80 (materials Gameface/ClearQuad, Path, Renoir, Standard(Rare), Stencil(Path/Rare), TexturesWithColorMix; uniforms Viewport, Data_VS, Data_PS): reads +0x00, +0x28/+0x38 and +0x40/+0x50 (address + byte pairs), +0x30, +0x48, vec4 +0x58 and +0x68 (RenderGameFaceDescription, 0x78 [I], meanings [?]). EditorBlockVolumeWireframe 0x66a1860 only reads +0x00 itself (uniforms MatColor, CameraDirection, FrameTime) and hands the description to callees.

### 6ie. Producer scan; RenderCubemapDescription (static, 62.01) [V stores]
tools/sigs/producerscan.py <thin clone rva> lists, for each call to a thin insert clone, the stores its caller makes into the stack description passed in rdx, plus sub-objects built in place (lea rcx, [desc+off]; call). Limits: producers that memcpy a prepared description or reuse the stack frame give partial or noisy results (EndSky, CelestialBody and Weather producers did), so each result needs a check against the extractor reads (descscan). Cubemap (only producer 0x6606580) is clean: ctor 0x668eb10 = { +0x00 resolved texture ref via 0x4dfb570, +0x10 mat4, +0x50 u16, +0x52 u8 }, then direct stores +0x60 qword, +0x68 byte, +0x70..+0x88 qwords, +0x90/+0xA0/+0xB0 vec4: RenderCubemapDescription (0xC0) in Descriptions.h. descscan also gained --start/--end/--reg for inline visitor cases (Sky tag 0 Atmosphere reads +0x00 u16, vec4 +0xA4/+0xB4/+0xC4, pointer +0xE0; most inline cases hand the description straight to callees).

### 6if. Producer stores for 18 thin clones recorded, not yet typed (static, 62.01) [? until cross-checked]
docs/data/producer_fields.txt holds producerscan output for every thin clone (BlitFlipbook, Particle, AimAssistHighlight, EditorHighlight, UIMesh, ChunkOccluders, LightDirectional, Editor 0..6, Cubemap, EndSky, CelestialBody, Weather). Clean-looking: ChunkOccluders (mat4 +0x00..+0x3F, +0x40 qword, +0x48 u16, then fields to about +0x10F), BlitFlipbook (texture reference via 0x4dfb570 at +0x00, fields to +0x9F), Editor1 and Editor2 (identical shapes, the same producer template), Editor5/Editor6 (near-identical). Noisy (overlapping misaligned stores = other stack locals inside the window): Editor0/3/4, UIMesh, EditorHighlight, EndSky, CelestialBody, Weather. Typing rule kept: a description becomes a struct only when the producer stores and the extractor reads agree (as for Weather 6ib, Particle 6ic, Cubemap 6ie). The Item visitor's inline cases move the description off rdi before reading it, so descscan --reg needs the right per-case register; ChunkOccluders' case (0x6646a89) is not settled yet. New uniform hashes seen in the Item visitor: CurrentColor, PBRData, CracksTexture, MERSUniforms, DitheringEnabledToggle.

### 6ig. RenderChunkOccludersDescription confirmed both ways (static, 62.01) [V]
Item visitor dispatch: rbx = frame builder, rdi = description (= [r8]), frame context spilled at rbp+0x8A0. Case 4 (0x6646a89) only fetches the "ChunkOccluder" material (0x67d6d70) and joins the shared tail; the description is read after 0x6647e04: +0x00..+0x3F mat4 (copied out), +0x40 mce::Mesh* (positions count from MeshData +0x58/+0x60 / 12, count +0x1D0 valid when +0x1D4), +0x48 u16. That matches the producer stores (mat4, +0x40 qword, +0x48 u16) exactly; nothing past +0x49 is read, so the producer's later stores were other locals. Typed as RenderChunkOccludersDescription (0x50 [I]); mce::Mesh +0x1D0/+0x1D4 named in FrameBuilder.h. Method note: for inline Item cases, scan from the shared tail with --reg rdi, not from the case entry.

### 6ih. RenderAtmosphereDescription and RenderBlockChangesDescription typed (static, 62.01) [V reads, ? meanings]

Sky tag 0 (visitor 0x66560f0, case 0x6656174, join 0x6656d4b, desc in rdi): descscan lists the reads. Overlapping reads exist (qword +0xE8 with float +0xEC; qword +0x100 with qword +0x104), so 64-bit fields are stored as uint32[2] and the overlapped ones as single dwords. The uniform per field (SkyColor, FogColor, SunMoonColor, StarsColor, UV0Transform, CubemapRotation, MinimumSkyLuminance, CloudRenderDistanceAndCloudHeight) is not resolved [?]. Size 0x138 [I: aligned read end]. BlockChanges (Item tag 9, case 0x6646bc6) copies 0x18 bytes into the pass vector at +0x98 (0x18-byte elements): vec4 +0x00, qword +0x10, size 0x18 [I]. Both in Descriptions.h with static_asserts; Release build OK.

6ih addendum (Atmosphere repack) [V]: case 0x6656174 does not write uniforms itself. At 0x6656d4b..0x6656f60 it repacks the description into a stack block at rbp+0x250..0x30C (vec4 +0x128 -> rbp+0x250; dword +4 -> +0x260; qword +8 -> +0x264; width = max(const, +0x10 - +0x0C) -> +0x26C; vec4s +0x14..+0x84 -> rbp+0x270..0x2E0 in order; qword +0x94 -> +0x2F0; dword +0x9C -> +0x2F8; byte +0xA0 -> +0x2FC; qwords +0xF0/+0xF8 -> +0x300/+0x308), copies another run of locals to rbp+0x348..0x378, takes a shared_ptr lock (lock inc [ctrl+8]) for the +0xD8/+0xE0 pair, and passes the block (rdx = rbp+0x250) onward. So the per-field uniform names live in the callee that consumes this block, not in the visitor case: that callee is the next target for the uniform mapping (read it from the call after 0x6656f56).
6ih correction [V]: there is no consumer call for the repacked block. After the repack the case looks up the pass object with 0x5aba5c0 (thread-local getter at frame ctx+0x9D0, 6gq), reads a key from a vcall (slot +0x10, dl = 0x4a), and appends an element to a std::vector on the pass at +0x548/+0x550 (grow path 0x6657173: element size 0x58, max 0x2E8BA2E8BA2E8BB). Each element holds a 0x30-byte block copied from rbp+0x348 plus a shared_ptr pair ({ptr, control block}, lock inc [ctrl+8]) at +0x30..+0x3F; the second variant (0x6657088, other tag) uses a vector at +0xB0/+0xB8 with 0x10-byte elements. So Atmosphere is QUEUED into the Sky pass like PlayerVision/BlockChanges, and the per-field uniform names are read by the Sky pass's own consumer of that vector (find the readers of pass+0x548), not in the visitor. The repack at rbp+0x250..0x30C is a local used by the same case before queueing [I: not stored in the element].
6ih reader scan [V scan, I reading]: a scan of 0x6600000-0x6800000 for `[reg + 0x548]` finds no reader of the Sky vector at the same base. The hits are other objects (0x6751ec3 and 0x67b8e4b treat +0x548 as a hash-map anchor next to +0x570 mask / +0x558 buckets; 0x6624855 is a vector reset in the 0x66248xx region; 0x66579bd/0x6657b04 are further queue cases of the same Sky visitor). Since 0x5aba5c0 returns a thread-local object (6gq), the Atmosphere queue is a per-thread staging vector that a later merge step moves into the pass; the reader is that merge, not a per-pass consumer. Next: find the merge by scanning for 0x58-stride (imul 0x58) loops over a vector whose begin is [obj+0x548] on the thread-local type, or follow the per-thread lists from the endFrame merge 0xc288880 (6hi) which already merges per-thread pending lists.
6ih BlitFlipbook (Effect tag 1, case 0x666aec2) [V calls, ? fields]: the case resolves its material through ResourceCache::get 0x675e440 (6gs) with the name record taken from [r12+0x40] and [r12+0x68], then checks the loaded flag ([material+0x10] == 1) and takes a shared_ptr (lock inc [ctrl+8]). The visitor's statics/strings for this and the neighbouring cases are Flipbook, UI/Base, BlitTexture, CurrentColor, MatTexture, PBRData, TextureOpacity, TintColor, VBlendControl. The register holding the description in this case is NOT r8 (descscan with --reg r8 finds no reads; with --reg r12 only +0x00/+0x10/+0x27/+0x40/+0x68 appear and r12 may be the frame context, not the description), so no struct is typed yet [?]. Correct next step: identify the description register at the case entry from the visitor prologue, then rerun descscan on 0x666aec2..0x666b700.
6ih correction (Effect visitor 0x666aca0 registers) [V]: the prologue loads r12 = rdx (the FRAME CONTEXT), rbx = rcx, r15 = [r8] (the DESCRIPTION), and dispatches on byte [r8+8] (the tag, <= 8) through a jump table. So in BlitFlipbook (case 0x666aec2) the [r12+0x40]/[r12+0x68] reads are fields of the frame context (inputs to ResourceCache::get), not the description; the description is r15. descscan with --reg r15 over the case body 0x666aec2..0x666afde finds no description reads: the case jumps to the shared tail 0x666b67c, where the reads happen ([r15+0x10] -> rax, [r15+0x18] -> r14 at 0x666b6cf/0x666b6d3, spill of r15 to [rbp+0x668]). Reading the tail separates BlitFlipbook from its neighbours only with its own bounds; not done yet [?]. Use `--reg r15` (not r12) for every Effect case.
6ih BlitFlipbook description (Effect tag 1) [V tail reads, ? rest]: the shared tail 0x666b67c..0x666c846 treats the description (r15) as holding an ARRAY: count = u64 [desc+0x10], data pointer = [desc+0x18], element stride 0x40 (loop 0x666b6cf: end = data + (count << 6), `add r14, 0x40` at 0x666b700). Per element (r14) the loop reads qword +0x00 (x3, 0x666b942), qword +0x18 and dword +0x20 (both feed uniform VBlendControl, 0x666b891/0x666b897) and a 16-byte block at +0x24 (0x666ba25); other element fields are not yet decoded. Each iteration also builds a per-element draw from the frame-context object ([r12], vcall chain through 0xe86b370 with the material instance resolved at the start of the case). The producer data (docs/data/producer_fields.txt: ctor 0x4dfb570 at +0x00, 16 bytes at +0x10) is consistent with a vector-like object at +0x00..+0x1F [I]. A RenderBlitFlipbookDescription struct is NOT added: only +0x10/+0x18 and the element stride 0x40 are verified. Next: scan the element loop with a tracked element register across 0x666b704..0x666c800 (r14 is advanced in place, so descscan's register kill rule hides later reads).
6ih BlitFlipbook element fields (loop 0x666b704..0x666c800, element register r14) [V reads]: +0x00 qword (read at 0x666b942/0x666b982/0x666ba76, after calls 0x5679db0 and 0x4dedd00: an object handle), +0x08 address passed to 0xbcaba00 (lea r8,[r14+8]; a 16-byte sub-object), +0x18 qword + +0x20 dword (both feed uniform VBlendControl), +0x24 16-byte block, +0x34..+0x3F not read. RenderBlitFlipbookDescription (0xA0 [I]) with the 0x40-byte Element is now in Descriptions.h with static_asserts on the verified offsets; Release build OK. Callees seen in the loop for later dossiers: 0xbcaba00, 0xbbd7c70, 0x4dedd00, 0x66a0f60, 0xe869ff0, 0x6760a80, 0x675f3b0, 0x675fb30, 0x67d7410, 0x6760710, 0x5aba6d0, 0x674a1d0.
6ih FadeToBlack (Effect tag 5, first callee 0x67d6d70; 6gj) [V reads, I meaning]: 0x67d6d70(rcx, rdx = frame ctx, r8 = description, r9) is a keyed find-or-insert, not a drawing extractor. It hashes the 8 key bytes at description +0x08..+0x0F byte by byte with FNV-1 64 (offset 0x...2325, prime 0x100000001b3; the movzx at [rbx+9], +0xB, +0xD, +0xF are the odd bytes, the even ones are read in the same unrolled chain), masks with [obj+0x70], walks the bucket array at [obj+0x58]/[obj+0x48] comparing against node fields +0x10/+0x18, and on a miss appends a 0x10-byte record to the vector at [obj+0x88/+0x90] (`add [obj+0x88], 0x10`). The description itself is therefore at least 0x10 bytes: +0x00 qword (read at 0x67d6def), +0x08 an 8-byte key; the +0x20/+0x28 reads in descscan's output belong to the found node (r8 is reused), not to the description. No FadeToBlack struct is typed: its meaningful payload is not read by the visitor's first callee [?]. The scan flags a general trap: a callee that reuses r8 makes descscan attribute later reads to the description; stop the scan at the first write to the description register.
6ih correction of 6gj (0xaa6580) [V]: 0xaa6580 is NOT the AimAssistHighlight / Editor 5-7 extractor. It is a 4x4 float matrix multiply: out = rcx, A = rdx (rows at +0x00/+0x10/+0x20/+0x30, kept as xmm), B = r8 (16 floats read as 16 scalar broadcasts: movups at +0,+4,+8,... then shufps 0 = broadcast of each element), result row i = sum_j B[i][j] * A.row[j], stored at rcx+0x00/+0x10/+0x20/+0x30, returns rax = rcx, uses xmm6-xmm15 (saved), no calls, ~0x1A0 bytes. The overlapping misaligned loads descscan listed as "description reads" are the element broadcasts. This matches the visitors passing (frame ctx, matrix, matrix) there only by coincidence of register layout: the case bodies for Effect 6 and Editor 5-7 are NOT yet identified, so the AimAssistHighlight and Editor 5-7 descriptions remain untyped. 0xaa6580 is a pure leaf with a tiny contract and joins the Q2 rewrite candidates (SIMD mat4 multiply, verified by comparing 16 floats). The lesson from 67d6d70 and this one: a "first callee" in the case table is only a first call; confirm what a callee is before reading its arguments as a description.
6ih AimAssistHighlight (Effect tag 6, case 0x666b143) [V]: the case body is inline (0xaa6580 is only the mat4 multiply it calls). It reads vec2 +0x0C and float +0x14, builds a local 4x4 translate/scale matrix at rbp+0x3f0 (constants from .rdata), multiplies it with the mat4 at desc+0x28 via 0xaa6580 into rbp+0x2a0, then resolves the material through 0x675e440 (strings include "CameraAimAssistHighlight") with the name record from [r12+0x40]/[r12+0x68] (frame ctx), takes the shared_ptr, and exits through the shared tail 0x666c8f0 (which reads a dword at [r15+0x84], shared with the other Effect cases: not attributed). RenderAimAssistHighlightDescription is in Descriptions.h with the verified offsets and a minimum size 0x68 [I: the true size is unknown]; Release build OK. This corrects the 6gj row that named 0xaa6580 as the extractor.
6ih FullscreenEffect (Effect tag 7, case 0x666ae72) [V reads, ? meaning]: the case reads a pointer at desc+0x78 (a shared_ptr-like pair; null skips the case, and [ptr+0x40] must be non-zero), reads the first pointer at desc+0x00 and, through it, a byte at +0x39 (r8b), then calls 0x66b2b30(rcx = local result at rbp+0x2a0, rdx = &desc+0x78, r8b = [[desc]+0x39]) and continues at 0x666b5cd/0x666b5cf with rdi = [[desc+0x78]+0x40]. descscan on 0x66b2b30 finds no description reads (its first call site passes the sub-object, not the description), and disrange returns nothing at that address, so its body is not read yet [?]. Not typed. Known description facts: +0x00 pointer (to a config with a byte at +0x39), +0x78 pointer to the object that carries +0x40; the size is at least 0x80.
6ih FullscreenEffect, 0x66b2b30 read [V code, I names]: my earlier "body not readable" was a helper misuse (disrange needs lo < hi; 0x66b3c0 is below 0x66b2b30). 0x66b2b30(rcx = out, rdx = &desc+0x78, r8b = flag) builds a 0x58-byte render-state record in `out`: it initialises out+0x0E = 2, +0x10 = 0, +0x14 = 0, zeroes +0x18..+0x33 and +0x54 (word), then takes cfg = [[rdx]+0x40] (the object the case checked non-zero) and reads cfg+0x185 (enable byte == 1) with cfg+0x180 / cfg+0x181 (two bytes) which it maps through an xor/or chain to a 0..9 enum written to out+0x00 (cases (3,2)->1, (6,8)->3, (3,8)->4, (4,5)->5, (6,3)->6, (6,8)... , (x,1)->9: the blend-mode pairs of a data-driven blend config: [I] src/dst factor ids -> preset index), then cfg+0x1A8 (a byte 0/1/2 mapped to 0/2/...) and further fields below 0x66b2c2b. So the FullscreenEffect description is {+0x00 pointer to a config holder whose +0x39 byte is passed as r8; +0x78 shared_ptr to a FullscreenEffectConfig whose +0x40 object holds blend (+0x180/+0x181/+0x185) and +0x1A8 state}, and the visitor converts the config into a render-state record that lines up with the DrawItemState block (DrawItem.h) [I]. Not typed as a struct yet: the config object is a loaded data asset (material/fullscreen-effect definition), larger than the description.
6ih EditorHighlight (Effect tag 8, case 0x666b316) [V]: the case gets the pass object through 0x5aba5c0 (ctx+0x9D0), asks a vcall for a key (slot +0x10, dl = 0x4a), then appends the description VERBATIM to the pass vector at +0x80/+0x88: qword +0x00, qword +0x08 (shared_ptr control block, `lock inc [ctrl+8]`), four 16-byte rows +0x10..+0x4F, qword +0x50, element stride 0x58 (`add [pass+0x80], 0x58`, 0x666b5c0). So the description is exactly 0x58 bytes; RenderEditorHighlightDescription is in Descriptions.h with static_asserts and a Release build OK. The meaning of the 64-byte block (probably a matrix) and +0x50 is unknown [?]. Note the same 0x58-byte element size as the Atmosphere queue (pass+0x548/0x550 on the Sky pass): both pass classes use 0x58-stride vectors.
6ih Light visitor register map and LightAmbient (Light tag 0, case 0x6640de8) [V]: the Light visitor 0x6640d80 keeps the variant in rdx (tag byte [rdx+8], description pointer [rdx] -> rsi, frame ctx -> rdi). Light cases 0x66415b0, 0x6640f1b, 0x66410a0(?), 0x6640ec1, 0x664187d call 0x5aba5c0 (queue), case 0x66417af first calls 0x67b6300 and appends 0xB00-byte elements at pass+0x260 (`add [rsi+0x260], 0xb00`, after 0x67e90d0). Case 0 (ambient) reads qword +0x00, float +0x08, float +0x10, u16 +0x14 and queues them packed into a 0x14-byte element at pass+0x800/+0x808; RenderLightAmbientDescription (0x18 [I]) is in Descriptions.h with static_asserts, Release build OK. The other Light cases are next, using this register map (description = rsi, not r8/r15; scan with --reg rsi).
6ih LightDirectional lead (Light tag 1, case 0x66415b0) [V reads, ? rest]: the case first requires [frame ctx + 0x70] == 1 (else jumps to 0x66447a0), then reads from the description (rsi): vec4 +0x00, byte +0x10, qword +0x14, dword +0x1C, word +0x20 (so size >= 0x22, probably 0x28 [I]); it builds a matrix-like local at rbp+0xb10..0xb2c (identity-ish with a constant), then calls 0x5aba5c0 (after which rsi is the PASS, so descscan stops tracking: its list is complete only up to that call). A dword/word queue tail at 0x6641768..0x66417a2 appends a 0x14-byte element at pass+0x800/+0x808, the same element shape as the ambient case 0x6640de8; the bytes before 0x6641768 do not disassemble from 0x6641760, so that tail is entered mid-instruction from another path and its link to this case is not established [?]. Not typed. Caveat: the earlier reading of case 0 as the only +0x800 writer is unproven; both cases (or a shared tail) append 0x14-byte elements there.
6ih LightDirectional resolved (case 0x66415b0) [V]: reading the case in order (my earlier mid-instruction disassembly from 0x6641760 was misaligned) shows one linear body. After the [ctx+0x70]==1 check it builds a local light record at rbp+0xbe0..0xc40 (type dword 2 at +0xbe4, desc vec4 +0x00 at +0xbec, flag byte 1 at +0xbfc, desc byte +0x10 at +0xbfd, constants 1.0/0 and a matrix-like block, desc qword +0x14 at +0xc20, desc float +0x1C at +0xc28), gets the pass via 0x5aba5c0, emplaces the record into the pass light vector at pass+0x648/+0x650 through 0x675fb30, computes the new light's index (`(end-begin)>>3 * 0xFFFF8E39 - 1` = division by the 0x38-byte... record size) into a word at [rbp-0xE], and appends a 0x14-byte SUMMARY to the list at pass+0x800/+0x808: {qword desc+0x00, float desc+0x08, dword desc+0x0C, word {flag 1, desc byte +0x10}}. The ambient case 0x6640de8 appends to the SAME summary list ({qword, float, float, u16}), so +0x800 is a per-frame light summary list shared by the light types. RenderLightDirectionalDescription (0x28 [I]) is in Descriptions.h with static_asserts, Release build OK. 6ih Light tag 5 (case 0x66417af; LightGroupTag::ServerGraphicsOverrideParameters) [V structure, ? field meanings]: the case calls 0x67b6300(out = [rbp-0x50] (a 0xB00-byte local), desc = rdx) to CONVERT the description into a 0xB00-byte record, gets the pass (0x5aba5c0) and copy-constructs the record into the pass vector at pass+0x260/+0x268 with 0x67e90d0 (stride 0xB00, `add [pass+0x260], 0xb00`, grow path at 0x6641dbb). 0x67b6300 (size 0x2BD, ends 0x67b6bbd) walks the description as a sequence of 0x40-byte sub-objects passed by address to one helper: sixteen at +0x40..+0x400 (+0x40 + k*0x40), a dword at +0x440, container-like members at +0x448/+0x458 (hash-container ops, "unordered_map/set too long" is its only string), qwords +0x460/+0x468/+0x470/+0x478, then twenty-five more 0x40-byte sub-objects at +0x480..+0xA80 (+0x480 + k*0x40). So this description is about 0xAC0 bytes: a bag of ~41 parameter objects (the server's graphics override parameters), each 0x40 bytes (std::string-sized with SSO plus a value is likely [?]), not a flat struct of scalars. Not typed: the 0x40-byte element layout and its meaning are unread. The conversion helper 0x67b6300 and the vector copy 0x67e90d0 are new dossier targets.
6ih Light tag 5 element = an MSVC std::unordered_map [V code, I types]: the 0x40-byte sub-object copy helper 0x67b7810 (size 0x359, ends 0x67b7b69; rcx = dst, rdx = src) copies dword +0x00, builds a hash table in place: allocates a list sentinel (0x18 bytes from the engine allocator, self-linked, stored at dst+0x8), clears dst+0x18..+0x2F (bucket vector begin/end/cap), copies qwords +0x30/+0x38 (mask / max index of the table), sizes the bucket vector from (src+0x20 - src+0x18) >> 3 (bucket vector length) and then walks the source list (`r14 = [src+8]`, nodes linked by their first qword, key byte-hashed with the FNV 64 constants 0xcbf29ce484222325 / 0x100000001b3 over `byte [node+0x10]` and following key bytes) re-inserting each node into the new table. So each of the ~41 parameter objects of the ServerGraphicsOverrideParameters description is {u32 id/type at +0, std::unordered_map (0x38 bytes: list sentinel +8, size +0x10, bucket vector +0x18, mask +0x30, max load +0x38)}, keyed by a hashed name; the 0xB00-byte record is a deep copy of those maps. This is NOT renderer data in the draw sense: it carries server-provided graphics settings (parameter tables) into the Light pass. Consequence for a filter/rewrite: tag 5 allocations happen per insert (deep copy of ~41 hash maps per frame): a candidate hot spot for the optimization module (skip the copy when the parameters did not change) [I].
